II=\frac{\Big\langle\partial_{t}\phi(t),\,\ddot{\chi}\Big(\frac{x-x_{1}(t)}{z(t)}\Big)\Big[\frac{\dot{x}_{1}(t)}{z(t)}+\frac{(x-x_{1}(t))\dot{z}(t)}{z(t)^{2}}\Big]g(t)\Big\rangle}{z(t)\left\lVert\partial_{x}H_{0,1}\right\rVert_{L^{2}}^{2}}+\frac{\Big\langle\partial_{t}\phi(t),\,\dot{\chi}\Big(\frac{x-x_{1}(t)}{z(t)}\Big)\frac{\dot{z}(t)}{z(t)^{2}}g(t)\Big\rangle}{\left\lVert\partial_{x}H_{0,1}\right\rVert_{L^{2}}^{2}}\\
+\frac{\Big\langle\partial_{t}\phi(t),\,\dot{\chi}\Big(\frac{x-x_{1}(t)}{z(t)}\Big)\Big[\frac{\dot{x}_{1}(t)}{z(t)}+\frac{(x-x_{1}(t))\dot{z}(t)}{z(t)^{2}}\Big]\partial_{x}g(t)\Big\rangle}{\left\lVert\partial_{x}H_{0,1}\right\rVert_{L^{2}}^{2}}.

(118)

First, note that since the support of
\dot{\chi}
is contained in
[\theta(1-\gamma),\,\theta],
from the estimates (D3) and (D4) we obtain that

\displaystyle\left\lVert\partial_{x}H^{x_{1}(t)}_{-1,0}\right\rVert_{L^{2}_{x}\Big(\supp\partial_{x}\chi_{0}(t,x)\Big)}^{2}=O\Big(e^{-2\sqrt{2}\theta(1-\gamma)z(t)}\Big),

(119)

\displaystyle\left\lVert\partial_{x}H^{x_{2}(t)}_{0,1}\right\rVert_{L^{2}_{x}\Big(\supp\partial_{x}\chi_{0}(t,x)\Big)}^{2}=O\Big(e^{-2\sqrt{2}(1-\theta)z(t)}\Big),

(120)

Now, we recall the identity

\partial_{t}\phi(t,x)=-\dot{x}_{1}(t)\partial_{x}H^{x_{1}(t)}_{-1,0}-\dot{x}_{2}(t)\partial_{x}H^{x_{2}(t)}_{0,1}+\partial_{t}g(t),

by using the estimates (
119
), (
120
) in the identity (
118
), we deduce that

II=O\Bigg(\left\lVert\dot{\chi}\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})}\frac{\max_{j\in\{1,\,2\}}\left\lvert\dot{x}_{j}(t)\right\rvert}{z(t)}\left\lVert\overrightarrow{g(t)}\right\rVert^{2}+\left\lVert\ddot{\chi}\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})}\left\lVert\overrightarrow{g(t)}\right\rVert^{2}\frac{\max_{j\in\{1,\,2\}}\left\lvert\dot{x}_{j}(t)\right\rvert}{z(t)^{2}}\\
+(e^{-\sqrt{2}\theta(1-\gamma)z(t)}+e^{-\sqrt{z(t)}(1-\theta)})\left\lVert\ddot{\chi}\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})}\frac{\max_{j\in\{1,\,2\}}\dot{x}_{j}(t)^{2}}{z(t)^{2}}\left\lVert\overrightarrow{g(t)}\right\rVert\\
+\left\lVert\overrightarrow{g(t)}\right\rVert(e^{-\sqrt{2}z(t)(1-\theta)}+e^{-\sqrt{2}\theta(1-\gamma)z(t)})\left[\frac{\left\lVert\ddot{\chi}\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})}}{z(t)^{2}}+\frac{\left\lVert\dot{\chi}\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})}}{z(t)}\right]\max_{j\in\{1,\,2\}}\dot{x}_{j}(t)^{2}\Bigg).

(121)

Since
\frac{1-\gamma}{2-\gamma}\leq\max((1-\theta),\theta(1-\gamma))
for
0<\gamma,\theta<1,
we have that the estimate (
121
) is minimal when
\theta=\frac{1-\gamma}{2-\gamma}.
So, from now on, we consider

\theta=\frac{1-\gamma}{2-\gamma},

(122)

which with (
108
) and (
121
) imply that

II=O(\alpha(t)).

Step 3.
(Estimate of
III.
)
We deduce from the identity

III=-\frac{\langle\partial_{x}(\chi_{0}(t)\partial_{t}g(t)),\,\partial_{t}\phi(t)\rangle}{\left\lVert\partial_{x}H_{0,1}\right\rVert_{L^{2}}^{2}}

that

III=-\frac{\Big\langle\dot{\chi}\Big(\frac{x-x_{1}(t)}{z(t)}\Big)\partial_{t}g(t),\,-\dot{x}_{1}(t)\partial_{x}H^{x_{1}(t)}_{-1,0}-\dot{x}_{2}(t)\partial_{x}H^{x_{2}(t)}_{0,1}+\partial_{t}g(t,x)\Big\rangle}{z(t)\left\lVert\partial_{x}H_{0,1}\right\rVert_{L^{2}}^{2}}\\
-\frac{\Big\langle\chi_{0}(t,x)\partial^{2}_{t,x}g(t,x),\,-\dot{x}_{1}(t)\partial_{x}H^{x_{1}(t)}_{-1,0}-\dot{x}_{2}(t)\partial_{x}H^{x_{2}(t)}_{0,1}+\partial_{t}g(t,x)\Big\rangle}{\left\lVert\partial_{x}H_{0,1}\right\rVert_{L^{2}}^{2}}=III.1+III.2.

(123)

The identity (
122
) and the estimates (
108
), (
119
) and (
120
) imply by Cauchy-Schwarz inequality that

III.1=O\Bigg(\frac{\max_{j\in\{1,\,2\}}\left\lvert\dot{x}_{j}(t)\right\rvert e^{-\sqrt{2}z(t)(\frac{1-\gamma}{2-\gamma})}}{\gamma z(t)}\left\lVert\overrightarrow{g(t)}\right\rVert+\frac{1}{z(t)\gamma}\left\lVert\overrightarrow{g(t)}\right\rVert^{2}\Bigg).

(124)

In conclusion, we have the estimate that

III.1=O(\alpha(t)).

Also, from condition (
116
) and the estimate (
4
), we can deduce that

\left\lVert(1-\chi_{0}(t))\partial^{2}_{x}H^{x_{1}(t)}_{-1,0}\right\rVert_{L^{2}_{x}(\mathbb{R})}+\left\lVert\chi_{0}(t)\partial^{2}_{x}H^{x_{2}(t)}_{0,1}\right\rVert_{L^{2}_{x}(\mathbb{R})}=O\Big(e^{-\sqrt{2}z(t)(\frac{1-\gamma}{2-\gamma})}\Big).

(125)

Also, we have that

III.2=-\frac{\Big\langle\chi_{0}(t,x)\Big[\partial^{2}_{t,x}\phi(t)+\dot{x}_{1}(t)\partial^{2}_{x}H^{x_{1}(t)}_{-1,0}+\dot{x}_{2}(t)\partial^{2}_{x}H^{x_{2}(t)}_{0,1}\Big],\,\partial_{t}\phi(t)\Big\rangle}{\left\lVert\partial_{x}H_{0,1}\right\rVert_{L^{2}}^{2}}.

(126)

By integration by parts, we have that

\left\lvert\Big\langle\chi\Big(\frac{x-x_{1}(t)}{z(t)}\Big)\partial^{2}_{t,x}\phi(t,x),\,\partial_{t}\phi(t,x)\Big\rangle\right\rvert=O\Big(\frac{1}{\gamma z(t)}\left\lVert\partial_{t}\phi(t)\right\rVert_{L^{2}_{x}(\supp\partial_{x}\chi_{0}(t))}^{2}\Big).

In conclusion, from the estimates (
108
), (
119
), (
120
) and identity (
122
), we obtain that

\left\lvert\Big\langle\chi\Big(\frac{x-x_{1}(t)}{z(t)}\Big)\partial^{2}_{t,x}\phi(t,x),\,\partial_{t}\phi(t,x)\Big\rangle\right\rvert=O\Big(\frac{1}{\gamma z(t)}\left\lVert\overrightarrow{g(t)}\right\rVert^{2}+\max_{j\in\{1,\,2\}}\dot{x}_{j}(t)^{2}\frac{1}{\gamma z(t)}\Big[e^{-2\sqrt{2}z(t)(\frac{1-\gamma}{2-\gamma})}\Big]\Big).

(127)

Also, from Lemma (
2.3
), the estimate (
4
) and the fact of
0\leq\chi_{0}\leq 1,
we deduce that

\displaystyle\left\lvert\Big\langle\chi_{0}(t,x)\partial^{2}_{x}H^{x_{2}(t)}_{0,1},\,\partial_{x}H^{x_{1}(t)}_{-1,0}\Big\rangle\right\rvert=O\Big(z(t)e^{-\sqrt{2}z(t)}\Big),

(128)

\displaystyle\left\lvert\Big\langle(1-\chi_{0}(t,x))\partial^{2}_{x}H^{x_{1}(t)}_{-1,0},\,\partial_{x}H^{x_{2}(t)}_{0,1}\Big\rangle\right\rvert=O\Big(z(t)e^{-\sqrt{2}z(t)}\Big).

(129)

From the estimates (
119
), (
120
) and identity (
122
), we can verify by integration by parts the following estimates

\Big\langle(1-\chi_{0}(t))\dot{x}_{1}(t)\partial^{2}_{x}H^{x_{1}(t)}_{-1,0},\,\dot{x}_{1}(t)\partial_{x}H^{x_{1}(t)}_{-1,0}\Big\rangle=O\Big(\frac{\dot{x}_{1}(t)^{2}}{\gamma z(t)}e^{-2\sqrt{2}z(t)(\frac{1-\gamma}{2-\gamma})}\Big),

(130)

\Big\langle\chi_{0}(t)\dot{x}_{2}(t)\partial^{2}_{x}H^{x_{2}(t)}_{0,1},\,\dot{x}_{2}(t)\partial_{x}H^{x_{2}(t)}_{0,1}\Big\rangle=O\Big(\frac{\dot{x}_{2}(t)^{2}}{\gamma z(t)}e^{-2\sqrt{2}z(t)(\frac{1-\gamma}{2-\gamma})}\Big).

(131)

Finally, from Cauchy-Schwarz inequality and the estimate (
125
) we obtain that

\displaystyle\Big\langle(1-\chi_{0}(t))\dot{x}_{1}(t)\partial^{2}_{x}H^{x_{1}(t)}_{-1,0},\,\partial_{t}g(t)\Big\rangle=O\Big(\left\lvert\dot{x}_{1}(t)\right\rvert\left\lVert\overrightarrow{g(t)}\right\rVert e^{-\sqrt{2}z(t)(\frac{1-\gamma}{2-\gamma})}\Big),

(132)

\displaystyle\Big\langle\chi_{0}(t)\dot{x}_{1}(t)\partial^{2}_{x}H^{x_{2}(t)}_{0,1},\,\partial_{t}g(t)\Big\rangle=O\Big(\left\lvert\dot{x}_{2}(t)\right\rvert\left\lVert\overrightarrow{g(t)}\right\rVert e^{-\sqrt{2}z(t)(\frac{1-\gamma}{2-\gamma})}\Big).

(133)

In conclusion, we obtain from the estimates (
128
), (
129
), (
130
), (
131
) (
132
) and (
133
) that

III.2=-\dot{x}_{1}(t)\frac{\Big\langle\partial^{2}_{x}H^{x_{1}(t)}_{-1,0},\,\partial_{t}\phi(t)\Big\rangle}{\left\lVert\partial_{x}H_{0,1}\right\rVert^{2}}+O(\alpha(t)).

(134)

This estimate of
III.2
and the estimate (
124
) of
III.1
imply

III=-\dot{x}_{1}(t)\frac{\Big\langle\partial^{2}_{x}H^{x_{1}(t)}_{-1,0},\,\partial_{t}\phi(t)\Big\rangle}{\left\lVert\partial_{x}H_{0,1}\right\rVert^{2}}+O(\alpha(t)).

(135)

In conclusion, from the estimates
II=O(\alpha(t)),
(
135
) and the definition of
I,
we have that

I+II+III=O(\alpha(t)).

Step 4.
(Estimate of
V.
)
We recall that

V=-\frac{\langle\partial_{x}\chi_{0}(t)g(t),\,\partial^{2}_{t}\phi(t)\rangle}{\left\lVert\partial_{x}H_{0,1}\right\rVert_{L^{2}}^{2}},

and that

\partial^{2}_{t}\phi(t)=\partial^{2}_{x}g(t)+\Big[\dot{U}\left(H^{x_{1}(t)}_{-1,0}\right)+\dot{U}\left(H^{x_{2}(t)}_{0,1}\right)-\dot{U}\left(H^{x_{1}(t)}_{-1,0}+H^{x_{2}(t)}_{0,1}\right)\Big]+\Big[\dot{U}\left(H^{x_{1}(t)}_{-1,0}+H^{x_{2}(t)}_{0,1}\right)-\dot{U}\left(H^{x_{1}(t)}_{-1,0}+H^{x_{2}(t)}_{0,1}+g(t)\right)\Big].

(136)

First, by integration by parts, using estimate (
108
), we have the following estimate

-\frac{1}{\left\lVert\partial_{x}H_{0,1}\right\rVert_{L^{2}}^{2}}\langle\partial_{x}\chi_{0}(t)\partial^{2}_{x}g(t),\,g(t)\rangle=O\Big(\Big[\frac{1}{\gamma z(t)}+\frac{1}{\gamma^{2}z(t)^{2}}\Big]\left\lVert\overrightarrow{g(t)}\right\rVert^{2}\Big)=O(\alpha(t)).

(137)

Second, since
U
is smooth and
\left\lVert g(t)\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}=O\big(\epsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}\big)
for all
t\in\mathbb{R},
we deduce that

\left\langle\dot{U}\left(H^{x_{1}(t)}_{-1,0}+H^{x_{2}(t)}_{0,1}\right)-\dot{U}\left(H^{x_{1}(t)}_{-1,0}+H^{x_{2}(t)}_{0,1}+g(t)\right),\partial_{x}\chi_{0}(t)g(t)\right\rangle=O\Big(\frac{1}{z(t)\gamma}\left\lVert\overrightarrow{g(t)}\right\rVert^{2}\Big)=O(\alpha(t)).

(138)

Next, from equation (
87
) and Lemma
2.3
, we have that

\left\lVert\dot{U}\left(H^{x_{1}(t)}_{-1,0}\right)+\dot{U}\left(H^{x_{2}(t)}_{0,1}\right)-\dot{U}\left(H^{x_{1}(t)}_{-1,0}+H^{x_{2}(t)}_{0,1}\right)\right\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R})}=O(e^{-\sqrt{2}z(t)}),

(139)

then, by Hölder inequality we have that

\left\langle\dot{U}\left(H^{x_{1}(t)}_{-1,0}\right)+\dot{U}\left(H^{x_{2}(t)}_{0,1}\right)-\dot{U}\left(H^{x_{1}(t)}_{-1,0}+H^{x_{2}(t)}_{0,1}\right),\partial_{x}\chi_{0}(t)\partial_{x}g(t)\right\rangle=O\Big(\frac{1}{\gamma z(t)}\left\lVert\overrightarrow{g(t)}\right\rVert e^{-\sqrt{2}z(t)}\Big)=O(\alpha(t)).

(140)

Clearly, the estimates (
137
), (
138
) and (
140
) imply that
V=O(\alpha(t)).

Step 5.
(Estimate of
VI.
)
We know that

VI=-\frac{\big\langle\partial_{x}g(t)\chi_{0}(t),\,\partial^{2}_{t}\phi(t)\big\rangle}{\left\lVert\partial_{x}H_{0,1}\right\rVert_{L^{2}}^{2}}.

We recall the equation (
136
) which implies that

\left\lVert\partial_{x}H_{0,1}\right\rVert_{L^{2}}^{2}VI=-\left\langle\partial_{x}g(t)\chi_{0}(t),\,\partial^{2}_{x}g(t)\right\rangle+\left\langle\partial_{x}g(t)\chi_{0}(t),\,\dot{U}\left(H^{x_{1}(t)}_{-1,0}+H^{x_{2}(t)}_{0,1}+g(t)\right)-\dot{U}\left(H^{x_{1}(t)}_{-1,0}+H^{x_{2}(t)}_{0,1}\right)\right\rangle\\
+\left\langle\partial_{x}g(t)\chi_{0}(t),\,\dot{U}\left(H^{x_{1}(t)}_{-1,0}+H^{x_{2}(t)}_{0,1}\right)-\dot{U}\left(H^{x_{1}(t)}_{-1,0}\right)-\dot{U}\left(H^{x_{2}(t)}_{0,1}\right)\right\rangle.

By integration by parts, we have from estimate (
108
) that

\langle\partial_{x}g(t,x)\chi_{0}(t,x),\,\partial^{2}_{x}g(t,x)\rangle=O\Big(\frac{1}{\gamma z(t)}\left\lVert\overrightarrow{g(t)}\right\rVert^{2}\Big).

(141)

From the estimate (
139
) and Cauchy-Schwarz inequality, we can obtain the following estimate

\left\langle\partial_{x}g(t)\chi_{0}(t),\,\dot{U}\left(H^{x_{1}(t)}_{-1,0}+H^{x_{2}(t)}_{0,1}\right)-\dot{U}\left(H^{x_{1}(t)}_{-1,0}\right)-\dot{U}\left(H^{x_{2}(t)}_{0,1}\right)\right\rangle=O\Big(e^{-\sqrt{2}z(t)}\left\lVert\overrightarrow{g(t)}\right\rVert\Big).

(142)

Then, to conclude the estimate of
VI
we just need to study the following term

C(t)\coloneqq\big\langle\partial_{x}g(t)\chi_{0}(t),\,\dot{U}(H^{x_{1}(t)}_{-1,0}+H^{x_{2}(t)}_{0,1}+g(t))-\dot{U}(H^{x_{1}(t)}_{-1,0}+H^{x_{2}(t)}_{0,1})\big\rangle.

Since we have from the Taylor’s theorem that

\dot{U}\left(H^{x_{1}(t)}_{-1,0}+H^{x_{2}(t)}_{0,1}+g(t)\right)-\dot{U}\left(H^{x_{1}(t)}_{-1,0}+H^{x_{2}(t)}_{0,1}\right)=\sum_{k=2}^{6}U^{(k)}\left(H^{x_{1}(t)}_{-1,0}+H^{x_{2}(t)}_{0,1}\right)\frac{g(t)^{k-1}}{(k-1)!},

from estimate (
108
), we can deduce by integration by parts that

C(t)=-\Big\langle\chi_{0}(t)\partial_{x}\left(H^{x_{1}(t)}_{-1,0}+H^{x_{2}(t)}_{0,1}\right),\,\sum_{k=3}^{6}U^{(k)}\left(H^{x_{1}(t)}_{-1,0}+H^{x_{2}(t)}_{0,1}\right)\frac{g(t)^{k-1}}{(k-1)!}\Big\rangle+O\left(\frac{1}{\gamma z(t)}\left\lVert\overrightarrow{g(t)}\right\rVert^{2}\right).

Since

\left\lVert\chi_{0}(t)\partial_{x}H^{x_{2}(t)}_{0,1}\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}+\left\lVert(1-\chi_{0}(t))\partial_{x}H^{x_{1}(t)}_{-1,0}\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}=O\Big(e^{-\sqrt{2}z(t)(\frac{1-\gamma}{2-\gamma})}\Big),

we obtain that

C(t)=-\Big\langle\partial_{x}H^{x_{1}(t)}_{-1,0},\,\sum_{k=3}^{6}U^{(k)}\left(H^{x_{1}(t)}_{-1,0}+H^{x_{2}(t)}_{0,1}\right)\frac{g(t)^{k-1}}{(k-1)!}\Big\rangle+O\left(\frac{1}{\gamma z(t)}\left\lVert\overrightarrow{g(t)}\right\rVert^{2}+e^{-\sqrt{2}z(t)(\frac{1-\gamma}{2-\gamma})}\left\lVert\overrightarrow{g(t)}\right\rVert^{2}\right).

Also, from Lemma
2.3
and the fact that
\left\lVert g(t)\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}\lesssim\left\lVert\overrightarrow{g(t)}\right\rVert
, we deduce that

\Big\langle\partial_{x}H^{x_{1}}_{-1,0},\Big[\ddot{U}\left(H^{x_{1}(t)}_{-1,0}\right)-\ddot{U}\left(H^{x_{1}(t)}_{-1,0}+H^{x_{2}(t)}_{0,1}\right)\Big]g(t)\Big\rangle=O\Big(e^{-\sqrt{2}z(t)}\left\lVert\overrightarrow{g(t)}\right\rVert\Big).

(143)

In conclusion, we obtain that

C(t)=-\int_{\mathbb{R}}\partial_{x}H^{x_{1}(t)}_{-1,0}\Big(\dot{U}\left(H^{x_{1}(t)}_{-1,0}+H^{x_{2}(t)}_{0,1}+g(t)\right)-\dot{U}\left(H^{x_{1}(t)}_{-1,0}+H^{x_{2}(t)}_{0,1}\right)\Big)\,dx+\int_{\mathbb{R}}\partial_{x}H^{x_{1}(t)}_{-1,0}\ddot{U}\left(H^{x_{1}(t)}_{-1,0}\right)g(t,x)\,dx\\
+O(\alpha(t)).

(144)

So

VI=\frac{-\int_{\mathbb{R}}\partial_{x}H^{x_{1}(t)}_{-1,0}\Big(\dot{U}\left(H^{x_{1}(t)}_{-1,0}+H^{x_{2}(t)}_{0,1}+g(t)\right)-\dot{U}(H^{x_{1}(t)}_{-1,0}+H^{x_{2}(t)}_{0,1})\Big)\,dx}{\left\lVert\partial_{x}H_{0,1}\right\rVert_{L^{2}}^{2}}\\
+\frac{\int_{\mathbb{R}}\partial_{x}H^{x_{1}(t)}_{-1,0}\ddot{U}\left(H^{x_{1}(t)}_{-1,0}\right)g(t,x)\,dx}{\left\lVert\partial_{x}H_{0,1}\right\rVert_{L^{2}}^{2}}+O(\alpha(t)).

(145)

Step 6.
(Sum of
IV,\,VI.
)
From the identities (
136
) and

IV=-\frac{\big\langle\partial_{x}H^{x_{1}(t)}_{-1,0},\,\partial^{2}_{t}\phi(t)\big\rangle}{\left\lVert\partial_{x}H_{0,1}\right\rVert_{L^{2}}^{2}},

we obtain that

IV=-\frac{\Big\langle\partial^{2}_{x}g(t)-\Big(\dot{U}\left(H^{x_{1}(t)}_{-1,0}+H^{x_{2}(t)}_{0,1}+g(t)\right)-\dot{U}\left(H^{x_{1}(t)}_{-1,0}+H^{x_{2}(t)}_{0,1}\right)\Big),\,\partial_{x}H^{x_{1}(t)}_{-1,0}\Big\rangle}{\left\lVert\partial_{x}H_{0,1}\right\rVert_{L^{2}}^{2}}\\
-\frac{\Big\langle\dot{U}\left(H^{x_{1}(t)}_{-1,0}\right)+\dot{U}\left(H^{x_{2}(t)}_{0,1}\right)-U\left(H^{x_{1}(t)}_{-1,0}+H^{x_{2}(t)}_{0,1}\right),\,\partial_{x}H^{x_{1}(t)}_{-1,0}\Big\rangle}{\left\lVert\partial_{x}H_{0,1}\right\rVert_{L^{2}}^{2}}.

(146)

In conclusion, from the identity

\Big[\partial_{x}^{2}-\ddot{U}\left(H^{x_{1}(t)}_{-1,0}\right)\Big]\partial_{x}H^{x_{1}(t)}_{-1,0}=0

and by integration by parts we have that

IV+VI=-\frac{\Big\langle\dot{U}\left(H^{x_{1}(t)}_{-1,0}\right)+\dot{U}\left(H^{x_{2}(t)}_{0,1}\right)-U\left(H^{x_{1}(t)}_{-1,0}+H^{x_{2}(t)}_{0,1}\right),\,\partial_{x}H^{x_{1}(t)}_{-1,0}\Big\rangle}{\left\lVert\partial_{x}H_{0,1}\right\rVert_{L^{2}}^{2}}+O(\alpha(t)).

(147)

From our previous results,
we conclude that

I+II+III+IV+V+VI=\\
-\frac{\Big\langle\dot{U}\left(H^{x_{1}(t)}_{-1,0}\right)+\dot{U}\left(H^{x_{2}(t)}_{0,1}\right)-\dot{U}\left(H^{x_{1}(t)}_{-1,0}+H^{x_{2}(t)}_{0,1}\right),\,\partial_{x}H^{x_{1}(t)}_{-1,0}\Big\rangle}{\left\lVert\partial_{x}H_{0,1}\right\rVert_{L^{2}}^{2}}+O(\alpha(t)).

(148)

The conclusion of the lemma follows from estimate (
148
) with identity

\dot{A}(z(t))=-\Big\langle\dot{U}\left(H_{-1,0}\right)+\dot{U}\left(H^{z(t)}_{0,1}\right)-\dot{U}\left(H_{-1,0}+H^{z(t)}_{0,1}\right),\,\partial_{x}H_{-1,0}\Big\rangle,

which can be obtained from (
13
) by integration by parts with the fact that

\left\langle\dot{U}\left(H_{-1,0}+H^{z(t)}_{0,1}\right),\,\partial_{x}H_{-1,0}+\partial_{x}H^{z(t)}_{0,1}\right\rangle=0.

∎

Remark 3.3
.

Since, we know from Lemma
2.3
that

\left\lvert\dot{A}(z(t))+4e^{-\sqrt{2}z(t)}\right\rvert\lesssim z(t)e^{-2\sqrt{2}z(t)},

and, by elementary calculus with change of variables, that

\left\lVert\partial_{x}H_{0,1}\right\rVert_{L^{2}}^{2}=\frac{1}{2\sqrt{2}},

then the estimates (
106
) and (
107
) obtained in Lemma
3.1
motivate us to study the following ordinary differential equation

\ddot{z}(t)=16\sqrt{2}e^{-\sqrt{2}z(t)}.

(149)

Clearly, the solution of (
149
) satisfies the equation

\frac{d}{dt}\Big[\frac{\dot{z}(t)^{2}}{4}+8e^{-\sqrt{2}z(t)}\Big]=0.

(150)

As a consequence, it can be verified that if
z(t_{0})>0
for some
t_{0}\in\mathbb{R},
then there are real constants
v>0,\,c
such that

z(t)=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\ln{\Big(\frac{8}{v^{2}}\cosh{\big(\sqrt{2}vt+c\big)}^{2}\Big)}\text{ for all $t\in\mathbb{R}.$}

(151)

In conclusion, the solution of the equations

\displaystyle\ddot{d}_{1}(t)=-8\sqrt{2}e^{-\sqrt{2}z(t)},

\displaystyle\ddot{d}_{2}(t)=8\sqrt{2}e^{-\sqrt{2}z(t)},

\displaystyle d_{2}(t)-d_{1}(t)=z(t)>0,

are given by

\displaystyle d_{2}(t)=a+bt+\frac{1}{2\sqrt{2}}\ln{\Big(\frac{8}{v^{2}}\cosh{\big(\sqrt{2}vt+c\big)}^{2}\Big)},

(152)

\displaystyle d_{1}(t)=a+bt-\frac{1}{2\sqrt{2}}\ln{\Big(\frac{8}{v^{2}}\cosh{\big(\sqrt{2}vt+c\big)}^{2}\Big)},

(153)

for
a,\,b
real constants. So, we now are motivated to study how close the modulations parameters
x_{1},\,x_{2}
of Theorem
2.9
can be to functions
d_{1},\,d_{2}
satisfying, respectively the identities (
153
) and (
152
) for constants
v\neq 0,\,a,\,b
.

At first view, the statement of the Lemma
3.1
seems too complex and unnecessary for use and that a simplified version should be more useful for our objectives. However, we will show later that for a suitable choice of
\gamma
depending on the energy excess of the solution
\phi(t),
we can get a high precision in the approximation of the modulation parameters
x_{1},\,x_{2}
by smooth functions
d_{1},\,d_{2}
satisfying (
153
) and (
152
) for a large time interval.

4
Energy Estimate Method

Before applying Lemma
3.1
, we need to construct a functional
F(t)
to get lower estimate on the value of
\left\lVert(g(t),\partial_{t}g(t))\right\rVert_{H^{1}\times L^{2}}
than that obtained in Theorem
2.9
.

From now on, we consider
\phi(t)=H_{0,1}(x-x_{2}(t))+H_{-1,0}(x-x_{1}(t))+g(t,x)
, with
x_{1}(t),\,x_{2}(t)
satisfying the orthogonality conditions of the Modulation Lemma and
x_{1},\,x_{2}
,
(g(t),\partial_{t}g(t))
and
\epsilon>0
satisfying all the properties of Theorem
2.9
. Before the enunciation of the main theorem of this section, to simplify the notation in computations, we denote:

D^{2}E(H^{x_{2}(t)}_{0,1}+H^{x_{1}(t)}_{-1,0})=\begin{bmatrix}-\partial^{2}_{x}+\ddot{U}(H^{x_{2}(t)}_{0,1}+H^{x_{1}(t)}_{-1,0})&0\\
0&\mathbb{I}\end{bmatrix}

as a bilinear operator from
H^{1}(\mathbb{R})\times L^{2}(\mathbb{R})
to
\mathbb{C}.

We also denote
\omega_{1}(t,x)=\omega\big(\frac{x-x_{1}(t)}{x_{2}(t)-x_{1}(t)}\big)
for
\omega
a smooth cut function with image contained in the interval
[0,1]
, satisfying the following condition

\omega(x)=\begin{cases}1,\text{ if $x\leq\frac{3}{4}$,}\\
0,\text{ if $x\geq\frac{4}{5}$.}\end{cases}

We consider now the following functional

F(t)=\big\langle D^{2}E(H^{x_{2}(t)}_{0,1}+H^{x_{1}(t)}_{-1,0})\overrightarrow{g(t)},\,\overrightarrow{g(t)}\big\rangle_{L^{2}\times L^{2}}+2\int_{\mathbb{R}}\partial_{t}g(t,x)\partial_{x}g(t,x)\Big[\dot{x}_{1}(t)\omega_{1}(t,x)+\dot{x}_{2}(t)(1-\omega_{1}(t,x))\Big]\,dx\\
-2\int_{\mathbb{R}}g(t,x)\Big(\dot{U}(H^{x_{1}(t)}_{-1,0}(x))+\dot{U}(H^{x_{2}(t)}_{0,1}(x))-\dot{U}(H^{x_{2}(t)}_{0,1}(x)+H^{x_{1}(t)}_{-1,0}(x))\Big)\,dx\\
+2\int_{\mathbb{R}}g(t,x)\Big[(\dot{x}_{1}(t))^{2}\partial^{2}_{x}H^{x_{1}(t)}_{-1,0}(x)+(\dot{x}_{2}(t))^{2}\partial^{2}_{x}H^{x_{2}(t)}_{0,1}(x)\Big]\,dx+\frac{1}{3}\int_{\mathbb{R}}U^{(3)}(H^{x_{2}(t)}_{0,1}(x)+H^{x_{1}(t)}_{-1,0}(x))g(t,x)^{3}\,dx.

(154)

Since
x_{1},\,x_{2}
are functions of class
C^{2}
, is not difficult to verify that
(g(t),\partial_{t}g(t))
solves the integral equation associated to the following partial differential equation

\partial^{2}_{t}g(t,x)-\partial^{2}_{x}g(t,x)+\ddot{U}(H^{x_{2}(t)}_{0,1}(x)+H^{x_{1}(t)}_{-1,0}(x))g(t,x)=\\
-\left[\dot{U}(H^{x_{2}(t)}_{0,1}(x)+H^{x_{1}(t)}_{-1,0}(x)+g(t,x))-\dot{U}(H^{x_{2}(t)}_{0,1}(x)+H^{x_{1}(t)}_{-1,0}(x))-\ddot{U}(H^{x_{2}(t)}_{0,1}(x)+H^{x_{1}(t)}_{-1,0}(x))g(t,x)\right]\\
+\dot{U}(H^{x_{1}(t)}_{-1,0}(x))+\dot{U}(H^{x_{2}(t)}_{0,1}(x))-\dot{U}(H^{x_{2}(t)}_{0,1}(x)+H^{x_{1}(t)}_{-1,0}(x))\\
-\dot{x}_{1}(t)^{2}\partial^{2}_{x}H^{x_{1}(t)}_{-1,0}(x)-\dot{x}_{2}(t)^{2}\partial^{2}_{x}H^{x_{2}(t)}_{0,1}(x)+\ddot{x}_{1}(t)\partial_{x}H^{x_{1}(t)}_{-1,0}(x)+\ddot{x}_{2}(t)\partial_{x}H^{x_{2}(t)}_{0,1}(x)

(155)

in the space
H^{1}(\mathbb{R})\times L^{2}(\mathbb{R}).

Theorem 4.1
.

Assuming the hypotheses of Theorem
2.9
and recalling its notation, let
\delta(t)
be the following quantity

\delta(t)=\left\lVert\overrightarrow{g(t)}\right\rVert\Big(e^{-\sqrt{2}z(t)}\max_{j\in\{1,2\}}\left\lvert\dot{x}_{j}(t)\right\rvert+\max_{j\in\{1,2\}}\left\lvert\dot{x}_{j}(t)\right\rvert^{3}e^{-\frac{\sqrt{2}z(t)}{5}}+\max_{j\in\{1,2\}}\left\lvert\dot{x}_{j}(t)\ddot{x}_{j}(t)\right\rvert\Big)\\
+\left\lVert\overrightarrow{g(t)}\right\rVert^{2}\Big(\frac{\max_{j\in\{1,\,2\}}\left\lvert\dot{x}_{j}(t)\right\rvert}{z(t)}+\max_{j\in\{1,\,2\}}\dot{x}_{j}(t)^{2}+\max_{j\in\{1,\,2\}}\left\lvert\ddot{x}_{j}(t)\right\rvert\Big)+\left\lVert\overrightarrow{g(t)}\right\rVert^{4}.

Then,
\exists
positive constants
A_{1},\,A_{2},\,A_{3}
such that the functional
F(t)
satisfies the inequalities

F(t)+A_{1}\epsilon^{2}\geq A_{2}\left\lVert\overrightarrow{g(t)}\right\rVert^{2},\,\left\lvert\dot{F}(t)\right\rvert\leq A_{3}\delta(t).

Remark 4.2
.

Theorem
2.9
and Theorem
4.1
imply

\left\lvert\dot{F}(t)\right\rvert\lesssim\frac{\epsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}}{\ln{(\frac{1}{\epsilon})}}\left\lVert\overrightarrow{g(t)}\right\rVert^{2}+\left\lVert\overrightarrow{g(t)}\right\rVert\epsilon^{\frac{3}{2}}.

Proof.

Since the formula defining function
F(t)
is very large, we decompose the function in a sum of five terms
F_{1},\,F_{2},\,F_{3},\,F_{4}
and
F_{5}
. More specifically:

\displaystyle F_{1}(t)=\int_{\mathbb{R}}\partial_{t}g(t,x)^{2}+\partial_{x}g(t,x)^{2}+\ddot{U}(H^{x_{1}(t)}_{-1,0}(x)+H^{x_{2}(t)}_{0,1}(x))g(t,x)^{2}\,dx,

\displaystyle F_{2}(t)=-2\int_{\mathbb{R}}g(t,x)\big[\dot{U}(H^{x_{1}(t)}_{-1,0}(x))+\dot{U}(H^{x_{2}(t)}_{0,1}(x))-\dot{U}(H^{x_{2}(t)}_{0,1}(x)+H^{x_{1}(t)}_{-1,0}(x))\big]\,dx,

\displaystyle F_{3}(t)=2\int_{\mathbb{R}}g(t,x)\big[\dot{x}_{1}(t)^{2}\partial^{2}_{x}H^{x_{1}(t)}_{-1,0}(x)+\dot{x}_{2}(t)^{2}\partial^{2}_{x}H^{x_{2}(t)}_{0,1}(x)\big]\,dx,

\displaystyle F_{4}(t)=2\int_{\mathbb{R}}\partial_{t}g(t,x)\partial_{x}g(t,x)(\dot{x}_{1}(t)\omega_{1}(t,x)+\dot{x}_{2}(t)(1-\omega_{1}(t,x)))\,dx,

\displaystyle F_{5}(t)=\frac{1}{3}\int_{\mathbb{R}}U^{(3)}(H^{x_{2}(t)}_{0,1}(x)+H^{x_{1}(t)}_{-1,0}(x))g(t,x)^{3}\,dx.

First, We prove that
\left\lvert\dot{F}(t)\right\rvert\lesssim\delta(t).

The main idea of the proof of this item is to estimate each derivative
\frac{dF_{j}(t)}{dt},
for
1\leq j\leq 5,
with an error of size
O(\delta(t)),
then we will check that the sum of these estimates are going to be a value of order
O(\delta(t)),
which means that the estimates of these derivatives cancel.

Step 1.
(The derivative of
F_{1}(t).
) By definition of
F_{1}(t),
we have that

\frac{dF_{1}(t)}{dt}=2\int_{\mathbb{R}}\left(\partial^{2}_{t}g(t,x)-\partial^{2}_{x}g(t,x)+\ddot{U}(H^{x_{2}(t)}_{0,1}(x)+H^{x_{1}(t)}_{-1,0}(x))g(t,x)\right)\partial_{t}g(t,x)\,dx\\
-\int_{\mathbb{R}}\left(\dot{x}_{1}(t)\partial_{x}H^{x_{1}(t)}_{-1,0}(x)+\dot{x}_{2}(t)\partial_{x}H^{x_{2}(t)}_{0,1}(x)\right)U^{(3)}\left(H^{x_{2}(t)}_{0,1}(x)+H^{x_{1}(t)}_{-1,0}(x)\right)g(t,x)^{2}\,dx.

(156)

Moreover, from the identity (
155
) satisfied by
g(t,x)
, we can rewrite the value of
\frac{dF_{1}(t)}{dt}
as

\frac{dF_{1}(t)}{dt}=2\int_{\mathbb{R}}\left[\dot{U}\left(H^{x_{1}(t)}_{-1,0}(x)\right)+\dot{U}\left(H^{x_{2}(t)}_{0,1}(x)\right)-\dot{U}\left(H^{x_{1}(t)}_{-1,0}(x)+H^{x_{2}(t)}_{0,1}(x)\right)\right]\partial_{t}g(t,x)\,dx\\
-2\int_{\mathbb{R}}\left[U\left(H^{x_{2}(t)}_{0,1}(x)+H^{x_{1}(t)}_{-1,0}(x)+g(t,x)\right)-\dot{U}\left(H^{x_{1}(t)}_{-1,0}(x)+H^{x_{2}(t)}_{0,1}(x)\right)-\ddot{U}\left(H^{x_{2}(t)}_{0,1}(x)+H^{x_{1}(t)}_{-1,0}(x)\right)g(t,x)\right]\partial_{t}g(t,x)\,dx\\
-2\int_{\mathbb{R}}\Big[\dot{x}_{1}(t)^{2}\partial^{2}_{x}H^{x_{1}(t)}_{-1,0}(x)+\dot{x}_{2}(t)^{2}\partial^{2}_{x}H^{x_{2}(t)}_{0,1}(x)\Big]\partial_{t}g(t,x)\,dx+2\int_{\mathbb{R}}\Big[\ddot{x}_{1}(t)\partial_{x}H^{x_{1}(t)}_{-1,0}(x)+\ddot{x}_{2}(t)\partial_{x}H^{x_{2}(t)}_{0,1}(x)\Big]\partial_{t}g(t,x)\,dx\\
-\int_{\mathbb{R}}\Big[\dot{x}_{1}(t)\partial_{x}H^{x_{1}(t)}_{-1,0}(x)+\dot{x}_{2}(t)\partial_{x}H^{x_{2}(t)}_{0,1}(x)\Big]U^{(3)}(H^{x_{2}(t)}_{0,1}(x)+H^{x_{1}(t)}_{-1,0}(x))g(t,x)^{2}\,dx,

and, from the orthogonality conditions of the Modulation Lemma, we obtain

\frac{dF_{1}(t)}{dt}=2\int_{\mathbb{R}}\left[\dot{U}\left(H^{x_{1}(t)}_{-1,0}(x)\right)+\dot{U}\left(H^{x_{2}(t)}_{0,1}(x)\right)-\dot{U}\left(H^{x_{1}(t)}_{-1,0}(x)+H^{x_{2}(t)}_{0,1}(x)\right)\right]\partial_{t}g(t,x)\,dx\\
-2\int_{\mathbb{R}}\left[U\left(H^{x_{2}(t)}_{0,1}(x)+H^{x_{1}(t)}_{-1,0}(x)+g(t,x)\right)-\dot{U}\left(H^{x_{1}(t)}_{-1,0}(x)+H^{x_{2}(t)}_{0,1}(x)\right)-\ddot{U}\left(H^{x_{2}(t)}_{0,1}(x)+H^{x_{1}(t)}_{-1,0}(x)\right)g(t,x)\right]\partial_{t}g(t,x)\,dx\\
-2\int_{\mathbb{R}}\Big[\dot{x}_{1}(t)^{2}\partial^{2}_{x}H^{x_{1}(t)}_{-1,0}(x)+\dot{x}_{2}(t)^{2}\partial^{2}_{x}H^{x_{2}(t)}_{0,1}(x)\Big]\partial_{t}g(t,x)\,dx+2\int_{\mathbb{R}}\Big[\ddot{x}_{1}(t)\dot{x}_{1}(t)\partial^{2}_{x}H^{x_{1}(t)}_{-1,0}(x)+\ddot{x}_{2}(t)\dot{x}_{2}(t)\partial^{2}_{x}H^{x_{2}(t)}_{0,1}(x)\Big]g(t,x)\,dx\\
-\int_{\mathbb{R}}\Big[\dot{x}_{1}(t)\partial_{x}H^{x_{1}(t)}_{-1,0}(x)+\dot{x}_{2}(t)\partial_{x}H^{x_{2}(t)}_{0,1}(x)\Big]U^{(3)}\left(H^{x_{2}(t)}_{0,1}(x)+H^{x_{1}(t)}_{-1,0}(x)\right)g(t,x)^{2}\,dx,

which implies

\frac{dF_{1}(t)}{dt}=2\int_{\mathbb{R}}\left[\dot{U}\left(H^{x_{1}(t)}_{-1,0}(x)\right)+\dot{U}\left(H^{x_{2}(t)}_{0,1}(x)\right)-\dot{U}\left(H^{x_{1}(t)}_{-1,0}(x)+H^{x_{2}(t)}_{0,1}(x)\right)\right]\partial_{t}g(t,x)\,dx\\
-2\int_{\mathbb{R}}\left[U\left(H^{x_{2}(t)}_{0,1}(x)+H^{x_{1}(t)}_{-1,0}(x)+g(t,x)\right)-\dot{U}\left(H^{x_{1}(t)}_{-1,0}(x)+H^{x_{2}(t)}_{0,1}(x)\right)-\ddot{U}\left(H^{x_{2}(t)}_{0,1}(x)+H^{x_{1}(t)}_{-1,0}(x)\right)g(t,x)\right]\partial_{t}g(t,x)\,dx\\
-2\int_{\mathbb{R}}\Big[\dot{x}_{1}(t)^{2}\partial^{2}_{x}H^{x_{1}(t)}_{-1,0}(x)+\dot{x}_{2}(t)^{2}\partial^{2}_{x}H^{x_{2}(t)}_{0,1}(x)\Big]\partial_{t}g(t,x)\,dx\\
-\int_{\mathbb{R}}\Big[\dot{x}_{1}(t)\partial_{x}H^{x_{1}(t)}_{-1,0}(x)+\dot{x}_{2}(t)\partial_{x}H^{x_{2}(t)}_{0,1}(x)\Big]U^{(3)}\left(H^{x_{2}(t)}_{0,1}(x)+H^{x_{1}(t)}_{-1,0}(x)\right)g(t,x)^{2}\,dx+O(\delta(t)).

(157)

Step 2.
(The derivative of
F_{2}(t).
)
It is not difficult to verify that

\frac{dF_{2}(t)}{dt}=-2\int_{\mathbb{R}}\partial_{t}g(t,x)\left[\dot{U}\left(H^{x_{1}(t)}_{-1,0}(x)\right)+\dot{U}\left(H^{x_{2}(t)}_{0,1}(x)\right)-\dot{U}\left(H^{x_{2}(t)}_{0,1}(x)+H^{x_{1}(t)}_{-1,0}(x)\right)\right]\,dx\\
+2\int_{\mathbb{R}}g(t,x)\left[\ddot{U}\left(H^{x_{1}(t)}_{-1,0}(x)\right)\partial_{x}H^{x_{1}(t)}_{-1,0}(x)\dot{x}_{1}(t)+\ddot{U}\left(H^{x_{2}(t)}_{0,1}(x)\right)\partial_{x}H^{x_{2}(t)}_{0,1}(x)\dot{x}_{2}(t)\right]\,dx\\
-2\int_{\mathbb{R}}\ddot{U}\left(H^{x_{2}(t)}_{0,1}(x)+H^{x_{1}(t)}_{-1,0}(x)\right)\left[\partial_{x}H^{x_{1}(t)}_{-1,0}(x)\dot{x}_{1}(t)+\partial_{x}H^{x_{2}(t)}_{0,1}(x)\dot{x}_{2}(t)\right]g(t,x)\,dx.

Since from the definition of the function
U
, we can deduce that

\displaystyle\left|\ddot{U}\left(H^{x_{2}(t)}_{0,1}(x)+H^{x_{1}(t)}_{-1,0}(x)\right)-\ddot{U}\left(H^{x_{1}(t)}_{-1,0}(x)\right)\right|=O\Big(\left|H^{x_{1}(t)}_{-1,0}(x)H^{x_{2}(t)}_{0,1}(x)\right|+\left|H^{x_{2}(t)}_{0,1}(x)\right|^{2}\Big),

\displaystyle|\ddot{U}\left(H^{x_{2}(t)}_{0,1}(x)+H^{x_{1}(t)}_{-1,0}(x)\right)-\ddot{U}\left(H^{x_{2}(t)}_{0,1}(x)\right)|=O\Big(\left|H^{x_{1}(t)}_{-1,0}(x)H^{x_{2}(t)}_{0,1}(x)\right|+\left|H^{x_{1}(t)}_{-1,0}(x)\right|^{2}\Big),

we obtain from Lemma
2.3
and Cauchy-Schwarz Inequality that

\displaystyle\left|\int_{\mathbb{R}}\left[\ddot{U}\left(H^{x_{2}(t)}_{0,1}(x)\right)-\ddot{U}\left(H^{x_{2}(t)}_{0,1}(x)+H^{x_{1}(t)}_{-1,0}(x)\right)\right]\partial_{x}H^{x_{2}(t)}_{0,1}(x)g(t,x)\,dx\right|=O\Big(\left\lVert\overrightarrow{g(t)}\right\rVert e^{-\sqrt{2}z(t)}\Big),

\displaystyle\left|\int_{\mathbb{R}}\left[\ddot{U}\left(H^{x_{1}(t)}_{-1,0}(x)\right)-\ddot{U}\left(H^{x_{2}(t)}_{0,1}(x)+H^{x_{1}(t)}_{-1,0}(x)\right)\right]\partial_{x}H^{x_{1}(t)}_{-1,0}(x)g(t,x)\,dx\right|=O\Big(\left\lVert\overrightarrow{g(t)}\right\rVert e^{-\sqrt{2}z(t)}\Big).

In conclusion, we obtain from the identity satisfied by
\frac{dF_{2}(t)}{dt}
that

\frac{dF_{2}(t)}{dt}=-2\int_{\mathbb{R}}\partial_{t}g(t,x)\left[\dot{U}\left(H^{x_{1}(t)}_{-1,0}(x)\right)+\dot{U}\left(H^{x_{2}(t)}_{0,1}(x)\right)-\dot{U}\left(H^{x_{2}(t)}_{0,1}(x)+H^{x_{1}(t)}_{-1,0}(x)\right)\right]\,dx+O(\delta(t)).

(158)

Step 3.
(The derivative of
F_{3}(t).
)
From the definition of
F_{3}(t),
we obtain that

\frac{dF_{3}(t)}{dt}=2\int_{\mathbb{R}}\partial_{t}g(t,x)\big[\dot{x}_{1}(t)^{2}\partial^{2}_{x}H^{x_{1}(t)}_{-1,0}(x)+\dot{x}_{2}(t)^{2}\partial^{2}_{x}H^{x_{2}(t)}_{0,1}(x)\big]\,dx\\
-2\int_{\mathbb{R}}g(t,x)\big[\dot{x}_{1}(t)^{3}\partial^{3}_{x}H^{x_{1}(t)}_{-1,0}(x)+\dot{x}_{2}(t)^{3}\partial^{3}_{x}H^{x_{2}(t)}_{0,1}(x)\big]\,dx+4\int_{\mathbb{R}}g(t,x)\big[\dot{x}_{1}(t)\ddot{x}_{1}(t)\partial^{2}_{x}H^{x_{1}(t)}_{-1,0}(x)+\dot{x}_{2}(t)\ddot{x}_{2}(t)\partial^{2}_{x}H^{x_{2}(t)}_{0,1}(x)\big]\,dx,

which can be rewritten as

\frac{dF_{3}(t)}{dt}=2\int_{\mathbb{R}}\partial_{t}g(t,x)\big[\dot{x}_{1}(t)^{2}\partial^{2}_{x}H^{x_{1}(t)}_{-1,0}(x)+\dot{x}_{2}(t)^{2}\partial^{2}_{x}H^{x_{2}(t)}_{0,1}(x)\big]\,dx-2\int_{\mathbb{R}}g(t,x)\big[\dot{x}_{1}(t)^{3}\partial^{3}_{x}H^{x_{1}(t)}_{-1,0}(x)+\dot{x}_{2}(t)^{3}\partial^{3}_{x}H^{x_{2}(t)}_{0,1}(x)\big]\,dx\\
+O(\delta(t)).

(159)

Step 4.
(Sum of
\frac{dF_{1}}{dt},\frac{dF_{2}}{dt},\frac{dF_{3}}{dt}.
) If we sum the estimates (
157
), (
158
) and (
159
), we obtain that

\sum_{i=1}^{3}\frac{dF_{i}(t)}{dt}=-2\int_{\mathbb{R}}\Big[\dot{U}\left(H^{x_{2}(t)}_{0,1}(x)+H^{x_{1}(t)}_{-1,0}(x)+g(t,x)\right)-\dot{U}\left(H^{x_{2}(t)}_{0,1}(x)+H^{x_{1}(t)}_{-1,0}(x)\right)\\
-\ddot{U}\left(H^{x_{2}(t)}_{0,1}(x)+H^{x_{1}(t)}_{-1,0}(x)\right)g(t,x)\Big]\partial_{t}g(t,x)\,dx\\
-\int_{\mathbb{R}}\left[\dot{x}_{1}(t)\partial_{x}H^{x_{1}(t)}_{-1,0}(x)+\dot{x}_{2}(t)\partial_{x}H^{x_{2}(t)}_{0,1}(x)\right]U^{(3)}\left(H^{x_{2}(t)}_{0,1}(x)+H^{x_{1}(t)}_{-1,0}(x)\right)g(t,x)^{2}\,dx\\
-2\int_{\mathbb{R}}g(t,x)\left[\dot{x}_{1}(t)^{3}\partial^{3}_{x}H^{x_{1}(t)}_{-1,0}(x)+\dot{x}_{2}(t)^{3}\partial^{3}_{x}H^{x_{2}(t)}_{0,1}(x)\right]\,dx+O(\delta(t)).

(160)

More precisely, from Taylor’s Expansion Theorem and since
\left\lVert\overrightarrow{g(t)}\right\rVert^{4}\leq\delta(t),

\sum_{i=1}^{3}\frac{dF_{i}(t)}{dt}=-\int_{\mathbb{R}}\left[U^{(3)}\left(H^{x_{2}(t)}_{0,1}(x)+H^{x_{1}(t)}_{-1,0}(x)\right)g(t,x)^{2}\right]\partial_{t}g(t,x)\,dx\\
-\int_{\mathbb{R}}\left[\dot{x}_{1}(t)\partial_{x}H^{x_{1}(t)}_{-1,0}(x)+\dot{x}_{2}(t)\partial_{x}H^{x_{2}(t)}_{0,1}(x)\right]U^{(3)}\left(H^{x_{2}(t)}_{0,1}(x)+H^{x_{1}(t)}_{-1,0}(x)\right)g(t,x)^{2}\,dx\\
-2\int_{\mathbb{R}}g(t,x)\left[\dot{x}_{1}(t)^{3}\partial^{3}_{x}H^{x_{1}(t)}_{-1,0}(x)+\dot{x}_{2}(t)^{3}\partial^{3}_{x}H^{x_{2}(t)}_{0,1}(x)\right]\,dx+O(\delta(t)).

(161)

Step 5.
(The derivative of
F_{4}(t).
) The computation of the derivative of
F_{4}(t)
will be more careful, since the motivation for the addition of this term is to cancel with the expression

-\int_{\mathbb{R}}\left[\dot{x}_{1}(t)\partial_{x}H^{x_{1}(t)}_{-1,0}(x)+\dot{x}_{2}(t)\partial_{x}H^{x_{2}(t)}_{0,1}(x)\right]U^{(3)}\left(H^{x_{2}(t)}_{0,1}(x)+H^{x_{1}(t)}_{-1,0}(x)\right)g(t,x)^{2}\,dx

of (
161
). The construction of functional
F_{4}(t)
is based on the
momentum correction term
of Lemma 4.2 of
[
19
]
. To estimate
\frac{dF_{4}(t)}{dt}
with precision of
O(\delta(t))
, it is just necessary to study the time derivative of

2\int_{\mathbb{R}}\partial_{t}g(t,x)\partial_{x}g(t,x)\dot{x}_{1}(t)\omega_{1}(t,x)\,dx,

(162)

since the estimate of the other term in
F_{4}(t)
is completely analogous.
First, we have the identity

\frac{d}{dt}\Big[2\int_{\mathbb{R}}\partial_{t}g(t,x)\partial_{x}g(t,x)\dot{x}_{1}(t)\omega_{1}(t,x)\,dx\Big]=2\ddot{x}_{1}(t)\int_{\mathbb{R}}\omega_{1}(t,x)\partial_{t}g(t,x)\partial_{x}g(t,x)\,dx\\
+2\dot{x}_{1}(t)\int_{\mathbb{R}}\omega_{1}(t,x)\partial^{2}_{t}g(t,x)\partial_{x}g(t,x)\,dx+2\dot{x}_{1}(t)\int_{\mathbb{R}}\partial_{t}\omega_{1}(t,x)\partial_{t}g(t,x)\partial_{x}g(t,x)\,dx+2\dot{x}_{1}(t)\int_{\mathbb{R}}\omega_{1}(t,x)\partial^{2}_{t,x}g(t,x)\partial_{t}g(t,x)\,dx.

(163)

From the definition of
\omega_{1}(t,x)=\omega\big(\frac{x-x_{1}(t)}{x_{2}(t)-x_{1}(t)}\big)
, we have

\partial_{t}\omega_{1}(t,x)=\dot{\omega}\Big(\frac{x-x_{1}(t)}{x_{2}(t)-x_{1}(t)}\Big)\Big(\frac{-\dot{x}_{1}(t)z(t)-\dot{z}(t)(x-x_{1}(t))}{z(t)^{2}}\Big).

(164)

Since in the support of
\dot{\omega}(x)
is contained in the set
\frac{3}{4}\leq x\leq\frac{4}{5}
, we obtain the following estimate:

2\dot{x}_{1}(t)\int_{\mathbb{R}}\partial_{t}\omega_{1}(t,x)\partial_{t}g(t,x)\partial_{x}g(t,x)\,dx=O\Big(\max_{j\in\{1,2\}}\frac{|\dot{x}_{j}(t)|}{z(t)}\left\lVert\overrightarrow{g(t)}\right\rVert^{2}\Big)=O(\delta(t)).

(165)

Clearly from integration by parts, we deduce that

2\dot{x}_{1}(t)\int_{\mathbb{R}}\omega_{1}(t,x)\partial^{2}_{t,x}g(t,x)\partial_{t}g(t,x)\,dx=O\Big(\max_{j\in\{1,2\}}\frac{\left\lvert\dot{x}_{j}(t)\right\rvert}{z(t)}\left\lVert\overrightarrow{g(t)}\right\rVert^{2}\Big)=O(\delta(t)).

(166)

Also, we have

2\ddot{x}_{1}(t)\int_{\mathbb{R}}\omega_{1}(t,x)\partial_{t}g(t,x)\partial_{x}g(t,x)\,dx=O\Big(\max_{j\in\{1,2\}}\left\lvert\ddot{x}_{j}(t)\right\rvert\left\lVert\overrightarrow{g(t)}\right\rVert^{2}\Big)=O(\delta(t)).

(167)

So, to estimate the time derivative of (
162
) with precision
O(\delta(t)),
it is enough to estimate

2\dot{x}_{1}(t)\int_{\mathbb{R}}\omega_{1}(t,x)\partial^{2}_{t}g(t,x)\partial_{x}g(t,x)\,dx.

We have that

2\dot{x}_{1}(t)\int_{\mathbb{R}}\omega_{1}(t,x)\partial^{2}_{t}g(t,x)\partial_{x}g(t,x)\,dx=2\dot{x}_{1}(t)\int_{\mathbb{R}}\omega_{1}(t,x)\partial^{2}_{x}g(t,x)\partial_{x}g(t,x)\,dx\\
-2\dot{x}_{1}(t)\int_{\mathbb{R}}\omega_{1}(t,x)\ddot{U}\left(H^{x_{1}(t)}_{-1,0}(x)+H^{x_{2}(t)}_{0,1}(x)\right)g(t,x)\partial_{x}g(t,x)\,dx\\
+2\dot{x}_{1}(t)\int_{\mathbb{R}}\omega_{1}(t,x)\left[\partial^{2}_{t}g(t,x)-\partial^{2}_{x}g(t,x)+\ddot{U}\left(H^{x_{1}(t)}_{-1,0}(x)+H^{x_{2}(t)}_{0,1}(x)\right)g(t,x)\right]\partial_{x}g(t,x)\,dx.

(168)

From integration by parts, the first term of the equation (
168
) satisfies

2\dot{x}_{1}(t)\int_{\mathbb{R}}\omega_{1}(t,x)\partial^{2}_{x}g(t,x)\partial_{x}g(t,x)\,dx=O\Big(\max_{j\in\{1,2\}}\frac{\left\lvert\dot{x}_{j}(t)\right\rvert}{z(t)}\left\lVert\overrightarrow{g(t)}\right\rVert^{2}\Big)=O(\delta(t)).

(169)

From Taylor’s Expansion Theorem, we have that

\left\lVert\dot{U}\left(H^{x_{2}(t)}_{0,1}+H^{x_{1}(t)}_{-1,0}+g(t)\right)-\dot{U}\left(H^{x_{2}(t)}_{0,1}+H^{x_{1}(t)}_{-1,0}\right)-\ddot{U}\left(H^{x_{2}(t)}_{0,1}+H^{x_{1}(t)}_{-1,0}\right)g(t)-U^{(3)}\left(H^{x_{2}(t)}_{0,1}+H^{x_{1}(t)}_{-1,0}\right)\frac{g(t)^{2}}{2}\right\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R})}\\
=O\Big(\left\lVert\overrightarrow{g(t)}\right\rVert^{3}\Big).

(170)

Also, we have verified the identity

\dot{U}(\phi)+\dot{U}(\theta)-\dot{U}(\phi+\theta)=24\phi\theta(\phi+\theta)-6\Big(\sum_{j=1}^{4}\begin{pmatrix}5\\
j\end{pmatrix}\phi^{j}\theta^{5-j}\Big),

which clearly with the inequalities (D1), (D2) and Lemma
2.3
imply the estimate

\left\lVert\dot{U}\left(H^{x_{2}(t)}_{0,1}\right)+\dot{U}\left(H^{x_{1}(t)}_{-1,0}\right)-\dot{U}\left(H^{x_{2}(t)}_{0,1}+H^{x_{1}(t)}_{-1,0}\right)\right\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R})}=O(e^{-\sqrt{2}z(t)}).

(171)

Finally, is not difficult to verify that

\left\lVert-\dot{x}_{1}(t)^{2}\partial^{2}_{x}H^{x_{1}(t)}_{-1,0}-\dot{x}_{2}(t)^{2}\partial^{2}_{x}H^{x_{2}(t)}_{0,1}+\ddot{x}_{1}(t)\partial_{x}H^{x_{1}(t)}_{-1,0}+\ddot{x}_{2}(t)\partial_{x}H^{x_{2}(t)}_{0,1}\right\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R})}=O\left(\max_{j\in\{1,2\}}\left\lvert\dot{x}_{j}(t)\right\rvert^{2}+\left\lvert\ddot{x}_{j}(t)\right\rvert\right).

(172)

Then, from estimates (
170
), (
171
) and (
172
) and the Partial Differential Equation (
155
) satisfied by
g(t,x)
, we can obtain the estimate

2\dot{x}_{1}(t)\int_{\mathbb{R}}\omega_{1}(t,x)\left[\partial^{2}_{t}g(t,x)-\partial^{2}_{x}g(t,x)+\ddot{U}\left(H^{x_{1}(t)}_{-1,0}(x)+H^{x_{2}(t)}_{0,1}(x)\right)g(t,x)\right]\partial_{x}g(t,x)\,dx=\\
-\dot{x}_{1}(t)\int_{\mathbb{R}}\omega_{1}(t,x)U^{(3)}\left(H^{x_{1}(t)}_{-1,0}(x)+H^{x_{2}(t)}_{0,1}(x)\right)g(t,x)^{2}\partial_{x}g(t,x)\,dx-2\dot{x}_{1}(t)^{3}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\partial^{2}_{x}H^{x_{1}(t)}_{-1,0}(x)\partial_{x}g(t,x)\,dx\\
-2\dot{x}_{1}(t)^{3}\int_{\mathbb{R}}(\omega_{1}(t,x)-1)\partial^{2}_{x}H^{x_{1}(t)}_{-1,0}(x)\partial_{x}g(t,x)\,dx-2\dot{x}_{1}(t)\dot{x}_{2}(t)^{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\omega_{1}(t,x)\partial^{2}_{x}H^{x_{2}(t)}_{0,1}(x)\partial_{x}g(t,x)\,dx\\
+O\Big(\max_{j\in\{1,2\}}\left\lvert\ddot{x}_{j}(t)\dot{x}_{j}(t)\right\rvert\left\lVert\overrightarrow{g(t)}\right\rVert+e^{-\sqrt{2}z(t)}\max_{j\in\{1,2\}}\left\lvert\dot{x}_{j}(t)\right\rvert\left\lVert\overrightarrow{g(t)}\right\rVert+\left\lVert\overrightarrow{g(t)}\right\rVert^{4}\max_{j\in\{1,2\}}\left\lvert\dot{x}_{j}(t)\right\rvert\Big),

which, by integration by parts and by Cauchy-Schwarz inequality using the estimate (
125
) for
\omega_{1},
we obtain that

2\dot{x}_{1}(t)\int_{\mathbb{R}}\omega_{1}(t,x)\left[\partial^{2}_{t}g(t,x)-\partial^{2}_{x}g(t,x)+\ddot{U}\left(H^{x_{1}(t)}_{-1,0}(x)+H^{x_{2}(t)}_{0,1}(x)\right)g(t,x)\right]\partial_{x}g(t,x)\,dx=\\
\frac{\dot{x}_{1}(t)}{3}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\omega_{1}(t)U^{(4)}\left(H^{x_{1}(t)}_{-1,0}+H^{x_{2}(t)}_{0,1}\right)\left[\partial_{x}H^{x_{1}(t)}_{-1,0}+\partial_{x}H^{x_{2}(t)}_{0,1}\right]g(t)^{3}\,dx+O\Big(\max_{j\in\{1,2\}}\frac{\left\lvert\dot{x}_{j}(t)\right\rvert}{z(t)}\left\lVert\overrightarrow{g(t)}\right\rVert^{3}\Big)\\
-2\dot{x}_{1}(t)^{3}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\partial^{2}_{x}H^{x_{1}(t)}_{-1,0}(x)\partial_{x}g(t,x)\,dx+O\Big(\max_{j\in\{1,2\}}\left\lvert\dot{x}_{j}(t)\right\rvert^{3}e^{-\frac{\sqrt{2}z(t)}{5}}\left\lVert\overrightarrow{g(t)}\right\rVert\Big)+O(\delta(t)).

(173)

Now, to finish the estimate of

2\dot{x}_{1}(t)\int_{\mathbb{R}}\omega_{1}(t,x)\partial^{2}_{t}g(t,x)\partial_{x}g(t,x)\,dx,

it remains to study the integral given by

-2\dot{x}_{1}(t)\int_{\mathbb{R}}\omega_{1}(t,x)\ddot{U}\left(H^{x_{1}(t)}_{-1,0}(x)+H^{x_{2}(t)}_{0,1}(x)\right)g(t,x)\partial_{x}g(t,x)\,dx,

(174)

which by integration by parts is equal to

\dot{x}_{1}(t)\int_{\mathbb{R}}\omega_{1}(t,x)U^{(3)}\left(H^{x_{1}(t)}_{-1,0}(x)+H^{x_{2}(t)}_{0,1}(x)\right)\big[\partial_{x}H^{x_{1}(t)}_{-1,0}(x)+\partial_{x}H^{x_{2}(t)}_{0,1}(x)\big]g(t,x)^{2}\,dx+O(\delta(t)).

(175)

Since the support of
\omega_{1}(t,x)
is included in
\{x|\,(x-x_{2}(t))\leq-\frac{z(t)}{5}\}
and the support of
1-\omega_{1}(t,x)
is included in
\{x|\,(x-x_{1}(t))\geq\frac{3z(t)}{4}\},
from the exponential decay properties of the kink solutions in (D1), (D2), (D3), (D4) we obtain the estimates

\displaystyle\left\lvert\dot{x}_{1}(t)\int_{\mathbb{R}}(\omega_{1}(t,x)-1)U^{(3)}\left(H^{x_{1}(t)}_{-1,0}(x)+H^{x_{2}(t)}_{0,1}(x)\right)(\partial_{x}H^{x_{1}(t)}_{-1,0}(x))g(t,x)^{2}\,dx\right\rvert=O(\delta(t)),

(176)

\displaystyle\left\lvert\dot{x}_{2}(t)\int_{\mathbb{R}}\omega_{1}(t,x)U^{(3)}\left(H^{x_{1}(t)}_{-1,0}(x)+H^{x_{2}(t)}_{0,1}(x)\right)\partial_{x}H^{x_{2}(t)}_{0,1}(x)g(t,x)^{2}\,dx\right\rvert=O(\delta(t)),

(177)

\displaystyle\left\lvert\frac{1}{3}\dot{x}_{1}(t)\int_{\mathbb{R}}(1-\omega_{1}(t))U^{(4)}(H^{x_{1}(t)}_{-1,0}+H^{x_{2}(t)}_{0,1})\partial_{x}H^{x_{1}(t)}_{-1,0}g(t)^{3}\,dt\right\rvert=O(\delta(t)),

(178)

\displaystyle\left\lvert\frac{1}{3}\dot{x}_{2}(t)\int_{\mathbb{R}}(\omega_{1}(t))U^{(4)}\left(H^{x_{1}(t)}_{-1,0}+H^{x_{2}(t)}_{0,1}\right)\partial_{x}H^{x_{2}(t)}_{0,1}g(t)^{3}\,dt\right\rvert=O(\delta(t)).

(179)

In conclusion, we obtain that the estimates (
176
), (
177
) imply the following estimate

-2\dot{x}_{1}(t)\int_{\mathbb{R}}\omega_{1}(t,x)\ddot{U}\left(H^{x_{1}(t)}_{-1,0}(x)+H^{x_{2}(t)}_{0,1}(x)\right)g(t,x)\partial_{x}g(t,x)\,dx=\int_{\mathbb{R}}\dot{x}_{1}(t)\partial_{x}H^{x_{1}(t)}_{-1,0}(x)U^{(3)}\left(H^{x_{2}(t)}_{0,1}+H^{x_{1}(t)}_{-1,0}\right)g(t)^{2}\,dx\\
+O(\delta(t)).

(180)

Then, the estimates (
168
), (
173
), (
178
), (
179
) and (
180
) imply that

\displaystyle 2\frac{d}{dt}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}}\partial_{t}g(t,x)\partial_{x}g(t,x)\dot{x}_{1}(t)\omega_{1}(t,x)\,dx\right)=\frac{1}{3}\int_{\mathbb{R}}U^{(4)}\left(H^{x_{1}(t)}_{-1,0}+H^{x_{2}(t)}_{0,1}\right)\big(\dot{x}_{1}(t)\partial_{x}H^{x_{1}(t)}_{-1,0}\big)g(t)^{3}\,dx

\displaystyle+\int_{\mathbb{R}}\big(\dot{x}_{1}(t)\partial_{x}H^{x_{1}(t)}_{-1,0})U^{(3)}(H^{x_{2}(t)}_{0,1}+H^{x_{1}(t)}_{-1,0})g(t)^{2}\,dx-2\dot{x}_{1}(t)^{3}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\partial^{2}_{x}H^{x_{1}(t)}_{-1,0}(x)\partial_{x}g(t,x)\,dx.+O(\delta(t)).

By an analogous argument, we deduce that

\displaystyle 2\frac{d}{dt}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}}\partial_{t}g(t,x)\partial_{x}g(t,x)\dot{x}_{2}(t)(1-\omega_{1}(t,x))\,dx\right)=\frac{1}{3}\int_{\mathbb{R}}U^{(4)}\left(H^{x_{1}(t)}_{-1,0}+H^{x_{2}(t)}_{0,1}\right)\big(\dot{x}_{2}(t)\partial_{x}H^{x_{2}(t)}_{0,1}\big)g(t)^{3}\,dx

\displaystyle+\int_{\mathbb{R}}\big(\dot{x}_{2}(t)\partial_{x}H^{x_{2}(t)}_{0,1})U^{(3)}\left(H^{x_{2}(t)}_{0,1}+H^{x_{1}(t)}_{-1,0}\right)g(t)^{2}\,dx-2\dot{x}_{2}(t)^{3}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\partial^{2}_{x}H^{x_{2}(t)}_{0,1}(x)\partial_{x}g(t,x)\,dx+O(\delta(t)).

In conclusion, we have that

\frac{dF_{4}(t)}{dt}=\int_{\mathbb{R}}\left[\dot{x}_{1}(t)\partial_{x}H^{x_{1}(t)}_{-1,0}+\dot{x}_{2}(t)\partial_{x}H^{x_{2}(t)}_{0,1}\right]U^{(3)}\left(H^{x_{2}(t)}_{0,1}+H^{x_{1}(t)}_{-1,0}\right)g(t)^{2}\,dx-2\dot{x}_{2}(t)^{3}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\partial^{2}_{x}H^{x_{2}(t)}_{0,1}(x)\partial_{x}g(t,x)\,dx\\
+\frac{1}{3}\int_{\mathbb{R}}U^{(4)}\left(H^{x_{1}(t)}_{-1,0}+H^{x_{2}(t)}_{0,1}\right)\left[\dot{x}_{1}(t)\partial_{x}H^{x_{1}(t)}_{-1,0}+\dot{x}_{2}(t)\partial_{x}H^{x_{2}(t)}_{0,1}\right]g(t)^{3}\,dx-2\dot{x}_{1}(t)^{3}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\partial^{2}_{x}H^{x_{1}(t)}_{-1,0}(x)\partial_{x}g(t,x)\,dx+O(\delta(t)).

(181)

Step 6.
(The derivative of
F_{5}(t).
) We have that

\frac{dF_{5}(t)}{dt}=\int_{\mathbb{R}}U^{(3)}\left(H^{x_{1}(t)}_{-1,0}+H^{x_{2}(t)}_{0,1}\right)g(t)^{2}\partial_{t}g(t)\,dx-\frac{1}{3}\int_{\mathbb{R}}U^{(4)}\left(H^{x_{1}(t)}_{-1,0}+H^{x_{2}(t)}_{0,1}\right)\big(\dot{x}_{1}(t)\partial_{x}H^{x_{1}(t)}_{-1,0}+\dot{x}_{2}(t)\partial_{x}H^{x_{2}(t)}_{0,1}\big)g(t)^{3}\,dx.

(182)

Step 7.
(Conclusion of estimate of
|\dot{F}(t)|
)
From the identities (
182
) and (
181
), we obtain that

\frac{dF_{4}(t)}{dt}+\frac{dF_{5}(t)}{dt}=\int_{\mathbb{R}}\big(\dot{x}_{1}(t)\partial_{x}H^{x_{1}(t)}_{-1,0}+\dot{x}_{2}(t)\partial_{x}H^{x_{2}(t)}_{0,1}\big)U^{(3)}\left(H^{x_{2}(t)}_{0,1}+H^{x_{1}(t)}_{-1,0}\right)g(t)^{2}\,dx-2\dot{x}_{1}(t)^{3}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\partial^{2}_{x}H^{x_{1}(t)}_{-1,0}(x)\partial_{x}g(t,x)\,dx\\
-2\dot{x}_{2}(t)^{3}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\partial^{2}_{x}H^{x_{2}(t)}_{0,1}(x)\partial_{x}g(t,x)\,dx+\int_{\mathbb{R}}U^{(3)}\left(H^{x_{1}(t)}_{-1,0}+H^{x_{2}(t)}_{0,1}\right)g(t)^{2}\partial_{t}g(t)\,dx+O(\delta(t)).

(183)

Then, the sum of identities (
161
) and (
183
) implies

\sum_{i=1}^{5}\frac{dF_{i}(t)}{dt}=O(\delta(t)),

this finishes the proof of inequality
\left\lvert\dot{F}(t)\right\rvert=O(\delta(t)).\\

Proof of
F(t)+A_{1}\epsilon^{2}\geq A_{2}\epsilon^{2}.
The Coercitivity Lemma implies that
\exists\,c>0,
such that

F_{1}(t)\geq c\left\lVert\overrightarrow{g(t)}\right\rVert^{2}.

Also, from Theorem
2.9
, we have the global estimate

\max_{j\in\{1,2\}}\left\lvert\dot{x}_{j}(t)\right\rvert^{2}+\left\lvert\ddot{x}_{j}(t)\right\rvert+e^{-\sqrt{2}z(t)}+\left\lVert\overrightarrow{g(t)}\right\rVert^{2}=O(\epsilon)

(184)

that implies that
\left\lvert F_{3}(t)\right\rvert=O\Big(\left\lVert\overrightarrow{g(t)}\right\rVert\epsilon\Big),\,\left\lvert F_{4}(t)\right\rvert=O\Big(\left\lVert\overrightarrow{g(t)}\right\rVert^{2}\epsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}\Big),\,\left\lvert F_{5}(t)\right\rvert=O\Big(\left\lVert\overrightarrow{g(t)}\right\rVert^{2}\epsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}\Big).

Also, since

\left\lvert U\left(H^{x_{1}(t)}_{-1,0}(x)\right)+\dot{U}\left(H^{x_{2}(t)}_{0,1}(x)\right)-\dot{U}\left(H^{x_{2}(t)}_{0,1}(x)+H^{x_{1}(t)}_{-1,0}(x)\right)\right\rvert=O\Big(\left\lvert H^{x_{1}(t)}_{-1,0}(x)H^{x_{2}(t)}_{0,1}(x)\big[H^{x_{2}(t)}_{0,1}(x)+H^{x_{1}(t)}_{-1,0}(x)\big]\right\rvert\Big),

the Lemma
2.3
and Cauchy-Schwarz Inequality imply that

|F_{2}(t)|=O\Big(\left\lVert\overrightarrow{g(t)}\right\rVert e^{-\sqrt{2}z(t)}\Big).

Then, the conclusion of
F(t)+A_{1}\epsilon^{2}\geq A_{2}\left\lVert\overrightarrow{g(t)}\right\rVert^{2}
follows from Young Inequality for
\epsilon
small enough.
∎

Remark 4.3
.

In the proof of Theorem
4.1
, from Theorem
2.9
we have
\left\lvert F_{2}(t)\right\rvert+\left\lvert F_{3}(t)\right\rvert=O\left(\left\lVert\overrightarrow{g(t)}\right\rVert\epsilon\right).
Since
\left\lvert F_{4}(t)\right\rvert+\left\lvert F_{5}(t)\right\rvert=O\left(\left\lVert\overrightarrow{g(t)}\right\rVert^{2}\epsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}\right)
and
\left\lvert F_{1}(t)\right\rvert\lesssim\left\lVert\overrightarrow{g(t)}\right\rVert^{2},
then Young Inequality implies that

\left\lvert F(t)\right\rvert\lesssim\left\lVert\overrightarrow{g(t)}\right\rVert^{2}+\epsilon^{2}.

Remark 4.4

(General Energy Estimate)
.

For any
0<\theta,\gamma<1,
we can create a smooth cut function
0\leq\chi(x)\leq 1
such that

\chi(x)=\begin{cases}0,\text{ if $x\leq\theta(1-\gamma),$}\\
1,\text{if $x\geq\theta.$}\end{cases}

We define

\chi_{0}(t,x)=\chi\left(\frac{x-x_{1}(t)}{x_{2}(t)-x_{1}(t)}\right).

If we consider the following functional

L(t)=\big\langle D^{2}E(H^{x_{2}(t)}_{0,1}+H^{x_{1}(t)}_{-1,0})\overrightarrow{g(t)},\,\overrightarrow{g(t)}\big\rangle_{L^{2}\times L^{2}}+2\int_{\mathbb{R}}\partial_{t}g(t,x)\partial_{x}g(t,x)\Big[\dot{x}_{1}(t)\chi_{0}(t,x)+\dot{x}_{2}(t)(1-\chi_{0}(t,x))\Big]\,dx\\
-2\int_{\mathbb{R}}g(t,x)\Big(\dot{U}(H^{x_{1}(t)}_{-1,0}(x))+\dot{U}(H^{x_{2}(t)}_{0,1}(x))-\dot{U}(H^{x_{2}(t)}_{0,1}(x)+H^{x_{1}(t)}_{-1,0}(x))\Big)\,dx\\
+2\int_{\mathbb{R}}g(t,x)\Big[(\dot{x}_{1}(t))^{2}\partial^{2}_{x}H^{x_{1}(t)}_{-1,0}(x)+(\dot{x}_{2}(t))^{2}\partial^{2}_{x}H^{x_{2}(t)}_{0,1}(x)\Big]\,dx+\frac{1}{3}\int_{\mathbb{R}}U^{(3)}(H^{x_{2}(t)}_{0,1}(x)+H^{x_{1}(t)}_{-1,0}(x))g(t,x)^{3}\,dx,

(185)

then, by a similar proof to the Theorem
4.1
, we obtain that if
0<\epsilon\ll 1
and

\delta_{1}(t)=\delta(t)+\max_{j\in\{1,2\}}\left\lvert\dot{x}_{j}(t)\right\rvert^{3}\max(e^{-\sqrt{2}z(t)(1-\theta)},e^{-\sqrt{2}z(t)\theta(1-\gamma)})\left\lVert\overrightarrow{g(t)}\right\rVert-\max_{j\in\{1,2\}}\left\lvert\dot{x}_{j}(t)\right\rvert^{3}e^{-\frac{\sqrt{2}}{5}z(t)}\left\lVert\overrightarrow{g(t)}\right\rVert,

(186)

then there are positive constants
A_{1},\,A_{2}>0
such that

\left\lvert\dot{L}(t)\right\rvert=O(\delta_{1}(t)),\,L(t)+A_{1}\epsilon^{2}\geq A_{2}\epsilon^{2}.

Our first application of Theorem
4.1
is to estimate the size of the remainder
\left\lVert\overrightarrow{g(t)}\right\rVert
during a long time interval. More precisely, this corresponds to the following theorem, which is a weaker version of Theorem
1.7
.

Theorem 4.5
.

There is
\delta>0,
such that if
0<\epsilon<\delta
enough,
(\phi(0),\partial_{t}\phi(0))\in S\times L^{2}(\mathbb{R})

and
E_{total}(\phi(0),\partial_{t}\phi(0))=2E_{pot}(H_{0,1})+\epsilon,
then
there are
x_{2},x_{1}\in C^{2}(\mathbb{R})
functions such that the unique global time solution of (
1
) is given, for

\phi(t)=H_{0,1}(x-x_{2}(t))+H_{-1,0}(x-x_{1}(t))+g(t),

(187)

with
g(t)
satisfying orthogonality conditions of the Modulation Lemma and

\left\lVert(g(t),\partial_{t}g(t))\right\rVert_{H^{1}\times L^{2}}^{2}\leq C\left[\left\lVert(g(0,x),\partial_{t}g(0,x))\right\rVert_{H^{1}\times L^{2}}^{2}+\epsilon^{2}\ln{\Big(\frac{1}{\epsilon}\Big)}^{2}\right]\exp\left(C\left\lvert t\right\rvert\left(\frac{\epsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}}{\ln{(\frac{1}{\epsilon})}}\right)\right).

(188)

Proof of Theorem
4.5
.

In notation of Theorem
4.1
, from Theorem
4.1
and Remark
4.3
, there are uniform positive constants
A_{2},\,A_{1}
such that for all
t\geq 0

A_{2}\left\lVert\overrightarrow{g(t)}\right\rVert^{2}\leq F(t)+A_{1}\epsilon^{2}\leq C\Big(\left\lVert\overrightarrow{g(t)}\right\rVert^{2}+\epsilon^{2}\Big).

(189)

From now on, we denote

G(t)\coloneqq F(t)+A_{1}\epsilon^{2}\ln{(\frac{1}{\epsilon})}^{2}.
From the inequality (
189
) and Remark
4.2
, there is a constant
C>0
such that, for all
t\geq 0,

G(t)
satisfies

G(t)\leq G(0)+C\Big(\int_{0}^{t}G(s)\frac{\epsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}}{\ln{(\frac{1}{\epsilon})}},ds\Big).

(190)

In conclusion, from the Fundamental Theorem of Calculus, we obtain that

G(t)\leq G(0)\exp\Big(\frac{Ct\epsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}}{\ln{(\frac{1}{\epsilon})}}\Big).

Then, from the definition of
G
and inequality (
189
), we verify the inequality (
188
).
∎

5
Global Dynamics of Modulation Parameters

Lemma 5.1
.

In notation of Theorem
1.7
,
\exists C>0,
such that if the hypotheses of Theorem
1.7
are true,
then for
(g_{0}(x),g_{1}(x))=\left(g(0,x),\partial_{t}g(0,x)\right)
we have that there are functions
p_{1}(t),\,p_{2}(t)\in C^{1}(R_{\geq 0}),
such that for
j\in\{1,\,2\},
we have:

\left\lvert\dot{x}_{j}(t)-p_{j}(t)\right\rvert\lesssim\Big(\left\lVert(g_{0},g_{1})\right\rVert_{H^{1}\times L^{2}}+\epsilon\ln{\Big(\frac{1}{\epsilon}\Big)}\Big)\epsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}\exp\Big(\frac{2C\epsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}t}{\ln{(\frac{1}{\epsilon})}}\Big),

(191)

\left\lvert\dot{p}_{j}(t)-(-1)^{j}8\sqrt{2}e^{-\sqrt{2}z(t)}\right\rvert\lesssim\frac{\Big(\left\lVert(g_{0},g_{1})\right\rVert_{H^{1}\times L^{2}}+\epsilon\ln{\Big(\frac{1}{\epsilon}\Big)}\Big)^{2}}{\ln{\ln{(\frac{1}{\epsilon})}}}\exp\Big(\frac{2Ct\epsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}}{\ln{(\frac{1}{\epsilon})}}\Big).

(192)

Proof.

In the notation of Lemma
3.1
, we consider the functions
p_{j}(t)
for
j\in\{1,\,2\}
and we consider
\theta=\frac{1-\gamma}{2-\gamma},
the value of
\gamma
will be chosen later. From Lemma
3.1
, we have
that

\left\lvert\dot{x}_{j}(t)-p_{j}(t)\right\rvert\lesssim\Big[1+\frac{1}{\gamma z(t)}\Big]\Big(\max_{j\in\{1,2\}}\left\lvert\dot{x}_{j}(t)\right\rvert\left\lVert\overrightarrow{g(t)}\right\rVert+\left\lVert\overrightarrow{g(t)}\right\rVert^{2}\Big)+\max_{j\in\{1,2\}}\left\lvert\dot{x}_{j}(t)\right\rvert z(t)e^{-\sqrt{2}z(t)}.

We recall from Theorem
2.9
the estimates
\max_{j\in\{1,\,2\}}\left\lvert\dot{x}_{j}(t)\right\rvert=O(\epsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}),\,e^{-\sqrt{2}z(t)}=O(\epsilon).

From Theorem
4.5
, we have that

\left\lVert\overrightarrow{g(t)}\right\rVert\lesssim\left(\left\lVert\overrightarrow{g(0)}\right\rVert+\epsilon\ln{\Big(\frac{1}{\epsilon}\Big)}\right)\exp\Big(\frac{C\epsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}t}{\ln{(\frac{1}{\epsilon})}}\Big).

To simplify our computations we denote
c_{0}=\frac{\left\lVert\overrightarrow{g(0)}\right\rVert+\epsilon\ln(\frac{1}{\epsilon})}{\epsilon\ln(\frac{1}{\epsilon})}.

Then, we obtain for
j\in\{1,\,2\}
that

\left\lvert\dot{x}_{j}(t)-p_{j}(t)\right\rvert\lesssim\left[1+\frac{1}{\gamma\ln{(\frac{1}{\epsilon})}}\right]\Big(c_{0}\epsilon^{\frac{3}{2}}\ln{\Big(\frac{1}{\epsilon}\Big)}\exp\Big(\frac{C\epsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}t}{\ln{(\frac{1}{\epsilon})}}\Big)+c_{0}^{2}\epsilon^{2}\ln{\Big(\frac{1}{\epsilon}\Big)}^{2}\exp\Big(\frac{2C\epsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}t}{\ln{\frac{1}{\epsilon}}}\Big)\Big).

(193)

Since
e^{-\sqrt{2}z(t)}\lesssim\epsilon,
we deduce for
\epsilon\ll 1
that
z(t)e^{-\sqrt{2}z(t)}\lesssim\epsilon\ln{(\frac{1}{\epsilon})}<\epsilon^{1-\frac{\gamma}{(2-\gamma)2}}\ln{(\frac{1}{\epsilon})}.
Then, we obtain from the same estimates and the definition (
105
) of
\alpha(t),
that

\alpha(t)\lesssim c_{0}^{2}\Big(\epsilon\ln{\Big(\frac{1}{\epsilon}\Big)}\Big)^{2}\left[\max_{k\in\{1,\,2\}}\Big(\frac{1}{\gamma z(t)}\Big)^{k}+\epsilon^{\frac{1-\gamma}{2-\gamma}}\right]\exp\Big(2\frac{C\epsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}t}{\ln{(\frac{1}{\epsilon})}}\Big)\\
+c_{0}\epsilon^{2-\frac{\gamma}{(2-\gamma)2}}\ln{\Big(\frac{1}{\epsilon}\Big)}\exp\Big(\frac{C\epsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}t}{\ln{(\frac{1}{\epsilon})}}\Big)\left[1+\frac{1}{\gamma z(t)}+\frac{\epsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}}{(\gamma z(t))^{2}}\right]+\frac{\epsilon^{1+\frac{2(1-\gamma)}{2-\gamma}}}{z(t)\gamma}.

(194)

However, if

\gamma\ln{(\frac{1}{\epsilon})}\leq 1
and
z(0)\cong\ln{(\frac{1}{\epsilon})},
which is possible, then the right-hand side of inequality (
194
) is greater than or equivalent to

\epsilon^{2}\ln{(\frac{1}{\epsilon})}^{2}
while
t\lesssim\frac{\ln{(\frac{1}{\epsilon})}}{\epsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}}.

But, it is not difficult to verify that for

\gamma=\frac{\ln{\ln{(\frac{1}{\epsilon})}}}{\ln{(\frac{1}{\epsilon})}},

the right-hand side of inequality (
194
) is smaller than
\epsilon^{2}\ln{(\frac{1}{\epsilon})}^{2}
. In conclusion, from now on, we are going to study the right-hand side of (
194
) for

\frac{1}{\ln(\frac{1}{\epsilon})}<\gamma<1
. Since we know that
\ln{(\frac{1}{\epsilon})}\lesssim z(t)
from Theorem
2.9
, the inequality (
194
) implies for
\frac{1}{\ln{(\frac{1}{\epsilon})}}<\gamma<1
that

\alpha(t)\lesssim\beta(t)\coloneqq\Big(c_{0}\epsilon\ln{\Big(\frac{1}{\epsilon}\Big)}\Big)^{2}\left[\frac{1}{\gamma\ln{(\frac{1}{\epsilon}})}+\epsilon^{\frac{1-\gamma}{2-\gamma}}\right]\exp\Big(2\frac{C\epsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}t}{\ln{(\frac{1}{\epsilon})}}\Big)+c_{0}\epsilon^{2-\frac{\gamma}{2(2-\gamma)}}\ln{\Big(\frac{1}{\epsilon}\Big)}\exp\Big(\frac{C\epsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}t}{\ln{(\frac{1}{\epsilon})}}\Big)+\frac{\epsilon^{1+\frac{2(1-\gamma)}{2-\gamma}}}{\gamma\ln{(\frac{1}{\epsilon})}}\\
=\beta_{1}(t)+\beta_{2}(t)+\beta_{3}(t),\text{ respectively.}

(195)

For
\epsilon>0
small enough, it is not difficult to verify that if
\beta_{3}(t)\geq\beta_{1}(t),
then
\gamma\geq\frac{\ln{\ln{(\frac{1}{\epsilon})}}}{\ln{(\frac{1}{\epsilon})}}.
Moreover, if we have that

1>\gamma>8\frac{\ln{\ln{(\frac{1}{\epsilon})}}}{\ln{(\frac{1}{\epsilon})}},
we obtain from the following estimate

\beta_{3}(t)=\frac{\epsilon^{2}\epsilon^{\frac{-\gamma}{2-\gamma}}}{\gamma\ln{(\frac{1}{\epsilon})}}>\frac{\epsilon^{2}}{\ln{(\frac{1}{\epsilon})}}\exp\left(\frac{8\ln{\ln\left(\frac{1}{\epsilon}\right)}}{2-\gamma}\right)=\frac{\epsilon^{2}}{\ln{(\frac{1}{\epsilon})}}\ln{\left(\frac{1}{\epsilon}\right)}^{\frac{8}{2-\gamma}},

that
\beta_{3}(t)>\frac{\epsilon^{2}\ln{(\frac{1}{\epsilon})}^{2}}{\ln{\ln{(\frac{1}{\epsilon})}}}.
If
\gamma\leq\frac{\ln{\ln{(\frac{1}{\epsilon})}}}{\ln{(\frac{1}{\epsilon})}},
then
\frac{\epsilon^{2}\ln{(\frac{1}{\epsilon})}^{2}}{\ln{\ln{(\frac{1}{\epsilon})}}}\lesssim\beta_{1}(t).
In conclusion, for any case we have that
\frac{\epsilon^{2}\ln{(\frac{1}{\epsilon})}^{2}}{\ln{\ln{(\frac{1}{\epsilon})}}}\lesssim\beta(t),

so we choose

\gamma=\frac{\ln{\ln{(\frac{1}{\epsilon})}}}{\ln{(\frac{1}{\epsilon})}}.
As a consequence,
\alpha(t)
is less than or equivalent to

c_{0}^{2}\epsilon^{2}\frac{\ln{(\frac{1}{\epsilon}})^{2}}{\ln{\ln{(\frac{1}{\epsilon})}}}\exp\Big(\frac{2C\epsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}t}{\ln{(\frac{1}{\epsilon})}}\Big).

(196)

So, the estimates (
193
), (
196
), Remark
3.3
and our choice of
\gamma
imply the inequalities (
191
) and (
192
).
∎

Remark 5.2
.

If
\frac{\epsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}}{\ln{(\frac{1}{\epsilon})}^{m}}\lesssim\left\lVert\overrightarrow{g(0)}\right\rVert
for a constant
m>0,
then, for
\gamma=\frac{1}{8},
we have from Lemma
3.1
that there is
p(t)\in C^{2}(\mathbb{R})
such that for all
t\geq 0

\displaystyle\left\lvert\dot{z}(t)-p(t)\right\rvert\lesssim\epsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}\left\lVert\overrightarrow{g(0)}\right\rVert,

(197)

\displaystyle\left\lvert\dot{p}(t)-16\sqrt{2}e^{-\sqrt{2}z(t)}\right\rvert\lesssim\frac{\left\lVert\overrightarrow{g(0)}\right\rVert^{2}}{z(t)}.

(198)

Then, for the smooth real function
d(t)
satisfying

\ddot{d}(t)=16\sqrt{2}e^{-\sqrt{2}d(t)},\,(d(0),\dot{d}(0))=(z(0),\dot{z}(0)),

and since
e^{-\sqrt{2}z(t)}\lesssim\epsilon,\,\ln{(\frac{1}{\epsilon})}\lesssim z(t),
we can deduce that
Y(t)=(z(t)-d(t))
satisfies the following integral inequality for a constant
K>0

\left\lvert Y(t)\right\rvert\leq K\left(\epsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}\left\lVert\overrightarrow{g(0)}\right\rVert t+\frac{\left\lVert\overrightarrow{g(0)}\right\rVert^{2}}{\ln{(\frac{1}{\epsilon})}}t^{2}+\int_{0}^{t}\int_{0}^{s}\epsilon\left\lvert Y(s_{1})\right\rvert\,ds_{1}\,ds\right),\\
Y(0)=0,\,\dot{Y}(0)=0.

(199)

In conclusion, from the Gronwall Lemma, we obtain that

\left\lvert Y(t)\right\rvert\lesssim Q(tK^{\frac{1}{2}}),
where
Q(t)
is the solution of the following integral equation

Q(t)=\epsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}\left\lVert\overrightarrow{g(0)}\right\rVert t+\frac{\left\lVert\overrightarrow{g(0)}\right\rVert^{2}}{\ln{(\frac{1}{\epsilon})}}t^{2}+\int_{0}^{t}\int_{0}^{s}\epsilon Q(s_{1})\,ds_{1}\,ds.

By standard ordinary differential equation techniques,
we deduce that

\left\lvert z(t)-d(t)\right\rvert\lesssim Q(tK^{\frac{1}{2}})=\left(\frac{\left\lVert\overrightarrow{g(0)}\right\rVert}{2}+\frac{\left\lVert\overrightarrow{g(0)}\right\rVert^{2}}{\epsilon\ln{(\frac{1}{\epsilon})}}\right)e^{\epsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}tK^{\frac{1}{2}}}+\left(\frac{-\left\lVert\overrightarrow{g(0)}\right\rVert}{2}+\frac{\left\lVert\overrightarrow{g(0)}\right\rVert^{2}}{\epsilon\ln{(\frac{1}{\epsilon})}}\right)e^{-\epsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}tK^{\frac{1}{2}}}-2\frac{\left\lVert\overrightarrow{g(0)}\right\rVert^{2}}{\epsilon\ln{(\frac{1}{\epsilon})}},

(200)

and from
\dot{z}(0)=\dot{d}(0)
and the estimates (
197
) and (
198
), we obtain that

\left\lvert\dot{z}(t)-\dot{d}(t)\right\rvert\lesssim\left\lvert p(0)-\dot{z}(0)\right\rvert+\int_{0}^{t}\epsilon\left\lvert z(s)-d(s)\right\rvert\,ds,

(201)

from which with (
200
), we obtain that

\left\lvert\dot{z}(t)-\dot{d}(t)\right\rvert\lesssim e^{\epsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}\left\lvert t\right\rvert K^{\frac{1}{2}}}\epsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}\left(\left\lVert\overrightarrow{g(0)}\right\rVert+\frac{\left\lVert\overrightarrow{g(0)}\right\rVert^{2}}{\epsilon\ln{(\frac{1}{\epsilon})}}\right).

(202)

However, the precision of the estimates (
200
) and (
202
) is very bad when
\epsilon^{-\frac{1}{2}}\ll t,
which motivate us to apply Lemma (
3.1
) to estimate the modulations parameters
x_{1}(t),\,x_{2}(t)
for
t\lesssim\frac{\ln{(\frac{1}{\epsilon})}}{\epsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}}.

We recall from Theorem
1.11
the definitions of the functions
d_{1}(t),\,d_{2}(t).
If
\left\lVert\overrightarrow{g(0)}\right\rVert\geq\frac{\epsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}}{\ln{(\frac{1}{\epsilon})}^{5}},
because Theorem
2.9
and
\max_{j\in\{1,2\}}\left\lvert\dot{d}_{j}(0)-\dot{x}_{j}(0)\right\rvert=0
imply that
\max_{j\in\{1,\,2\}}\left\lvert d_{j}(t)-x_{j}(t)\right\rvert=O(\min(\epsilon t,\epsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}t)),\,\max_{j\in\{1,\,2\}}\left\lvert\dot{d}_{j}(t)-\dot{x}_{j}(t)\right\rvert=O(\epsilon t),
we deduce for a constant
C>0
large enough the estimates (
9
) and (
10
) of Theorem
1.11
. For the case
\left\lVert\overrightarrow{g(0)}\right\rVert\leq\frac{\epsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}}{\ln{(\frac{1}{\epsilon})}^{5}}
, the estimates of
\max_{j\in\{1,2\}}\left\lvert x_{j}(t)-d_{j}(t)\right\rvert,\,\max_{j\in\{1,\,2\}}\left\lvert\dot{x}_{j}(t)-\dot{d}_{j}(t)\right\rvert
will be done by studying separated cases depending on the initial data
z(0),\,\dot{z}(0).

Lemma 5.3
.

\exists K>0
such that
if
\left\lVert\overrightarrow{g(0)}\right\rVert\leq\frac{\epsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}}{\ln{(\frac{1}{\epsilon})}^{5}},\,(g_{0}(x),g_{1}(x))=\left(g(0,x),\partial_{t}g(0,x)\right)
and all the hypotheses of Theorem
1.11
are true and
\frac{\epsilon}{\ln{(\frac{1}{\epsilon})}^{8}}\lesssim e^{-\sqrt{2}z(0)}\lesssim\epsilon,
then we have for
0\leq t
that

\displaystyle\max_{j\in\{1,\,2\}}\left\lvert x_{j}(t)-d_{j}(t)\right\rvert=O\left(\frac{\max\Big(\left\lVert(g_{0},g_{1})\right\rVert,\epsilon\ln{(\frac{1}{\epsilon})}\Big)^{2}\ln{(\frac{1}{\epsilon})}^{6}}{\epsilon\ln{\ln(\frac{1}{\epsilon})}}\exp\Big(\frac{K\epsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}t}{\ln{(\frac{1}{\epsilon})}}\Big)\right),

(203)

\displaystyle\max_{j\in\{1,\,2\}}\left\lvert\dot{x}_{j}(t)-\dot{d}_{j}(t)\right\rvert=O\left(\max\Big(\left\lVert(g_{0},g_{1})\right\rVert,\epsilon\ln{\Big(\frac{1}{\epsilon}\Big)}\Big)^{2}\frac{\ln{(\frac{1}{\epsilon})}^{6}}{\epsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}\ln{\ln{(\frac{1}{\epsilon})}}}\exp\Big(\frac{K\epsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}t}{\ln{(\frac{1}{\epsilon})}}\Big)\right).

(204)

Proof of Lemma
5.3
.

First, in notation of Lemma
5.1
, we define

p(t)\coloneqq p_{2}(t)-p_{1}(t),\,z(t)\coloneqq x_{2}(t)-x_{1}(t),\,\dot{z}(t)\coloneqq\dot{x}_{2}(t)-\dot{x}_{1}(t).

Also, motivated by Remark
3.3
, we consider the smooth function
d(t)

solution of the following ordinary differential equation

\begin{cases}\ddot{d}(t)=16\sqrt{2}e^{-\sqrt{2}d(t)},\\
(d(0),\dot{d}(0))=(z(0),\dot{z}(0)).\end{cases}

Step 1.
(Estimate of
z(t),\,\dot{z}(t)
)
From now on, we denote the functions
W(t)=z(t)-d(t),\,V(t)=p(t)-\dot{d}(t).

Then, Lemma
5.1
implies that
W,\,V
satisfy the following ordinary differential estimates

\displaystyle\left\lvert\dot{W}(t)-V(t)\right\rvert=O\left(\max\Big(\left\lVert(g_{0},g_{1})\right\rVert,\epsilon\ln{\Big(\frac{1}{\epsilon}\Big)}\Big)\epsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}\exp\Big(\frac{2C\epsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}t}{\ln{(\frac{1}{\epsilon})}}\Big)\right),

\displaystyle\left\lvert\dot{V}(t)+16\sqrt{2}e^{-\sqrt{2}d(t)}-16\sqrt{2}e^{-\sqrt{2}z(t)}\right\rvert=O\left(\frac{\max\Big(\left\lVert(g_{0},g_{1})\right\rVert,\epsilon\ln{\Big(\frac{1}{\epsilon}\Big)}\Big)^{2}}{\ln{\ln{(\frac{1}{\epsilon})}}}\exp\Big(\frac{2C\epsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}t}{\ln{(\frac{1}{\epsilon})}}\Big)\right).

From the above estimates and the Taylor’s Expansion Theorem, we deduce the following almost ordinary differential system of equations, while
\left\lvert W(t)\right\rvert<1:

\begin{cases}\dot{W}(t)=V(t)+O\Big(\max\Big(\left\lVert(g_{0},g_{1})\right\rVert,\epsilon\ln{\Big(\frac{1}{\epsilon}\Big)}\Big)\epsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}\exp\Big(\frac{2C\epsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}t}{\ln{(\frac{1}{\epsilon})}}\Big)\Big),\\
\dot{V}(t)=-32e^{-\sqrt{2}d(t)}W(t)+O\left(e^{-\sqrt{2}d(t)}W(t)^{2}\right)+O\left(\frac{\max\Big(\left\lVert(g_{0},g_{1})\right\rVert,\epsilon\ln{(\frac{1}{\epsilon})}\Big)^{2}}{\ln{\ln{(\frac{1}{\epsilon})}}}\exp\Big(\frac{2C\epsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}t}{\ln{(\frac{1}{\epsilon})}}\Big)\right).\end{cases}

Recalling Remark
3.3
,
we have that

d(t)=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\ln{\Big(\frac{8}{v^{2}}\cosh{(\sqrt{2}vt+c)}^{2}\Big)},

(205)

where
v>0
and
c\in\mathbb{R}
are chosen such that
(d(0),\dot{d}(0))=(z(0),\dot{z}(0)).

Moreover, it is not difficult to verify that

\displaystyle v=\Big(\frac{\dot{z}(0)^{2}}{4}+8e^{-\sqrt{2}z(0)}\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}},\,c=\arctanh{\left(\frac{\dot{z}(0)}{\big[32e^{-\sqrt{2}z(0)}+\dot{z}(0)^{2}\big]^{\frac{1}{2}}}\right)}.

Moreover, since

8e^{-\sqrt{2}z(0)}=v^{2}\sech{(c)}^{2}\leq 4v^{2}e^{-2\left\lvert c\right\rvert},

we obtain
from the hypothesis for
e^{-\sqrt{2}z(0)}
that

\frac{\epsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}}{\ln{(\frac{1}{\epsilon})}^{4}}\lesssim v\lesssim\epsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}
and as a consequence the estimate
|c|\lesssim\ln{(\ln{(\frac{1}{\epsilon})})}.

Also, it is not difficult to verify that the functions

n(t)=(\sqrt{2}vt+c)\tanh{(\sqrt{2}vt+c)}-1,\,m(t)=\tanh{(\sqrt{2}vt+c)}

generate all solutions of the following ordinary differential equation

\ddot{y}(t)=-32e^{-\sqrt{2}d(t)}y(t),

(206)

which is obtained from the linear part of the system (
5
).

To simplify our computations we use the following notation

\displaystyle error_{1}(t)=\max\Big(\left\lVert(g_{0},g_{1})\right\rVert,\epsilon\ln{\Big(\frac{1}{\epsilon}\Big)}\Big)\epsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}\exp\Big(\frac{2C\epsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}t}{\ln{(\frac{1}{\epsilon})}}\Big),

\displaystyle error_{2}(t)=e^{-\sqrt{2}d(t)}(z(t)-d(t))^{2}+\frac{\max\Big(\left\lVert(g_{0},g_{1})\right\rVert,\epsilon\ln{\Big(\frac{1}{\epsilon}\Big)}\Big)^{2}}{\ln{\ln{(\frac{1}{\epsilon})}}}\exp\Big(\frac{2C\epsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}t}{\ln{(\frac{1}{\epsilon})}}\Big).

From the variation of parameters technique for ordinary differential equation, we can write that

\begin{bmatrix}W(t)\\
V(t)\end{bmatrix}=c_{1}(t)\begin{bmatrix}m(t)\\
\dot{m}(t)\end{bmatrix}+c_{2}(t)\begin{bmatrix}n(t)\\
\dot{n}(t)\end{bmatrix},

(207)

such that

\begin{cases}\begin{bmatrix}m(t)&n(t)\\
\dot{m}(t)&\dot{n}(t)\end{bmatrix}\begin{bmatrix}\dot{c}_{1}(t)\\
\dot{c}_{2}(t)\end{bmatrix}=\begin{bmatrix}O(error_{1}(t))\\
O(error_{2}(t))\end{bmatrix},\\
\\
\begin{bmatrix}m(0)&n(0)\\
\dot{m}(0)&\dot{n}(0)\end{bmatrix}\par\begin{bmatrix}c_{1}(0)\\
c_{2}(0)\end{bmatrix}=\begin{bmatrix}0\\
O\Big(\Big[\left\lVert(g_{0},g_{1})\right\rVert+\epsilon\ln{(\frac{1}{\epsilon})}\Big]\epsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}\Big)\end{bmatrix}.\end{cases}

The presence of an error in the condition of the initial data
c_{1}(0),\,c_{2}(0)
comes from estimate (
191
) of Lemma
5.1
.
Since for all
t\in\mathbb{R}

m(t)\dot{n}(t)-\dot{m}(t)n(t)=\sqrt{2}v
, we can verify by Cramer’s rule and from the fact that
\frac{\epsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}}{\ln{(\frac{1}{\epsilon})}^{4}}\lesssim v
that

\displaystyle c_{1}(0)=O\left(\max\Big(\left\lVert\overrightarrow{g(0)}\right\rVert,\epsilon\ln{\Big(\frac{1}{\epsilon}\Big)}\Big)\left\lvert c\tanh{(c)}-1\right\rvert\ln{\Big(\frac{1}{\epsilon}\Big)}^{4}\right),

(208)

\displaystyle c_{2}(0)=O\left(\max\Big(\left\lVert\overrightarrow{g(0)}\right\rVert,\epsilon\ln{\Big(\frac{1}{\epsilon}\Big)}\Big)\left\lvert\tanh{(c)}\right\rvert\ln{\Big(\frac{1}{\epsilon}\Big)}^{4}\right),

(209)

and

\left\lvert\dot{c}_{1}(t)\right\rvert=O\left(\left\lvert m(t)+(\sqrt{2}vt+c)\sech{(\sqrt{2}vt+c)}^{2}\right\rvert\max\left(\left\lVert(g_{0},g_{1})\right\rVert,\epsilon\ln{\Big(\frac{1}{\epsilon}\Big)}\right)\epsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}\exp\Big(\frac{2C\epsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}t}{\ln{(\frac{1}{\epsilon})}}\Big)\right)\\
+O\left(\left[v\sech{(\sqrt{2}vt+c)}^{2}\left\lvert W(t)\right\rvert^{2}+\frac{\max\Big(\left\lVert(g_{0},g_{1})\right\rVert,\epsilon\ln{(\frac{1}{\epsilon})}\Big)^{2}}{v\ln{\ln{(\frac{1}{\epsilon})}}}\exp\Big(\frac{2C\epsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}t}{\ln{(\frac{1}{\epsilon})}}\Big)\right]\left\lvert n(t)\right\rvert\right),

(210)

\left\lvert\dot{c}_{2}(t)\right\rvert=O\left(\left[v\sech{(\sqrt{2}vt+c)}^{2}\left\lvert W(t)\right\rvert^{2}+\frac{\max\Big(\left\lVert(g_{0},g_{1})\right\rVert,\epsilon\ln{\Big(\frac{1}{\epsilon}\Big)}\Big)^{2}}{v\ln{\ln{(\frac{1}{\epsilon})}}}\exp\Big(\frac{2C\epsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}t}{\ln{(\frac{1}{\epsilon})}}\Big)\right]\left\lvert m(t)\right\rvert\right)\\
+O\left(\max\left(\left\lVert(g_{0},g_{1})\right\rVert,\epsilon\ln{\Big(\frac{1}{\epsilon}\Big)}\right)\exp\Big(\frac{2C\epsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}t}{\ln{(\frac{1}{\epsilon})}}\Big)\epsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}\sech(\sqrt{2}vt+c)^{2}\right).

(211)

Since we have for all
x\geq 0
that

\frac{d}{dx}\left(-\frac{\sech{(x)}^{2}x}{2}+\frac{3\tanh{(x)}}{2}\right)=\frac{\sech{(x)}^{2}}{2}+x\tanh{(x)}\sech{(x)}^{2}\geq\frac{\left\lvert x\tanh{(x)}-1\right\rvert\sech{(x)}^{2}}{2},

we deduce from the Fundamental Theorem of Calculus, the identity
n(t)=(\sqrt{2}vt+c)\tanh(\sqrt{2}vt+c)-1,
the inequality
\frac{\epsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}}{\ln{(\frac{1}{\epsilon})}^{4}}\lesssim v
and the estimates (
210
), (
211
) that

|c_{1}(t)-c_{1}(0)|=O\left(\max\left(\left\lVert(g_{0},g_{1})\right\rVert,\epsilon\ln{\Big(\frac{1}{\epsilon}\Big)}\right)\ln{\Big(\frac{1}{\epsilon}\Big)}\left[\exp\Big(\frac{2Ct\epsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}}{\ln{(\frac{1}{\epsilon})}}\Big)-1\right]\right)\\
+O\left(\left[\exp\Big(\frac{2C\epsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}t}{\ln{(\frac{1}{\epsilon})}}\Big)-1\right]\left\lVert n(s)\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}_{s}[0,t]}\max\Big(\left\lVert(g_{0},g_{1})\right\rVert,\epsilon\ln{\Big(\frac{1}{\epsilon}\Big)}\Big)^{2}\frac{\ln{(\frac{1}{\epsilon})}^{5}}{\epsilon\ln{\ln(\frac{1}{\epsilon})}}\right)\\
+O\left(\left\lvert-\frac{\sech{(x)}^{2}x}{2}+\frac{3\tanh{(x)}}{2}\Big|_{c}^{\sqrt{2}vt+c}\right\rvert\left\lVert W(s)\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}_{s}[0,t]}^{2}\right).

(212)

From a similar argument, we deduce that

\left\lvert c_{2}(t)-c_{2}(0)\right\rvert=O\left(\left\lVert W(s)\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}_{s}[0,t]}^{2}\Big[\tanh{(\sqrt{2}vt+c)}-\tanh{(c)}\Big]\right)\\
+O\left(\max\Big(\left\lVert(g_{0},g_{1})\right\rVert,\epsilon\ln{\Big(\frac{1}{\epsilon}\Big)}\Big)^{2}\left[\exp\left(\frac{2C\epsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}t}{\ln{(\frac{1}{\epsilon})}}\right)-1\right]\frac{\ln{(\frac{1}{\epsilon})}^{5}}{\epsilon\ln{\ln{(\frac{1}{\epsilon})}}}+\max\Big(\left\lVert(g_{0},g_{1})\right\rVert,\epsilon\ln{\Big(\frac{1}{\epsilon}\Big)}\Big)\ln{\Big(\frac{1}{\epsilon}\Big)}\exp\left(2Ct\frac{\epsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}}{\ln{(\frac{1}{\epsilon})}}\right)\right).

(213)

From the estimates
v\lesssim\epsilon^{\frac{1}{2}},\,|c|\lesssim\ln{\ln{(\frac{1}{\epsilon})}},
we obtain for
\epsilon\ll 1

while

\left\lVert W(s)\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}_{s}[0,t]}\Big[\epsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}t+\ln{\ln{\Big(\frac{1}{\epsilon}\Big)}}\Big]\ln{\ln{\Big(\frac{1}{\epsilon}\Big)}}\leq 1,

(214)

that

\left\lVert W(s)\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}_{s}[0,t]}^{2}(1+\left\lvert n(t)\right\rvert)\lesssim\left\lVert W(s)\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}_{s}[0,t]}\frac{1}{\ln{\ln{(\frac{1}{\epsilon})}}}.

(215)

Also, from

\left\lvert n(t)\right\rvert\leq(\sqrt{2}v|t|+|c|),

we deduce for
t\geq 0
that

\left\lvert n(t)\right\rvert\lesssim\epsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}t+\ln{\ln{\left(\frac{1}{\epsilon}\right)}}\lesssim\ln{\left(\frac{1}{\epsilon}\right)}\exp\left(\frac{\epsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}t}{\ln{(\frac{1}{\epsilon})}}\right)

(216)

In conclusion, the estimates (
212
), (
213
), (
215
), (
216
) and the definition of
W(t)=z(t)-d(t)
implies that while the condition (
214
) is true, then

\left\lvert W(t)\right\rvert\lesssim f(t)=\frac{\max\Big(\left\lVert(g_{0},g_{1})\right\rVert,\epsilon\ln{(\frac{1}{\epsilon})}\Big)^{2}\ln{(\frac{1}{\epsilon})}^{6}}{\epsilon\ln{\ln(\frac{1}{\epsilon})}}\exp\left(\frac{(2C+1)\epsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}t}{\ln{(\frac{1}{\epsilon})}}\right)

(217)

Then, from the expression for
V(t)
in the equation (
207
) and the estimates (
212
), (
213
), (
216
), we obtain that if inequality (
217
) is true, then

\left\lvert V(t)\right\rvert\lesssim\max\Big(\left\lVert(g_{0},g_{1})\right\rVert,\epsilon\ln{\Big(\frac{1}{\epsilon}\Big)}\Big)^{2}\frac{\ln{(\frac{1}{\epsilon})}^{6}}{\epsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}\ln{\ln{(\frac{1}{\epsilon})}}}\exp\left(\frac{(4C+3)\epsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}t}{\ln{(\frac{1}{\epsilon})}}\right)\\
+\max\Big(\left\lVert(g_{0},g_{1})\right\rVert,\epsilon\ln{\Big(\frac{1}{\epsilon}\Big)}\Big)^{4}\frac{\ln{(\frac{1}{\epsilon})}^{12}}{\epsilon^{\frac{3}{2}}\big[\ln{\ln{(\frac{1}{\epsilon})}}\big]^{2}}\exp\Big(\frac{(4C+3)\epsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}t}{\ln{(\frac{1}{\epsilon})}}\Big),

(218)

which implies the following estimate

\left\lvert\dot{W}(t)\right\rvert\lesssim\max\Big(\left\lVert\overrightarrow{g(0)}\right\rVert,\epsilon\ln{\Big(\frac{1}{\epsilon}\Big)}\Big)^{2}\frac{\ln{(\frac{1}{\epsilon})}^{6}}{\epsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}\ln{\ln{(\frac{1}{\epsilon})}}}\exp\Big(\frac{(4C+3)\epsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}t}{\ln{(\frac{1}{\epsilon})}}\Big).

(219)

Indeed, from the bound
\left\lVert\overrightarrow{g(0)}\right\rVert\lesssim\frac{\epsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}}{\ln{(\frac{1}{\epsilon})}^{4}},
we deduce that (
214
) is true for
0\leq t\leq\frac{\ln{\ln{(\frac{1}{\epsilon})}}\ln{(\frac{1}{\epsilon})}}{(4C+2)\epsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}}.
As a consequence, the estimates (
217
) and (
219
) are true for
0\leq t\leq\frac{\ln{\ln{(\frac{1}{\epsilon})}}\ln{(\frac{1}{\epsilon})}}{(4C+2)\epsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}}.
But, for
t\geq 0,
we have that

\left\lvert W(t)\right\rvert\lesssim\epsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}t\lesssim 3\ln{\left(\frac{1}{\epsilon}\right)}\exp\left(\frac{\epsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}t}{3\ln{(\frac{1}{\epsilon})}}\right),\,\left\lvert\dot{W}(t)\right\rvert\lesssim\epsilon t\lesssim 3\epsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}\ln{\left(\frac{1}{\epsilon}\right)}\exp\left(\frac{\epsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}t}{3\ln{(\frac{1}{\epsilon})}}\right).

(220)

Since
f(t)
defined in inequality (
217
) is strictly increasing and
f(0)\lesssim\frac{1}{\ln{(\frac{1}{\epsilon})}^{2}\ln{\ln{(\frac{1}{\epsilon})}}}
, there is an instant
T_{M}>0
such that

\exp\left(\frac{\epsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}T_{M}}{\ln{(\frac{1}{\epsilon})}}\right)f(T_{M})=\frac{1}{\ln{(\frac{1}{\epsilon})}\ln{\ln{(\frac{1}{\epsilon})}}^{2}},

(221)

from which with estimate (
217
) and condition (
214
) we deduce that (
217
) is true for
0\leq t\leq T_{M}.
Also, from the identity (
221
) and the fact that
\left\lVert\overrightarrow{g(0)}\right\rVert\lesssim\frac{\epsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}}{\ln{(\frac{1}{\epsilon})}^{4}}
we deduce

\frac{1}{\ln{(\frac{1}{\epsilon})}\ln{\ln{(\frac{1}{\epsilon})}}^{2}}\lesssim\frac{1}{\ln{(\frac{1}{\epsilon})}^{2}\ln{\ln{(\frac{1}{\epsilon})}}}\exp\left(\frac{(2C+2)\epsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}T_{M}}{\ln{(\frac{1}{\epsilon})}}\right),

from which we obtain that
T_{M}\geq\frac{3}{8(C+1)}\frac{\ln{\ln{(\frac{1}{\epsilon})}}\ln{(\frac{1}{\epsilon})}}{\epsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}}
for
\epsilon\ll 1.

In conclusion, since
f(t)
is an increasing function, we have for
t\geq T_{M}
and
\epsilon\ll 1
that

f(t)\exp\left(\frac{[17(C+1)+4]\epsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}t}{3\ln{(\frac{1}{\epsilon})}}\right)\geq\frac{1}{\ln{(\frac{1}{\epsilon})}\ln{\ln{(\frac{1}{\epsilon})}}^{2}}\exp\left(\frac{[17(C+1)+1]\epsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}t}{3\ln{(\frac{1}{\epsilon})}}\right)\geq\frac{\ln{(\frac{1}{\epsilon})}^{1+\frac{1}{8}}}{\ln{\ln{(\frac{1}{\epsilon})}}^{2}}\exp\left(\frac{\epsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}t}{3\ln{(\frac{1}{\epsilon}})}\right),

from which with the estimates (
220
) and (
217
) we deduce for all
t\geq 0
that

\left\lvert W(t)\right\rvert\lesssim\frac{\max\Big(\left\lVert(g_{0},g_{1})\right\rVert,\epsilon\ln{(\frac{1}{\epsilon})}\Big)^{2}\ln{(\frac{1}{\epsilon})}^{6}}{\epsilon\ln{\ln(\frac{1}{\epsilon})}}\exp\left(\frac{(8C+9)\epsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}t}{\ln{(\frac{1}{\epsilon})}}\right).

(222)

As consequence, we obtain from the estimates (
208
), (
209
), (
212
), (
213
) and (
222
) that

\left\lvert\dot{W}(t)\right\rvert\lesssim\frac{\max\Big(\left\lVert(g_{0},g_{1})\right\rVert,\epsilon\ln{(\frac{1}{\epsilon})}\Big)^{2}\ln{(\frac{1}{\epsilon})}^{6}}{\epsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}\ln{\ln(\frac{1}{\epsilon})}}\exp\left(\frac{(16C+18)\epsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}t}{\ln{(\frac{1}{\epsilon})}}\right)

(223)

for all
t\geq 0.

Step 2.
(Estimate of
\left\lvert x_{1}(t)+x_{2}(t)\right\rvert,\,\left\lvert\dot{x}_{1}(t)+\dot{x}_{2}(t)\right\rvert.
)
First, we define

M(t)\coloneqq(x_{1}(t)+x_{2}(t))-(d_{1}(t)+d_{2}(t)),\,N(t)\coloneqq(p_{1}(t)+p_{2}(t))-(\dot{d}_{1}(t)+\dot{d}_{2}(t)).

(224)

From the inequalities (
191
), (
192
) of Lemma
5.1
, we obtain, respectively:

\left\lvert\dot{M}(t)-N(t)\right\rvert\lesssim\max\Big(\left\lVert(g_{0},g_{1})\right\rVert,\epsilon\ln{\left(\frac{1}{\epsilon}\right)}\Big)\epsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}\exp\Big(\frac{C\epsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}t}{\ln{\left(\frac{1}{\epsilon}\right)}}\Big),\,\left\lvert\dot{N}(t)\right\rvert\lesssim\frac{\max\Big(\left\lVert(g_{0},g_{1})\right\rVert,\epsilon\ln{(\frac{1}{\epsilon})}\Big)^{2}}{\ln{\ln(\frac{1}{\epsilon})}}\exp\Big(\frac{2C\epsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}t}{\ln{(\frac{1}{\epsilon})}}\Big).

Also, from inequality (
191
) and the fact that for
j\in\{1,2\}\,d_{j}(0)=x_{j}(0),\,\dot{d}_{j}(0)=\dot{x}_{j}(0),
we deduce that
M(0)=0
and
\left\lvert N(0)\right\rvert\lesssim\max\left(\left\lVert\overrightarrow{g(0)}\right\rVert,\epsilon\ln{(\frac{1}{\epsilon})}\right)\epsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}.
Then,
from the Fundamental Theorem of Calculus, we obtain that

\displaystyle N(t)=O\left(\frac{\max\Big(\left\lVert(g_{0},g_{1})\right\rVert,\epsilon\ln{(\frac{1}{\epsilon})}\Big)^{2}\ln{(\frac{1}{\epsilon})}}{\epsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}\ln{\ln{(\frac{1}{\epsilon})}}}\exp\Big(\frac{4C\epsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}t}{\ln{(\frac{1}{\epsilon})}}\Big)\right),

(225)

\displaystyle M(t)=O\left(\frac{\max\Big(\left\lVert(g_{0},g_{1})\right\rVert,\epsilon\ln{(\frac{1}{\epsilon})}\Big)^{2}\ln{(\frac{1}{\epsilon})}^{2}}{\epsilon\ln{\ln{(\frac{1}{\epsilon})}}}\exp\Big(\frac{4C\epsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}t}{\ln{(\frac{1}{\epsilon})}}\Big)\right).

(226)

In conclusion, for
K=16C+18,
we verify from triangle inequality that the estimates (
222
) and (
226
) imply (
203
) and the estimates (
223
) and (
225
) imply (
204
).
∎

Remark 5.4
.

The estimates (
226
) and (
225
) are true for any initial data
(g_{0},g_{1})\in H^{1}(\mathbb{R})\times L^{2}(\mathbb{R})
such that the hypothesis of Theorem
1.11
are true.

Remark 5.5

(Similar Case)
.

If we add the following conditions

e^{-\sqrt{2}z(0)}\ll\frac{\epsilon}{\ln{(\frac{1}{\epsilon})}^{8}},\,\frac{\epsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}}{\ln{(\frac{1}{\epsilon})}^{4}}\lesssim v\lesssim\epsilon^{\frac{1}{2}},\,-\ln{\Big(\frac{1}{\epsilon}\Big)}^{2}<c<0,

to the hypotheses of Theorem
1.11
, then, by repeating the above proof of Lemma
5.3
, we would still obtain (
210
), (
211
), (
212
) and (
213
).

However, since now
\left\lvert c\right\rvert\leq\ln{(\frac{1}{\epsilon})}^{2},
if
\epsilon\ll 1
enough, we can verify while

\left\lVert W(s)\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}_{s}[0,t]}\left(\epsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}t+\ln{\left(\frac{1}{\epsilon}\right)}^{2}\right)\ln{\ln{\left(\frac{1}{\epsilon}\right)}}\leq 1,

(227)

that

\left\lVert W(s)\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}_{s}[0,t]}^{2}(1+\left\lvert n(t)\right\rvert)\lesssim\left\lVert W(s)\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}_{s}[0,t]}\frac{1}{\ln{\ln{(\frac{1}{\epsilon})}}},

which implies by a similar reasoning to the proof of Lemma
5.3
for a uniform constant
C>1
the following estimates

\displaystyle\left\lvert W(t)\right\rvert\lesssim\frac{\max\Big(\left\lVert(g_{0},g_{1})\right\rVert,\epsilon\ln{(\frac{1}{\epsilon})}\Big)^{2}\ln{(\frac{1}{\epsilon})}^{7}}{\epsilon\ln{\ln(\frac{1}{\epsilon})}}\exp\Big(\frac{C\epsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}t}{\ln{(\frac{1}{\epsilon})}}\Big)=f_{1}(t,C),

(228)

\displaystyle\left\lvert\dot{W}(t)\right\rvert\lesssim\max\Big(\left\lVert(g_{0},g_{1})\right\rVert,\epsilon\ln{\Big(\frac{1}{\epsilon}\Big)}\Big)^{2}\frac{\ln{(\frac{1}{\epsilon})}^{7}}{\epsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}\ln{\ln{(\frac{1}{\epsilon})}}}\exp\Big(\frac{C\epsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}t}{\ln{(\frac{1}{\epsilon})}}\Big)=f_{2}(t,C).

(229)

From the estimates (
228
), (
229
) and
\left\lVert\overrightarrow{g(0)}\right\rVert\leq\frac{\epsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}}{\ln{(\frac{1}{\epsilon})}^{5}}
, we deduce that the condition (
227
) holds while
0\leq t\leq\frac{\ln{\ln{(\frac{1}{\epsilon})}}\ln{(\frac{1}{\epsilon})}}{4(C+1)\epsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}}.

Indeed, since
\left\lVert\overrightarrow{g(0)}\right\rVert^{2}\leq\frac{\epsilon}{\ln{(\frac{1}{\epsilon})}^{10}},
we can verify that there is an instant
\frac{\ln{\ln{(\frac{1}{\epsilon})}}\ln{(\frac{1}{\epsilon})}}{4(C+1)\epsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}}\leq T_{M}
such that (
227
) and (
228
) are true for
0\leq t\leq T_{M}
and

f_{1}(T_{M},C)\exp\left(\frac{\epsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}T_{M}}{\ln{(\frac{1}{\epsilon})}}\right)=\frac{1}{\ln{(\frac{1}{\epsilon})}^{2+\frac{1}{2}}\ln{\ln{(\frac{1}{\epsilon})}}}.

In conclusion, we can repeat the argument in the proof of step
1
of Lemma
5.3
and deduce that there is
1<K\lesssim C+1
such that for all
t\geq 0

|W(t)|\lesssim f_{1}(t,K),\,|\dot{W}(t)|\lesssim f_{2}(t,K).

(230)

Lemma 5.6
.

In notation of Theorem
1.11
,
\exists K>1,\,\delta>0
such that if
0<\epsilon<\delta,\,0<v\leq\frac{\epsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}}{\ln{(\frac{1}{\epsilon})}^{4}},\,(g_{0}(x),g_{1}(x))=\left(g(0,x),\partial_{t}g(0,x)\right)
and
\left\lVert\overrightarrow{g(0)}\right\rVert\leq\frac{\epsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}}{\ln{(\frac{1}{\epsilon})}^{5}},
then we have for
0\leq t
that

\displaystyle\max_{j\in\{1,\,2\}}\left\lvert d_{j}(t)-x_{j}(t)\right\rvert=O\left(\frac{\max\Big(\left\lVert(g_{0},g_{1})\right\rVert,\epsilon\ln{(\frac{1}{\epsilon})}\Big)^{2}}{\epsilon\ln{\ln{(\frac{1}{\epsilon})}}}\ln{\Big({\frac{1}{\epsilon}}\Big)}^{2}\exp\Big(\frac{Kt\epsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}}{\ln{\frac{1}{\epsilon}}}\Big)\right),

(231)
