\displaystyle\max_{j\in\{1,\,2\}}\left\lvert\dot{d}_{j}(t)-\dot{x}_{j}(t)\right\rvert=O\left(\frac{\max\Big(\left\lVert(g_{0},g_{1})\right\rVert,\epsilon\ln{(\frac{1}{\epsilon})}\Big)^{2}}{\epsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}\ln{\ln{(\frac{1}{\epsilon})}}}\ln{\Big({\frac{1}{\epsilon}}\Big)}\exp\Big(\frac{Kt\epsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}}{\ln{\frac{1}{\epsilon}}}\Big)\right).

(232)

Proof of Lemma
5.6
.

First, we recall that

d(t)=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\ln{\left(\frac{8}{v^{2}}\cosh{\left(\sqrt{2}vt+c\right)}\right)},

which implies that

e^{-\sqrt{2}d(t)}=\frac{v^{2}}{8}\sech{\left(\sqrt{2}vt+c\right)}^{2}.

(233)

We recall the notation

W(t)=z(t)-d(t),\,V(t)=p(t)-\dot{d}(t).
From the first inequality of Lemma
5.1
, we have that

\left\lvert V(0)\right\rvert\lesssim\max\left(\left\lVert(g_{0},g_{1})\right\rVert,\epsilon\ln{\left(\frac{1}{\epsilon}\right)}\right)\epsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}.

(234)

We already verified that
W,\,V
satisfy the following ordinary differential system

\begin{cases}\dot{W}(t)=V(t)+O\Big(\max\Big(\left\lVert(g_{0},g_{1})\right\rVert,\epsilon\ln{\Big(\frac{1}{\epsilon}\Big)}\Big)\epsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}\exp\Big(\frac{C\epsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}t}{\ln{(\frac{1}{\epsilon})}}\Big)\Big),\\
\\
\dot{V}(t)=-32e^{-\sqrt{2}d(t)}W(t)+O(e^{-\sqrt{2}z(t)}(W(t))^{2})+O\Big(\frac{\max(\left\lVert(g_{0},g_{1})\right\rVert,\epsilon\ln{(\frac{1}{\epsilon})})^{2}}{\ln{\ln{(\frac{1}{\epsilon})}}}\exp\Big(\frac{2C\epsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}t}{\ln{(\frac{1}{\epsilon})}}\Big)\Big).\end{cases}

However, since
v^{2}\leq\frac{\epsilon}{\ln{(\frac{1}{\epsilon})}^{8}},
we deduce from (
233
) that
e^{-\sqrt{2}d(t)}\lesssim\frac{\epsilon}{\ln{(\frac{1}{\epsilon})}^{8}}
for all
t\geq 0.

So, while
\left\lVert W(s)\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}[0,t]}<1,
we have from the differential ordinary system (
5
) for
t\geq 0
and some constant
C>0
independent of
\epsilon
that

\left\lvert\dot{V}(t)\right\rvert\lesssim\frac{\epsilon}{\ln{(\frac{1}{\epsilon})}^{8}}\left\lVert W(s)\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}[0,t]}+\frac{\max\Big(\left\lVert(g_{0},g_{1})\right\rVert,\epsilon\ln{(\frac{1}{\epsilon})}\Big)^{2}}{\ln{\ln{(\frac{1}{\epsilon})}}}\exp\Big(\frac{2C\epsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}t}{\ln{(\frac{1}{\epsilon})}}\Big),

from which we deduce the following estimate

\left\lvert V(t)-V(0)\right\rvert=O\left(\frac{\epsilon t}{\ln{(\frac{1}{\epsilon})}^{8}}\left\lVert W(s)\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}[0,t]}\right)+O\left(\frac{\max\Big(\left\lVert(g_{0},g_{1})\right\rVert,\epsilon\ln{(\frac{1}{\epsilon})}\Big)^{2}\ln{(\frac{1}{\epsilon})}}{\epsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}\ln{\ln{(\frac{1}{\epsilon})}}}\exp\Big(\frac{2C\epsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}t}{\ln{(\frac{1}{\epsilon})}}\Big)\right).

In conclusion, while
\left\lVert W(s)\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}[0,t]}<1,
we have that

\left\lvert\dot{W}(t)\right\rvert\leq\left\lvert V(0)\right\rvert+O\left(\frac{\max\Big(\left\lVert(g_{0},g_{1})\right\rVert,\epsilon\ln{(\frac{1}{\epsilon})}\Big)^{2}\ln{(\frac{1}{\epsilon})}}{\epsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}\ln{\ln{(\frac{1}{\epsilon})}}}\exp\Big(\frac{2C\epsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}t}{\ln{(\frac{1}{\epsilon})}}\Big)\right)+O\left(\frac{\epsilon t}{\ln{(\frac{1}{\epsilon})}^{8}}\left\lVert W(s)\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}[0,t]}\right).

(235)

Finally, since
W(0)=0,
the fundamental theorem of calculus and (
235
) imply the following estimate

\left\lVert W(s)\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}[0,t]}\leq\left\lvert V(0)\right\rvert t+O\left(\frac{\epsilon t^{2}}{\ln{(\frac{1}{\epsilon})}^{8}}\left\lVert W(s)\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}[0,t]}+\frac{\max\Big(\left\lVert(g_{0},g_{1})\right\rVert,\epsilon\ln{(\frac{1}{\epsilon})}\Big)^{2}\ln{(\frac{1}{\epsilon})}^{2}}{\epsilon\ln{\ln{(\frac{1}{\epsilon})}}}\exp\Big(\frac{2C\epsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}t}{\ln{(\frac{1}{\epsilon})}}\Big)\right).

(236)

Then, the estimates (
234
) and (
236
) imply if
\epsilon\ll 1
that

\left\lvert W(t)\right\rvert\lesssim\frac{\max\Big(\left\lVert(g_{0},g_{1})\right\rVert,\epsilon\ln{(\frac{1}{\epsilon})}\Big)^{2}\ln{(\frac{1}{\epsilon})}^{2}}{\epsilon\ln{\ln{(\frac{1}{\epsilon})}}}\exp\Big(\frac{(2C+1)\epsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}t}{\ln{(\frac{1}{\epsilon})}}\Big),

(237)

for
0\leq t\leq\frac{\ln{(\frac{1}{\epsilon})}\ln{\ln(\frac{1}{\epsilon})}}{(8C+4)\epsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}}.

From (
237
) and (
235
), we deduce for
0\leq t\leq\frac{\ln{(\frac{1}{\epsilon})}\ln{\ln{(\frac{1}{\epsilon})}}}{(8C+4)\epsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}}
that

\left\lvert\dot{W}(t)\right\rvert\lesssim\frac{\max\Big(\left\lVert(g_{0},g_{1})\right\rVert,\epsilon\ln{(\frac{1}{\epsilon})}\Big)^{2}\ln{(\frac{1}{\epsilon})}^{2}}{\epsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}\ln{\ln{(\frac{1}{\epsilon})}}}\exp\Big(\frac{(2C+1)\epsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}t}{\ln{(\frac{1}{\epsilon})}}\Big).

(238)

Since
\left\lvert W(t)\right\rvert\lesssim\epsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}t,\,\left\lvert\dot{W}(t)\right\rvert\lesssim\epsilon t
for all
t\geq 0,
we can verify by a similar argument to the proof of Step
1
of Lemma
5.3
that for all
t\geq 0
there is a constant
1<K\lesssim(C+1)
such that

\displaystyle\left\lvert W(t)\right\rvert\lesssim\frac{\max\Big(\left\lVert(g_{0},g_{1})\right\rVert,\epsilon\ln{(\frac{1}{\epsilon})}\Big)^{2}\ln{(\frac{1}{\epsilon})}^{2}}{\epsilon\ln{\ln{(\frac{1}{\epsilon})}}}\exp\Big(\frac{K\epsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}t}{\ln{(\frac{1}{\epsilon})}}\Big),

(239)

\displaystyle\left\lvert\dot{W}(t)\right\rvert\lesssim\frac{\max\Big(\left\lVert(g_{0},g_{1})\right\rVert,\epsilon\ln{(\frac{1}{\epsilon})}\Big)^{2}\ln{(\frac{1}{\epsilon})}^{2}}{\epsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}\ln{\ln{(\frac{1}{\epsilon})}}}\exp\Big(\frac{K\epsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}t}{\ln{(\frac{1}{\epsilon})}}\Big).

(240)

In conclusion, estimates (
231
) and (
232
) follow from Remark
5.4
, inequalities (
239
), (
240
) and triangle inequality.
∎

Remark 5.7
.

We recall the definition (
205
) of
d(t).
It is not difficult to verify that if
\left\lVert\overrightarrow{g(0)}\right\rVert\leq\frac{\epsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}}{\ln(\frac{1}{\epsilon})^{5}},\,\frac{\epsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}}{\ln(\frac{1}{\epsilon})^{4}}\lesssim v

and one of the following statements

1.

e^{-\sqrt{2}z(0)}\ll\frac{\epsilon}{\ln{(\frac{1}{\epsilon})}^{8}}
and
c>0,

2.

e^{-\sqrt{2}z(0)}\ll\frac{\epsilon}{\ln{(\frac{1}{\epsilon})}^{8}}
and
c\leq-\ln{(\frac{1}{\epsilon})}^{2}

were true, then we would have that
e^{-\sqrt{2}d(t)}\ll\frac{\epsilon}{\ln{(\frac{1}{\epsilon})}^{8}}
for
0\leq t\lesssim\frac{\ln{(\frac{1}{\epsilon})}^{2}}{\epsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}}.

Moreover, assuming
e^{-\sqrt{2}z(0)}\ln{(\frac{1}{\epsilon})}^{8}\ll\epsilon,
if
c>0,
then we have for all
t\geq 0
that

e^{-\sqrt{2}d(t)}=\frac{v^{2}}{8}sech{(\sqrt{2}vt+c)}^{2}\leq\frac{v^{2}}{8}sech{(c)}^{2}=e^{-\sqrt{2}z(0)}\ll\frac{\epsilon}{\ln{(\frac{1}{\epsilon})}^{8}},

otherwise if
c\leq-\ln{(\frac{1}{\epsilon})}^{2},
since
v\lesssim\epsilon^{\frac{1}{2}},
then there is
1\lesssim K
such that for
0\leq t\leq\frac{K\ln{(\frac{1}{\epsilon})}^{2}}{\epsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}},
then
2\left\lvert\sqrt{2}vt+c\right\rvert>\left\lvert c\right\rvert,
and so

e^{-\sqrt{2}d(t)}\leq v^{2}\sech{\left(-\frac{c}{2}\right)}^{2}\ll\frac{\epsilon}{\ln{(\frac{1}{\epsilon})}^{8}}.

In conclusion, the result of Lemma
5.6
would be true for these two cases.

From the following inequality

\max\Big(\left\lVert\overrightarrow{g(0)}\right\rVert,\epsilon\ln{\Big(\frac{1}{\epsilon}\Big)}\Big)\leq\ln{\Big(\frac{1}{\epsilon}\Big)}\max\Big(\left\lVert\overrightarrow{g(0)}\right\rVert,\epsilon\Big),

we deduce from Lemmas
5.3
,
5.6
and Remarks
5.4
,
5.5
and
5.7
the statement of Theorem
1.11
.

6
Proof of Theorem
1.7

If
\left\lVert\overrightarrow{g(0)}\right\rVert\geq\epsilon\ln{(\frac{1}{\epsilon})}
the result of Theorem
1.7
is a direct consequence of Theorem
4.5
. So, from now on, we assume that
\left\lVert\overrightarrow{g(0)}\right\rVert<\epsilon\ln{(\frac{1}{\epsilon})}.

We recall from Theorem
1.11
the notations
v,\,c,\,d_{1}(t),\,d_{2}(t)
and we denote
d(t)=d_{2}(t)-d_{1}(t)
that satisfies

d(t)=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\ln{\Big(\frac{8}{v^{2}}\cosh{(\sqrt{2}vt+c)}^{2}\Big)},\,e^{-\sqrt{2}d(t)}=\frac{v^{2}}{8}\sech{(\sqrt{2}vt+c)}^{2}.

From the definition of
d_{1}(t),\,d_{2}(t),\,d(t)
, we know that
\max_{j\{1,\,2\}}\left\lvert\ddot{d}_{j}(t)\right\rvert+e^{-\sqrt{2}d(t)}=O\Big(v^{2}\sech{(\sqrt{2}vt+c)}^{2}\Big)
and since
z(0)=d(0),\,\dot{z}(0)=\dot{d}(0),
we have that
v,\,c
satisfy the following identities

v=\left(e^{-\sqrt{2}z(0)}+\Big(\frac{\dot{x}_{2}(0)-\dot{x}_{1}(0)}{2}\Big)^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}},\,c=\arctanh{\Big(\frac{\dot{x}_{2}(0)-\dot{x}_{1}(0)}{2v}\Big)},

so Theorem
2.9
implies that
v\lesssim\epsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}.

From the Corollary
1.13
and the Theorem
1.11
, we deduce that
\exists C>0
such that if
\epsilon\ll 1
and
0\leq t\leq\frac{\ln{\ln{(\frac{1}{\epsilon})}}\ln{(\frac{1}{\epsilon})}}{\epsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}},
then we have that

\displaystyle\max_{j\in\{1,\,2\}}\left\lvert\ddot{x}_{j}(t)\right\rvert=O\Big(\max_{j\in\{1,2\}}\left\lvert\ddot{d}_{j}(t)\right\rvert\Big)+O\left(\epsilon^{\frac{3}{2}}\ln{\Big(\frac{1}{\epsilon}\Big)}^{9}\exp\Big(\frac{Ct\epsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}}{\ln{(\frac{1}{\epsilon})}}\Big)\right),

(241)

\displaystyle e^{-\sqrt{2}z(t)}=e^{-\sqrt{2}d(t)}+O\left(\max\left(e^{-\sqrt{2}d(t)},e^{-\sqrt{2}z(t)}\right)\left\lvert z(t)-d(t)\right\rvert\right)=e^{-\sqrt{2}d(t)}+O\left(\epsilon^{2}\ln{\Big(\frac{1}{\epsilon}\Big)}^{9}\exp\Big(\frac{Ct\epsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}}{\ln{(\frac{1}{\epsilon})}}\Big)\right).

(242)

Next, we consider a smooth function
0\leq\chi_{2}(x)\leq 1
that satisfies

\chi_{2}(x)=\begin{cases}1,\text{ if $x\leq\frac{9}{20}$,}\\
0,\text{ if $x\geq\frac{1}{2}$.}\end{cases}

We denote

\chi_{2}(t,x)=\chi_{2}\Big(\frac{x-x_{1}(t)}{x-x_{2}(t)}\Big).

From Theorem
4.1
and Remark
4.4
, the estimates (
241
) and (
242
) of the modulation parameters imply that for the following functional

L_{1}(t)=\big\langle D^{2}E\left(H^{x_{2}(t)}_{0,1}+H^{x_{1}(t)}_{-1,0}\right)\overrightarrow{g(t)},\,\overrightarrow{g(t)}\big\rangle_{L^{2}\times L^{2}}+2\int_{\mathbb{R}}\partial_{t}g(t,x)\partial_{x}g(t,x)\Big[\dot{x}_{1}(t)\chi_{2}(t,x)+\dot{x}_{2}(t)(1-\chi_{2}(t,x))\Big]\,dx\\
-2\int_{\mathbb{R}}g(t,x)\Big(\dot{U}\left(H^{x_{1}(t)}_{-1,0}(x)\right)+\dot{U}\left(H^{x_{2}(t)}_{0,1}(x)\right)-\dot{U}\left(H^{x_{2}(t)}_{0,1}(x)+H^{x_{1}(t)}_{-1,0}(x)\right)\Big)\,dx\\
+2\int_{\mathbb{R}}g(t,x)\Big[(\dot{x}_{1}(t))^{2}\partial^{2}_{x}H^{x_{1}(t)}_{-1,0}(x)+(\dot{x}_{2}(t))^{2}\partial^{2}_{x}H^{x_{2}(t)}_{0,1}(x)\Big]\,dx+\frac{1}{3}\int_{\mathbb{R}}U^{(3)}\left(H^{x_{2}(t)}_{0,1}(x)+H^{x_{1}(t)}_{-1,0}(x)\right)g(t,x)^{3}\,dx,

and the following quantity
\delta_{1}(t)
denoted by

\delta_{1}(t)=\left\lVert\overrightarrow{g(t)}\right\rVert\Big(e^{-\sqrt{2}z(t)}\max_{j\in\{1,2\}}\left\lvert\dot{x}_{j}(t)\right\rvert+\max_{j\in\{1,2\}}\left\lvert\dot{x}_{j}(t)\right\rvert^{3}e^{-\frac{9\sqrt{2}z(t)}{20}}+\max_{j\in\{1,2\}}\left\lvert\dot{x}_{j}(t)\right\rvert\left\lvert\ddot{x}_{j}(t)\right\rvert\Big)\\
+\left\lVert\overrightarrow{g(t)}\right\rVert^{2}\Big(\frac{\max_{j\in\{1,\,2\}}\left\lvert\dot{x}_{j}(t)\right\rvert}{z(t)}+\max_{j\in\{1,\,2\}}\dot{x}_{j}(t)^{2}+\max_{j\in\{1,\,2\}}\left\lvert\ddot{x}_{j}(t)\right\rvert\Big)+\left\lVert\overrightarrow{g(t)}\right\rVert^{4},

we have
\left\lvert\dot{L}_{1}(t)\right\rvert=O(\delta_{1}(t))
for
t\geq 0.

Moreover, estimates (
241
), (
242
) and the bound
\dot{L}_{1}(t)=O(\delta_{1}(t))
implies that for

\delta_{2}(t)=\left\lVert\overrightarrow{g(t)}\right\rVert v^{2}\epsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}\sech{(\sqrt{2}vt+c)}^{2}+\left\lVert\overrightarrow{g(t)}\right\rVert\epsilon^{2}\ln{\Big(\frac{1}{\epsilon}\Big)}^{9}\exp\Big(\frac{Ct\epsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}}{\ln{(\frac{1}{\epsilon})}}\Big)\\
+\epsilon^{\frac{3}{2}}e^{-\frac{9\sqrt{2}z(t)}{20}}\left\lVert\overrightarrow{g(t)}\right\rVert+\max_{j\in\{1,2\}}\frac{\left\lvert\dot{x}_{j}(t)\right\rvert}{z(t)}\left\lVert\overrightarrow{g(t)}\right\rVert^{2}+\left\lVert\overrightarrow{g(t)}\right\rVert^{4},

\left\lvert\dot{L}_{1}(t)\right\rvert=O(\delta_{2}(t))
if
0\leq t\leq\frac{\ln{\ln{(\frac{1}{\epsilon})}}\ln{(\frac{1}{\epsilon})}}{\epsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}}.

Now, similarly to the proof of Theorem
4.5
, we denote
G(s)=\max\big(\left\lVert\overrightarrow{g(s)}\right\rVert,\epsilon\big).

From Theorem
4.1
and Remark
4.4
, we have that there are positive constants
K,k>0
independent of
\epsilon
such that

k\left\lVert\overrightarrow{g(t)}\right\rVert^{2}\leq L_{1}(t)+K\epsilon^{2}.

We recall that Theorem
2.9
implies that

\ln{\Big(\frac{1}{\epsilon}\Big)}\lesssim z(t),\,e^{-\sqrt{2}z(t)}+\max_{j}\left\lvert\dot{x}_{j}(t)\right\rvert^{2}+\max_{j\in\{1,\,2\}}\left\lvert\ddot{x}_{j}(t)\right\rvert=O(\epsilon),

from which with the definition of
G(s)
and estimates (
241
) and (
242
) we deduce that

\delta_{1}(t)\lesssim G(t)v^{2}\sech{(\sqrt{2}vt+c)}^{2}\epsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}+G(t)\epsilon^{\frac{39}{20}}+G(t)^{2}\frac{\epsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}}{\ln{(\frac{1}{\epsilon})}},

while
0\leq t\leq\frac{\ln{\ln{(\frac{1}{\epsilon})}}\ln{(\frac{1}{\epsilon})}}{\epsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}}.

In conclusion, the Fundamental Theorem of Calculus implies that
\exists K>0
independent of
\epsilon
such that

G(t)^{2}\leq K\left(G(0)^{2}+\int_{0}^{t}G(s)v^{2}\sech{(\sqrt{2}vs+c)}^{2}\epsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}+G(s)\epsilon^{\frac{39}{20}}+G(s)^{2}\frac{\epsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}}{\ln{(\frac{1}{\epsilon})}}\,ds\right),

(243)

while
0\leq t\leq\frac{\ln{\ln{(\frac{1}{\epsilon})}}\ln{(\frac{1}{\epsilon})}}{\epsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}}.
Since
\frac{d}{dt}[\tanh{(\sqrt{2}vt+c)}]=\sqrt{2}v\sech{(\sqrt{2}vt+c)}^{2},
we verify that while the term
G(s)v^{2}\sech{(\sqrt{2}vt+c)}^{2}\epsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}
is the dominant in the integral of the estimate (
243
), then
G(t)\lesssim G(0).

The remaining case corresponds when
G(s)^{2}\frac{\epsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}}{\ln{(\frac{1}{\epsilon})}}
is the dominant term in the integral of (
243
) from an instant
0\leq t_{0}\leq\frac{\ln{\ln{(\frac{1}{\epsilon})}}\ln{(\frac{1}{\epsilon})}}{\epsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}}.
Similarly to the proof of
4.5
, we have for
t_{0}\leq t\leq\frac{\ln{\ln{(\frac{1}{\epsilon})}}\ln{(\frac{1}{\epsilon})}}{\epsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}}
that

G(t)\lesssim G(t_{0})\exp\Big(C\frac{(t-t_{0})\epsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}}{\ln{(\frac{1}{\epsilon})}}\Big).

In conclusion, in any case we have for
0\leq t\leq\frac{\ln{\ln{(\frac{1}{\epsilon})}}\ln{(\frac{1}{\epsilon})}}{\epsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}}
that

G(t)\lesssim G(0)\exp\Big(C\frac{t\epsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}}{\ln{(\frac{1}{\epsilon})}}\Big).

(244)

However, for
T\geq\frac{\ln{\ln{(\frac{1}{\epsilon})}}\ln{(\frac{1}{\epsilon})}}{\epsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}}
and
K>2
we have that

\epsilon\ln{\Big(\frac{1}{\epsilon}\Big)}\exp\left(K\frac{\epsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}T}{\ln{(\frac{1}{2})}}\right)\leq\epsilon\exp\left(\frac{2K\epsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}T}{\ln{(\frac{1}{2})}}\right).

In conclusion, from the result of Theorem
4.5
, we can exchange the constant
C>0
by a larger constant such that estimate (
244
) is true for all
t\geq 0.

Appendix A
Auxiliary Results

We start the Appendix Section by presenting the following lemma:

Lemma A.1
.

With the same hypothesis as in Theorem
1.11
and using its notation, we have while
\max_{j\in\{1\,,2\}}\left\lvert d_{j}(t)-x_{j}(t)\right\rvert<1
that

\max_{j\in\{1,\,2\}}\left\lvert\ddot{d}_{j}(t)-\ddot{x}_{j}(t)\right\rvert=O\Big(\max_{j\in\{1,\,2\}}\left\lvert d_{j}(t)-x_{j}(t)\right\rvert\epsilon+\epsilon z(t)e^{-\sqrt{2}z(t)}+\left\lVert\overrightarrow{g(t)}\right\rVert\epsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}\Big).

Lemma A.2
.

For
U(\phi)=\phi^{2}(1-\phi^{2})^{2},
we have that

\dot{U}\left(H^{x_{1}(t)}_{-1,0}(x)+H^{x_{2}(t)}_{0,1}(x)\right)-\dot{U}\left(H^{x_{1}(t)}_{-1,0}(x)\right)-\dot{U}\left(H^{x_{2}(t)}_{0,1}(x)\right)=24e^{-\sqrt{2}z(t)}\left(\frac{H^{x_{1}(t)}_{-1,0}(x)}{(1+e^{-2\sqrt{2}(x-x_{1}(t))})^{\frac{1}{2}}}+\frac{H^{x_{2}(t)}_{0,1}(x)}{(1+e^{2\sqrt{2}(x-x_{2}(t))})^{\frac{1}{2}}}\right)\\
-30e^{-\sqrt{2}z(t)}\left(\frac{H^{x_{1}(t)}_{-1,0}(x)^{3}}{(1+e^{-2\sqrt{2}(x-x_{1}(t))})^{\frac{1}{2}}}+\frac{H^{x_{2}(t)}_{0,1}(x)^{3}}{(1+e^{2\sqrt{2}(x-x_{2}(t))})^{\frac{1}{2}}}\right)+r(t,x),

such that
\left\lVert r(t)\right\rVert_{L^{2}_{x}(\mathbb{R})}=O(e^{-2\sqrt{2}z(t)}).

Proof.

By directly computations, we verify that

\dot{U}\left(H^{x_{1}(t)}_{-1,0}(x)+H^{x_{2}(t)}_{0,1}(x)\right)-\dot{U}\left(H^{x_{1}(t)}_{-1,0}(x)\right)-\dot{U}\left(H^{x_{2}(t)}_{0,1}(x)\right)=-24H^{x_{1}(t)}_{-1,0}H^{x_{2}(t)}_{0,1}(H^{x_{1}(t)}_{-1,0}+H^{x_{2}(t)}_{0,1})\\
+30H^{x_{1}(t)}_{-1,0}H^{x_{2}(t)}_{0,1}((H^{x_{1}(t)}_{-1,0})^{3}+(H^{x_{2}(t)}_{0,1})^{3})+60(H^{x_{1}(t)}_{-1,0}H^{x_{2}(t)}_{0,1})^{2}(H^{x_{1}(t)}_{-1,0}+H^{x_{2}(t)}_{0,1}).

First, from the definition of
H_{0,1}(x),
we verify that

\displaystyle 60(H^{x_{1}(t)}_{-1,0}H^{x_{2}(t)}_{0,1})^{2}(H^{x_{1}(t)}_{-1,0}+H^{x_{2}(t)}_{0,1})=60e^{-2\sqrt{2}z(t)}\left(\frac{H^{x_{2}(t)}_{0,1}}{(1+e^{2\sqrt{2}(x-x_{2}(t))})(1+e^{-2\sqrt{2}(x-x_{1}(t))})}\right)

\displaystyle+60e^{-2\sqrt{2}z(t)}\left(\frac{H^{x_{1}(t)}_{-1,0}}{(1+e^{-2\sqrt{2}(x-x_{1}(t))})(1+e^{2\sqrt{2}(x-x_{2}(t))})}\right).

Using (
4
), we can verify using by induction for any
k\in\mathbb{N}
that

\left\lvert\frac{d^{k}}{dx^{k}}\left[\frac{1}{(1+e^{2\sqrt{2}x})}\right]\right\rvert=\left\lvert\frac{d^{k}}{dx^{k}}\left[1-\frac{e^{2\sqrt{2}x}}{(1+e^{2\sqrt{2}x})}\right]\right\rvert=\left\lvert\frac{d^{k}}{dx^{k}}\left[H_{0,1}(x)^{2}\right]\right\rvert=O(1),

(245)

and since

\frac{H_{0,1}(x)}{(1+e^{2\sqrt{2}x})}=\frac{e^{\sqrt{2}x}}{(1+e^{2\sqrt{2}x})^{\frac{3}{2}}}

is a Schwartz function, we deduce that

60(H^{x_{1}(t)}_{-1,0}H^{x_{2}(t)}_{0,1})^{2}(H^{x_{1}(t)}_{-1,0}+H^{x_{2}(t)}_{0,1})
is in
H^{k}_{x}(\mathbb{R})
for all
k>0
and

\left\lVert(H^{x_{1}(t)}_{-1,0}H^{x_{2}(t)}_{0,1})^{2}(H^{x_{1}(t)}_{-1,0}+H^{x_{2}(t)}_{0,1})\right\rVert_{H^{k}(\mathbb{R})}=O(e^{-2\sqrt{2}z(t)}).

(246)

Next, using the identity

H^{x_{1}(t)}_{-1,0}(x)H^{x_{2}(t)}_{0,1}(x)=-\frac{e^{-\sqrt{2}z(t)}}{(1+e^{2\sqrt{2}(x-x_{2}(t))})^{\frac{1}{2}}(1+e^{-2\sqrt{2}(x-x_{1}(t))})^{\frac{1}{2}}},

(247)

the identity

1-\frac{1}{(1+e^{2\sqrt{2}x})^{\frac{1}{2}}}=\frac{e^{2\sqrt{2}x}}{(1+e^{2\sqrt{2}x})^{\frac{1}{2}}+(1+e^{2\sqrt{2}x})},

and Lemma
2.3
, we deduce that

\displaystyle\left\lVert 24(H^{x_{1}(t)}_{-1,0})^{2}H^{x_{2}(t)}_{0,1}+24e^{-\sqrt{2}z(t)}\frac{H^{x_{1}(t)}_{-1,0}(x)}{(1+e^{-2\sqrt{2}(x-x_{1}(t))})^{\frac{1}{2}}}\right\rVert_{L^{2}_{x}(\mathbb{R})}=O(e^{-2\sqrt{2}z(t)}),

(248)

\displaystyle\left\lVert 30(H^{x_{1}(t)}_{-1,0})^{4}H^{x_{2}(t)}_{0,1}+30e^{-\sqrt{2}z(t)}\left(\frac{(H^{x_{1}(t)}_{-1,0}(x))^{3}}{(1+e^{-2\sqrt{2}(x-x_{1}(t))})^{\frac{1}{2}}}\right)\right\rVert_{L^{2}_{x}(\mathbb{R})}=O(e^{-3\sqrt{2}z(t)}).

(249)

The estimate of the remaining terms
-24H^{x_{1}(t)}_{-1,0}(H^{x_{2}(t)}_{0,1})^{2},\,30H^{x_{1}(t)}_{-1,0}(H^{x_{2}(t)}_{0,1})^{4}
is completely analogous to (
248
) and (
249
) respectively. In conclusion, all of the estimates above imply the estimate stated in the Lemma
A.2
.
∎

Proof of Lemma
A.1
.

First, we recall the global estimate
e^{-\sqrt{2}z(t)}\lesssim\epsilon.
We also recall the identity (
27
)

\int_{\mathbb{R}}\big(8(H_{0,1}(x))^{3}-6(H_{0,1}(x))^{5}\big)e^{-\sqrt{2}x}\,dx=2\sqrt{2},

and the global estimate
e^{-\sqrt{2}z(t)}\lesssim\epsilon.

which, by integration by parts, implies that

\int_{\mathbb{R}}24\frac{H_{0,1}(x)\partial_{x}H_{0,1}(x)}{(1+e^{2\sqrt{2}(x)})^{\frac{1}{2}}}-30\frac{(H_{0,1}(x))^{3}\partial_{x}H_{0,1}(x)}{(1+e^{2\sqrt{2}(x)})^{\frac{1}{2}}}\,dx=4.

(250)

We recall
d_{1}(t),\,d_{2}(t)
defined in (
7
) and (
8
) respectively and
d(t)=d_{2}(t)-d_{1}(t).
Since, we have for
j\in\{1,\,2\}
that

\ddot{d}_{j}(t)=(-1)^{j}8\sqrt{2}e^{-\sqrt{2}d(t)},
we have
\ddot{d}(t)=16\sqrt{2}e^{-\sqrt{2}d(t)},
which clearly with the fact that

\left\lVert\partial_{x}H_{0,1}\right\rVert_{L^{2}}^{2}=\left\lVert\partial^{2}_{x}H_{0,1}\right\rVert_{L^{2}}^{2}=\frac{1}{2\sqrt{2}},

imply that
\ddot{d}_{j}(t)\left\lVert\partial_{x}H_{0,1}\right\rVert_{L^{2}}^{2}=(-1)^{j}4e^{-\sqrt{2}d(t)}.

We also recall the partial differential equation satisfied by the remainder
g(t,x)
(
155
), which can be rewritten as

\dot{U}\left(H^{x_{2}(t)}_{0,1}(x)+H^{x_{1}(t)}_{-1,0}(x)\right)-\dot{U}\left(H^{x_{1}(t)}_{-1,0}(x)\right)-\dot{U}\left(H^{x_{2}(t)}_{0,1}(x)\right)-\ddot{x}_{2}(t)\partial_{x}H^{x_{2}(t)}_{0,1}(x)=\\
-\left(\partial^{2}_{t}g(t,x)-\partial^{2}_{x}g(t,x)+\ddot{U}\left(H^{x_{2}(t)}_{0,1}(x)+H^{x_{1}(t)}_{-1,0}(x)\right)g(t,x)\right)+\sum_{k=3}^{6}U^{(k)}\left(H^{x_{1}(t)}_{-1,0}+H^{x_{2}(t)}_{0,1}\right)\frac{g(t)^{k-1}}{(k-1)!}\\
-\dot{x}_{1}(t)^{2}\partial^{2}_{x}H^{x_{1}(t)}_{-1,0}(x)-\dot{x}_{2}(t)^{2}\partial^{2}_{x}H^{x_{2}(t)}_{0,1}(x)+\ddot{x}_{1}(t)\partial_{x}H^{x_{1}(t)}_{-1,0}(x).

(251)

In conclusion, from the estimate (
250
), Lemma
A.2
and Lemma
2.3
, we obtain that

\left\langle\dot{U}\left(H^{x_{1}(t)}_{-1,0}+H^{x_{2}(t)}_{0,1}\right)-\dot{U}\left(H^{x_{1}(t)}_{-1,0}\right)-\dot{U}\left(H^{x_{2}(t)}_{0,1}\right),\,\partial_{x}H^{x_{2}(t)}_{0,1}\right\rangle_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R})}-\ddot{x}_{2}(t)\left\lVert\partial_{x}H_{0,1}\right\rVert_{L^{2}}^{2}=-(\ddot{x}_{2}(t)-\ddot{d}_{2}(t))\left\lVert\partial_{x}H_{0,1}\right\rVert_{L^{2}}^{2}\\
+O\Big(\left\lvert\ddot{x}_{1}(t)\right\rvert z(t)e^{-\sqrt{2}z(t)}+e^{-\sqrt{2}z(t)}\max_{j\in\{1,\,2\}}\left\lvert x_{j}(t)-d_{j}(t)\right\rvert+e^{-2\sqrt{2}z(t)}z(t)\Big).

(252)

We recall from the proof of Theorem
4.1
the following estimate

\left\lvert\int_{\mathbb{R}}\left[\ddot{U}\left(H^{x_{2}(t)}_{0,1}(x)\right)-\ddot{U}\left(H^{x_{2}(t)}_{0,1}(x)+H^{x_{1}(t)}_{-1,0}(x)\right)\right]\partial_{x}H^{x_{2}(t)}_{0,1}(x)g(t,x)\,dx\right\rvert=O\Big(\left\lVert\overrightarrow{g(t)}\right\rVert e^{-\sqrt{2}z(t)}\Big).

Also, from the Modulation Lemma, we have that

\displaystyle\langle\partial^{2}_{t}g(t),\partial_{x}H^{x_{2}(t)}_{0,1}\rangle_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle=\frac{d}{dt}\left[\langle\partial_{t}g(t),\partial_{x}H^{x_{2}(t)}_{0,1}\rangle_{L^{2}}\right]+\dot{x}_{2}(t)\langle\partial_{t}g(t),\partial_{x}H^{x_{2}(t)}_{0,1}\rangle_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle=\frac{d}{dt}\Big[\dot{x}_{2}(t)\langle g(t),\partial^{2}_{x}H^{x_{2}(t)}_{0,1}\rangle_{L^{2}}\Big]+\dot{x}_{2}(t)\langle\partial_{t}g(t),\partial_{x}H^{x_{2}(t)}_{0,1}\rangle_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle=\ddot{x}_{2}(t)\langle g(t),\partial^{2}_{x}H^{x_{2}(t)}_{0,1}\rangle_{L^{2}}+2\dot{x}_{2}(t)\langle\partial_{t}g(t),\partial_{x}H^{x_{2}(t)}_{0,1}\rangle_{L^{2}}.

In conclusion, since
\partial_{x}H^{x_{2}(t)}_{0,1}\in kerD^{2}E_{pot}\left(H^{x_{2}(t)}_{0,1}\right),
we obtain from (
252
) and (
251
) that

\left\lvert\ddot{x}_{2}(t)-\ddot{d}_{2}(t)\right\rvert=O\Big(\max_{j\in\{1,\,2\}}\left\lvert d_{j}(t)-x_{j}(t)\right\rvert\epsilon+\epsilon z(t)e^{-\sqrt{2}z(t)}\\
+\left\lVert\overrightarrow{g(t)}\right\rVert e^{-\sqrt{2}z(t)}+\left\lVert\overrightarrow{g(t)}\right\rVert\epsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}\Big),

the estimate of
\left\lvert\ddot{x}_{1}(t)-\ddot{d}_{1}(t)\right\rvert
is completely analogous, which finishes the demonstration.
∎

Lemma A.3
.

For any
\delta>0
there is a
\epsilon(\delta)>0
such that if

\left\lVert\phi(x)-H_{0,1}(x)\right\rVert_{H^{1}(\mathbb{R})}<+\infty,\,0<E_{pot}(\phi(x))-E_{pot}(H_{0,1})<\epsilon(\delta),

(253)

then there is a real number
y
such that

\left\lVert\phi(x)-H_{0,1}(x-y)\right\rVert_{H^{1}}\leq\delta.

Proof of Lemma
A.3
.

The proof of Lemma
2.6
will follow by a contradiction argument. We assume that there is a
c>0
and sequence of real functions
\left(\phi_{n}(x)\right)_{n}
satisfying

\displaystyle\lim_{n\to+\infty}E_{pot}(\phi_{n})=E_{pot}(H_{0,1}),

(254)

\displaystyle\left\lVert\phi_{n}(x)-H_{0,1}(x)\right\rVert_{H^{1}(\mathbb{R})}<+\infty,

(255)

such that

\inf_{y\in\mathbb{R}}\left\lVert\phi_{n}(x)-H_{0,1}(x+y)\right\rVert_{H^{1}(\mathbb{R})}>c.

(256)

Since
H_{0,1}
has minimum potential energy
E_{pot}
for all real functions
\phi(x)
with
\left\lVert\phi(x)-H_{0,1}(x)\right\rVert_{H^{1}_{x}}<+\infty,

we deduce that following condition

E_{pot}(H_{0,1})\leq E_{pot}(\phi_{n})

holds for all
n\in\mathbb{N}.

Also, for each
n\in\mathbb{N},
the function
h_{n}(x)=\min\left(\phi_{n}(x),1\right)
satisfies

\frac{dh_{n}(x)}{dx}=\begin{cases}0,\text{if $\phi_{n}(x)\geq 1,$}\\
\frac{d\phi_{n}(x)}{dx}\text{, if $\phi_{n}(x)<1,$}\end{cases}

for almost every
x\in\mathbb{R}
with respect to the Lebesgue measure.
In conclusion, from the definition of the potential energy
E_{pot}
and that
U(\phi)=\phi^{2}(1-\phi^{2})^{2},
we obtain that

E_{pot}(H_{0,1})\leq E_{pot}(h_{n})\leq E_{pot}(\phi_{n}),

from which we conclude that

\left\lVert\phi_{n}(x)-1\right\rVert_{L^{2}\left(\{x|\phi_{n}(x)>1\}\right)}^{2}+\left\lVert\frac{d\phi_{n}(x)}{dx}\right\rVert_{L^{2}\left(\{x|\phi_{n}(x)>1\}\right)}^{2}\lesssim\left\lvert E_{pot}(\phi_{n})-E_{pot}(H_{0,1})\right\rvert.

By an analogous argument, we can verify that

\left\lVert\phi_{n}(x)\right\rVert_{L^{2}\left(\{x|-\frac{1}{2}<\phi_{n}(x)<0\}\right)}^{2}+\left\lVert\frac{d\phi_{n}(x)}{dx}\right\rVert_{L^{2}\left(\{x|-\frac{1}{2}<\phi_{n}(x)<0\}\right)}^{2}\lesssim\left\lvert E_{pot}(\phi_{n})-E_{pot}(H_{0,1})\right\rvert,

and if there is
x_{0}\in\mathbb{R}
such that
\phi_{n}(x_{0})\leq-\frac{1}{2},
we would obtain that

\displaystyle\int_{x_{0}}^{+\infty}\frac{1}{2}\frac{d\phi_{n}(x)}{dx}^{2}+U(\phi_{n}(x))\,dx=\int_{x_{0}}^{+\infty}\sqrt{2U(\phi_{n}(x))}\left\lvert\frac{d\phi_{n}(x)}{dx}\right\rvert\,dx+\frac{1}{2}\int_{x_{0}}^{+\infty}\left(\left\lvert\frac{d\phi_{n}(x)}{dx}\right\rvert-\sqrt{2U(\phi_{n}(x))}\right)^{2}\,dx

\displaystyle\geq\int_{-\frac{1}{2}}^{1}\sqrt{2U(\phi)}\,d\phi=E_{pot}(H_{0,1})+\int_{-\frac{1}{2}}^{0}\sqrt{2U(\phi)}\,d\phi>E_{pot}(H_{0,1}),

which contradicts (
254
) if
n\gg 1.

In conclusion, after we replace
\phi_{n}(x)
with
\phi_{1,n}=\max\left(\min\left(\phi_{n}(x),1\right),0\right),
we can restrict the proof to the case where
0\leq\phi_{n}(x)\leq 1
and
n\gg 1.
Now, from the density of
H^{2}(\mathbb{R})
in
H^{1}(\mathbb{R})
and with the use of a mollifier distribution, we can also restrict the contradiction hypotheses to the situation where
0\leq\phi_{n}(x)\leq 1
and
\frac{d\phi_{n}}{dx}(x)
is a continuous function for all
n\in\mathbb{N}.

Also, we have that if
\left\lVert\phi(x)-H_{0,1}(x)\right\rVert_{H^{1}(\mathbb{R})}<+\infty,
then
E_{pot}(\phi(x))\geq E_{pot}(H_{0,1}(x)).
In conclusion, there is a sequence of positive numbers
\left(\epsilon_{n}\right)_{n}
such that

E_{pot}(\phi_{n})=E_{pot}(H_{0,1})+\epsilon_{n},\,\lim_{n\to+\infty}\epsilon_{n}=0.

Also,
\tau_{y}\phi(x)=\phi(x-y)
satisfies
E_{pot}(\phi(x))=E_{pot}(\tau_{y}\phi(x))
for any
y\in\mathbb{R}.
In conclusion, since for all
n\in\mathbb{N},

\lim_{x\to+\infty}\phi_{n}(x)=1
and
\lim_{x\to-\infty}\phi_{n}(x)=0,
we can restrict to the case where

\phi_{n}(0)=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}},

for all
n\in\mathbb{N}.
For
(v)_{+}=\max(v,0)
and
(v)_{-}=-\left(v-(v)_{+}\right),
since
\frac{d\phi_{n}(x)}{dx}
is a continuous function on
x,
we deduce that
\left(\frac{d\phi_{n}(x)}{dx}\right)_{+}
and
\left(\frac{d\phi_{n}(x)}{dx}\right)_{-}
are also continuous functions on
x
for all
n\in\mathbb{N}.
In conclusion, for any
n\in\mathbb{N},
we have that the set

U=\left\{x\in\mathbb{R}|\,\frac{d\phi_{n}(x)}{dx}<0\right\}

(257)

is an enumerable union of disjoint open intervals
(a_{k,n},b_{k,n})_{k\in\mathbb{N}}
, which are bounded, since
\lim_{x\to+\infty}\phi_{n}(x)=1,\,\lim_{x\to-\infty}\phi_{n}(x)=0
and
0\leq\phi_{n}(x)\leq 1.
Now, let
E
be a set of open bounded intervals
(h_{i,n},l_{i,n})\subset\mathbb{R}
satisfying the conditions

\phi_{n}(h_{i,n})=\phi_{n}(l_{i,n}),

(258)

\{i|\,(h_{i,n},l_{i,n})\in E\}=I\subset\mathbb{Z}
and
l_{i,n}<h_{i+1,n}.
For any
i\in I,
the following function

f_{i,n}(x)=\begin{cases}\phi_{n}(x)\text{ if $x\leq h_{i,n}$},\\
\phi_{n}(x+l_{i,n}-h_{i,n})\text{ if $x>h_{i,n},$}\\
\end{cases}

satisfies
E_{pot}(H_{0,1})\leq E_{pot}(f_{i,n})\leq E_{pot}(\phi_{n})=E_{pot}(H_{0,1})+\epsilon_{n},
which implies that

\int_{h_{i,n}}^{l_{i,n}}\frac{1}{2}\frac{d\phi_{n}(x)}{dx}^{2}+U(\phi_{n}(x))\leq\epsilon_{n}.

Furthermore, we can deduce from Lebesgue’s dominated convergence theorem that

\sum_{i\in I}\int_{h_{i,n}}^{l_{i,n}}\frac{1}{2}\frac{d\phi_{n}(x)}{dx}^{2}+U(\phi_{n}(x))\leq\epsilon_{n},

(259)

for every finite or enumerable collection
E
of disjoint open bounded intervals
(h_{i,n},l_{i,n})\subset\mathbb{R},\,i\in I\subset\mathbb{Z}
such that

\phi_{n}(h_{i,n})=\phi_{n}(l_{i,n}).
In conclusion, we can deduce from (
259
) that

\int_{\mathbb{R}}\left(\frac{d\phi_{n}(x)}{dx}\right)_{-}^{2}\,dx\leq 2\epsilon_{n},

(260)

and so for
1\ll n
we have that

\left\lVert\frac{d\phi_{n}(x)}{dx}-\left\lvert\frac{d\phi_{n}(x)}{dx}\right\rvert\right\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R})}^{2}\leq 8\epsilon_{n},\,\phi_{n}(0)=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}.

(261)

Moreover, we can verify that

E_{pot}(\phi_{n})=\frac{1}{2}\left[\int_{\mathbb{R}}\left(\left\lvert\frac{d\phi_{n}(x)}{dx}\right\rvert-\sqrt{2U(\phi_{n}(x))}\right)^{2}\,dx\right]+\int_{\mathbb{R}}\sqrt{2U(\phi_{n}(x))}\left\lvert\frac{d\phi_{n}(x)}{dx}\right\rvert\,dx,

from which we deduce with
\lim_{x\to-\infty}\phi_{n}(x)=0
and
\lim_{x\to+\infty}\phi_{n}(x)=1
that

\displaystyle E_{pot}(H_{0,1})+\epsilon_{n}=E_{pot}(\phi_{n})

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1}{2}\left[\int_{\mathbb{R}}\left(\left\lvert\frac{d\phi_{n}(x)}{d(x)}\right\rvert-\sqrt{2U(\phi_{n}(x))}\right)^{2}\,dx\right]+\int_{0}^{1}\sqrt{2U(\phi)}\,d\phi

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\left[\int_{\mathbb{R}}\left(\left\lvert\frac{d\phi_{n}(x)}{d(x)}\right\rvert-\sqrt{2U(\phi_{n}(x))}\right)^{2}\,dx\right]+E_{pot}(H_{0,1}).

Then, from estimate (
261
), we have that

\frac{d\phi_{n}(x)}{dx}=\sqrt{2U(\phi_{n}(x))}+r_{n}(x),\,\phi_{n}(0)=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}},

(262)

with
\left\lVert r_{n}\right\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R})}\lesssim\epsilon_{n}
for all
1\ll n.
We recall that
U(\phi)=\phi^{2}(1-\phi^{2})^{2}
is a Lipschitz function in the set
\{\phi|\,0\leq\phi\leq 1\}.
Then, because
H_{0,1}(x)
is the unique solution of the following ordinary differential equation

\begin{cases}\frac{d\phi(x)}{dx}=\sqrt{2U(\phi(x))},\\
\phi(0)=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}},\end{cases}

we deduce from Gronwall Lemma that for any
K>0
we have

\lim_{n\to+\infty}\left\lVert\phi_{n}(x)-H_{0,1}(x)\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}[-K,K]}=0,\,\lim_{n\to+\infty}\left\lVert\frac{d\phi_{n}(x)}{dx}-\dot{H}_{0,1}(x)\right\rVert_{L^{2}[-K,K]}=0.

(263)

Also, if
1\ll n,
then
\left\lVert\frac{d\phi_{n}(x)}{dx}\right\rVert_{L^{2}_{x}(\mathbb{R})}^{2}<2E_{pot}(H_{0,1})+1,
and so we obtain from Cauchy-Schwartz inequality that

\left\lvert\phi_{n}(x)-\phi_{n}(y)\right\rvert\leq\left\lvert x-y\right\rvert^{\frac{1}{2}}\left\lVert\frac{d\phi_{n}}{dx}\right\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R})}^{2}<M\left\lvert x-y\right\rvert^{\frac{1}{2}},

(264)

for a constant
M>0.
The inequality (
264
) implies that for any
1>\omega>0
there is a number
h(\omega)\in\mathbb{N}
such that
if
n\geq h(\omega)
then

\left\lVert\phi_{n}(x)-H_{0,1}(x)\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}\{x|\,\frac{1}{\omega}<\left\lvert x\right\rvert\}}<\omega,

(265)

otherwise we would obtain that there are
0<\theta<\frac{1}{4},
a subsequence
(m_{n})_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
and a sequence of real numbers
(x_{n})_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
with
\lim_{n\to+\infty}m_{n}=+\infty,\,\left\lvert x_{n}\right\rvert>n+1
such that

\displaystyle\left\lvert\phi_{m_{n}}(x_{n})-1\right\rvert>\theta\text{ if $x_{n}>0,$}

(266)

\displaystyle\left\lvert\phi_{m_{n}}(x_{n})\right\rvert|>\theta\text{ if $x_{n}<0.$}

(267)

However, since we are considering
\phi_{n}(x)\in C^{1}(\mathbb{R})
and
0\leq\phi_{n}\leq 1,
we would obtain from the mean value theorem that there would exist a sequence
(y_{n})_{n}
with
y_{n}>x_{n}>n+1
or
y_{n}<x_{n}<-n-1
such that

\displaystyle 1-\theta\leq\phi_{m_{n}}(y_{n})\leq 1+\theta,\text{ if $y_{n}>0,$}

(268)

\displaystyle\phi_{m_{n}}(y_{n})=\theta\text{ otherwise.}

(269)

But, estimates (
264
), (
268
), (
269
) and identity
U(\phi)=\phi^{2}(1-\phi^{2})^{2}
would imply that

1\lesssim\int_{\left\lvert x\right\rvert\geq n-2}U(\phi_{m_{n}}(x))\,dx\text{ for all $n\gg 1,$}

(270)

and because of estimate (
263
) and the following identity

\lim_{K\to+\infty}\int_{-K}^{K}\frac{1}{2}\dot{H}_{0,1}(x)^{2}+U(H_{0,1}(x))=E_{pot}(H_{0,1}(x)),

(271)

estimate (
270
) would imply that
\lim_{n\to+\infty}E_{pot}(\phi_{m_{n}})>E_{pot}(H_{0,1})
which contradicts our hypotheses. In conclusion, for any
1>\omega>0
there is a number
h(\omega)
such that if
n\geq h(\omega)
then (
265
) holds. So we deduce for any
0<\omega<1
that there is a number
h_{1}(\omega)
such that

\text{if $n\geq h_{1}(\omega),$ then }\left\lvert\phi_{n}(x)-H_{0,1}(x)\right\rvert\leq\omega\text{ for all $x\in\mathbb{R}.$}

(272)

Then, if
\omega\leq\frac{1}{100},\,n\geq h(\omega)
and
K\geq 200,
estimates (
272
) and (
263
) imply that

\displaystyle\int_{K}^{+\infty}U(\phi_{n}(x))+\frac{1}{2}\frac{d\phi_{n}(x)}{dx}^{2}\,dx\geq\frac{1}{2}\int_{K}^{+\infty}\left(1-\phi_{n}(x)\right)^{2}+\frac{d\phi_{n}(x)}{dx}^{2}\,dx,

(273)

\displaystyle\int_{-\infty}^{-K}U(\phi_{n}(x))+\frac{1}{2}\frac{d\phi_{n}(x)}{dx}^{2}\,dx\geq\frac{1}{2}\int_{-\infty}^{-K}\phi_{n}(x)^{2}+\frac{d\phi_{n}(x)}{dx}^{2}\,dx.

(274)

In conclusion, from estimates (
272
), (
273
), (
274
) and

\lim_{K\to+\infty}\int_{|x|\geq K}\frac{1}{2}\dot{H}_{0,1}(x)^{2}+U(H_{0,1}(x))\,dx=0,

we obtain that
\lim_{n\to+\infty}\left\lVert\phi_{n}-H_{0,1}(x)\right\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R})}=0
and, from the equation in (
262
) is satisfied for each
\phi_{n},
we conclude that
\lim_{n\to+\infty}\left\lVert\frac{d\phi_{n}}{dx}-\dot{H}_{0,1}(x)\right\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R})}=0.
In conclusion, if
1\ll n,
inequality (
256
) is false.
∎

From Lemma
A.3
, we obtain the following corollary:

Corollary A.4
.

For any
\delta>0
there is a
\epsilon_{0}>0
such that if
\epsilon\leq\epsilon_{0},\,\left\lVert\phi(x)-H_{0,1}(x)-H_{-1,0}(x)\right\rVert_{H^{1}(\mathbb{R})}<+\infty
and
E_{pot}(\phi)=2E_{pot}(H_{0,1})+\epsilon,
then there are
x_{2},x_{1}\in\mathbb{R}
such that

x_{2}-x_{1}\geq\frac{1}{\delta},\,\left\lVert\phi(x)-H_{0,1}(x-x_{2})+H_{-1,0}(x-x_{1})\right\rVert_{H^{1}_{x}(\mathbb{R})}\leq\delta.

(275)

proof of Corollary
A.4
.

First, from a similar reasoning to the proof of Lemma
A.3
we can assume by density that
\frac{d\phi(x)}{dx}\in H^{1}_{x}(\mathbb{R}).
Next, from hypothesis
\left\lVert\phi(x)-H_{0,1}(x)-H_{-1,0}(x)\right\rVert_{H^{1}(\mathbb{R})}<+\infty,
we deduce using the mean value theorem that there is an
y\in\mathbb{R}
such that
\phi(y)=0.
Now, we consider the functions

\phi_{-}(x)=\begin{cases}\phi(x)\text{ if $x\leq y,$}\\
0\text{ otherwise,}\end{cases}

and

\phi_{+}(x)=\begin{cases}0\text{ if $x\leq y,$}\\
\phi(x)\text{ otherwise.}\end{cases}

Clearly,
\phi(x)=\phi_{-}(x)
for
x<y
and
\phi(x)=\phi_{+}(x)
for
x>y.
From identity
U(0)=0,
we deduce that

E_{pot}(\phi)=E_{pot}(\phi_{-})+E_{pot}(\phi_{+}),

also we have that

E_{pot}(H_{-1,0})<E_{pot}(\phi_{-}),\,E_{pot}(H_{0,1})<E_{pot}(\phi_{+}).

In conclusion, since
E_{pot}(\phi)=2E_{pot}(H_{0,1})+\epsilon,
Lemma
A.3
implies that if
\epsilon<\epsilon_{0}\ll 1,
then there is
x_{2},\,x_{1}\in\mathbb{R}
such that

\left\lVert\phi(x)-H_{0,1}(x-x_{2})-H_{-1,0}(x-x_{1})\right\rVert_{H^{1}}\leq\left\lVert\phi_{+}-H_{0,1}(x-x_{2})\right\rVert_{H^{1}}+\left\lVert\phi_{-}-H_{-1,0}(x-x_{1})\right\rVert_{H^{1}}\leq\delta.

(276)

So, to finish the proof of Corollary
A.4
, we need only to verify that we have
x_{2}-x_{1}\geq\frac{1}{\delta}
if
0<\epsilon_{0}\ll 1.
But, we recall that
H_{0,1}(0)=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}},
from which with estimate (
276
) we deduce that

\left\lvert\phi_{+}(x_{2})-\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\right\rvert\lesssim\delta,\left\lvert\phi_{-}(x_{1})+\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\right\rvert\lesssim\delta,

(277)

so if
\epsilon_{0}\ll 1,
then
x_{1}<y<x_{2}.
From Lemma
2.8
, we can verify that
f(z)=\left\lVert DE_{pot}(H_{0,1}^{z}(x)+H_{-1,0}(x))\right\rVert_{L^{2}}
is a bounded function in
\mathbb{R}_{+},
from which with estimate (
276
) we deduce that if
0<\epsilon_{0}\ll 1,
then

\left\lvert E_{pot}(\phi)-E_{pot}\left(H_{0,1}(x-x_{2})+H_{-1,0}(x-x_{1})\right)\right\rvert<e^{-2\sqrt{2}\frac{1}{\delta}}.

In conclusion, we obtain from Lemma
2.4
and the estimate above that
x_{2}-x_{1}\geq\frac{1}{\delta}
if
0<\epsilon_{0}\ll 1
and
\epsilon<\epsilon_{0}.

∎

Now, we complement our material by presenting the proof of Identity (
27
) and the proof of The Modulation Lemma.

Proof of Identity (
27
).

From the definition of the function
H_{0,1}(x),
we have

\int_{\mathbb{R}}\big(8(H_{0,1}(x))^{3}-6(H_{0,1}(x))^{5}\big)e^{-\sqrt{2}x}\,dx=\int_{\mathbb{R}}\frac{8e^{2\sqrt{2}x}+2e^{4\sqrt{2}x}}{(1+e^{2\sqrt{2}x})^{\frac{5}{2}}}\,dx,

by the change of variable
y(x)=(1+e^{2\sqrt{2}x}),
we obtain

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}}\big(8(H_{0,1}(x))^{3}-6(H_{0,1}(x))^{5}\big)e^{-\sqrt{2}x}\,dx

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2\sqrt{2}}\int_{1}^{\infty}\frac{8}{y^{\frac{5}{2}}}+\frac{2(y-1)}{y^{\frac{5}{2}}}\,dy=\frac{1}{2\sqrt{2}}\int_{1}^{\infty}\frac{6}{y^{\frac{5}{2}}}+\frac{2}{y^{\frac{3}{2}}}\,dy,

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2\sqrt{2}}(-4y^{-\frac{3}{2}}-4y^{-\frac{1}{2}})\Big|_{1}^{\infty}=2\sqrt{2}.

∎

Proof of the Modulation Lemma.

First, let
x_{2},\,x_{1}\in\mathbb{R}
and
g\in H^{1}(\mathbb{R})
such that
x_{2}-x_{1}\geq\frac{1}{\delta_{0}}
with
\delta_{0}>0
small enough to be chosen later. Then, we define the following map
F:\mathbb{R}^{2}\times H^{1}(\mathbb{R})\to\mathbb{R}^{2}
by

F((h_{2},h_{1}),g(x))=\begin{bmatrix}\langle\partial_{x}H^{x_{2}+h_{2}}_{0,1},\,H^{x_{2}}_{0,1}+H^{x_{1}}_{-1,0}-H^{x_{1}+h_{1}}_{-1,0}+g\rangle_{L^{2}}\\
\langle\partial_{x}H^{x_{1}+h_{1}}_{-1,0},\,H^{x_{2}}_{0,1}+H^{x_{1}}_{-1,0}-H^{x_{2}+h_{2}}_{0,1}+g\rangle_{L^{2}}\\
\end{bmatrix}

for any
((h_{1},h_{2}),g)\in\mathbb{R}^{2}\times H^{1}(\mathbb{R}).
Clearly,
F(0,0,0)=(0,0),
also,
we can verify that the Derivative
DF_{h_{2},h_{1}}((0,0),g)
is given by

\begin{bmatrix}\left\lVert\partial_{x}H_{0,1}\right\rVert_{L^{2}}^{2}+\langle\partial_{x}H^{x_{2}}_{0,1},\,\frac{dg}{dx}\rangle&\langle\partial_{x}H^{x_{2}}_{0,1},\,\partial_{x}H^{x_{1}}_{-1,0}\rangle\\
\langle\partial_{x}H^{x_{2}}_{0,1},\,\partial_{x}H^{x_{1}}_{-1,0}\rangle&\left\lVert\partial_{x}H_{0,1}\right\rVert_{L^{2}}^{2}+\langle\partial_{x}H^{x_{1}}_{-1,0},\,\frac{dg}{dx}\rangle\end{bmatrix}.

Then,
R_{g}(h_{2},h_{1})=F(h_{2},h_{1},g)-F(0,0,g)-DF_{h_{2},h_{1}}(0,0,g)(h_{2},h_{1})
satisfies the following identity

R_{g}(h_{2},h_{1})=\begin{bmatrix}\langle\partial_{x}H^{x_{2}+h_{2}}_{0,1}-\partial_{x}H^{x_{2}}_{0,1}+h_{2}\partial^{2}_{x}H^{x_{2}}_{0,1},\,g\rangle\\
\langle\partial_{x}H^{x_{1}+h_{1}}_{-1,0}-\partial_{x}H^{x_{1}}_{-1,0}+h_{1}\partial^{2}_{x}H^{x_{1}}_{-1,0},\,g\rangle\end{bmatrix}+\begin{bmatrix}\langle\partial_{x}H^{x_{2}+h_{2}}_{0,1},\,H^{x_{2}}_{0,1}-H^{x_{2}+h_{2}}_{0,1}-h_{2}\partial_{x}H^{x_{2}+h_{2}}_{0,1}\rangle\\
\langle\partial_{x}H^{x_{1}+h_{1}}_{-1,0},\,H^{x_{1}}_{-1,0}-H^{x_{1}+h_{1}}_{-1,0}-h_{1}\partial_{x}H^{x_{1}+h_{1}}_{-1,0}\rangle\end{bmatrix}\\
+\begin{bmatrix}\langle\partial_{x}H^{x_{2}+h_{2}}_{0,1},\,H^{x_{1}}_{-1,0}-H^{x_{1}+h_{1}}_{-1,0}\rangle-h_{1}\langle\partial_{x}H^{x_{2}}_{0,1},\,\partial_{x}H^{x_{1}}_{-1,0}\rangle\\
\langle\partial_{x}H^{x_{1}+h_{1}}_{-1,0},\,H^{x_{2}}_{0,1}-H^{x_{2}+h_{2}}_{0,1}\rangle-h_{2}\langle\partial_{x}H^{x_{2}}_{0,1},\,\partial_{x}H^{x_{1}}_{-1,0}\rangle\end{bmatrix}

(278)

for all
(h_{2},h_{1})\in\mathbb{R}^{2},
also it is not difficult to verify that
R_{g}(0,0)=(0,0).
Also for
\delta_{0}>0
small enough, if
\max\{\left\lvert h_{1}\right\rvert,\left\lvert h_{2}\right\rvert\}=O(\delta_{0}),\,\left\lVert g\right\rVert_{H^{1}(\mathbb{R})}\leq\delta_{0},
it is not difficult to verify from Lemma
2.3
that

\left\lvert R_{g}(h_{2},h_{1})\right\rvert\lesssim\left\lVert g\right\rVert_{H^{1}}\max\{\left\lvert h_{1}\right\rvert,\left\lvert h_{2}\right\rvert\}^{2}+\max\{\left\lvert h_{1}\right\rvert,\left\lvert h_{2}\right\rvert\}^{3}+\max\{\left\lvert h_{1}\right\rvert,\left\lvert h_{2}\right\rvert\}^{2}(x_{2}-x_{1})e^{-\sqrt{2}(x_{2}-x_{1})},

from which we deduce that

\left\lvert R_{g}(h_{2},h_{1})\right\rvert=O\Big((\delta_{0}^{2}+e^{-\frac{\sqrt{2}}{2\delta_{0}}})(\left\lvert h_{1}\right\rvert+\left\lvert h_{2}\right\rvert)\Big),

(279)

for any
((h_{2},h_{1}),g)\in\mathbb{R}^{2}\times H^{1}(\mathbb{R})
such that
\max\{\left\lvert h_{2}\right\rvert,\left\lvert h_{1}\right\rvert\}=O(\delta_{0})
and
\left\lVert g\right\rVert_{H^{1}}\leq\delta_{0}.

In particular, estimate (
279
) implies that

DF_{h_{2},h_{1}}((h_{2},h_{1}),g)
is an uniformly non-degenerate matrix, for any
(h_{2},h_{1}),\,(x_{2},x_{1})\in\mathbb{R}^{2}
and
g\in H^{1}(\mathbb{R})
such
x_{2}-x_{1}\geq\frac{1}{\delta_{0}},\left\lVert g\right\rVert_{H^{1}}\leq\delta_{0}
and
\max\{\left\lvert h_{2}\right\rvert,\left\lvert h_{1}\right\rvert\}=O(\delta_{0}).
As a consequence, the result of the Modulation Lemma follows from the Implicit Function Theorem for Banach Spaces with the fact that
F((0,0),0)=(0,0)
.
∎

Appendix B
Optimality of Theorem
1.7

Theorem B.1
.

In notation of Theorem
1.7
, for any constant
C>0
and any function
s:\mathbb{R}_{+}\to\mathbb{R}_{+}
with
\lim_{h\to 0}s(h)=0,
we can find a positive value
\delta(s)
such that if
0<\epsilon\leq\delta(s),
then for any
\left\lVert\overrightarrow{g(0)}\right\rVert\leq\epsilon s(\epsilon)
there is a
0<T\lesssim\frac{\ln{(\frac{1}{\epsilon})}}{\epsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}}

such that

\epsilon\lesssim\left\lVert\overrightarrow{g(T)}\right\rVert.

Proof of Optimality of Theorem
1.7
.

We use the notations of Theorem
1.7
and Theorem
1.11
.
Clearly, if the result of Theorem
B.1
is false, then by contradiction there is a function
q:\mathbb{R}_{+}\to\mathbb{R}_{+}
with
\lim_{h\to 0}q(h)=0
such that for any
1\ll N\in\mathbb{N}
is possible to have

\left\lVert\overrightarrow{g(t)}\right\rVert\leq q(\epsilon)\epsilon

(280)

for all
0\leq t\leq N\frac{\ln{(\frac{1}{\epsilon})}}{\epsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}}=T
if
\epsilon\ll 1
enough. From Modulation Lemma, we can denote the solution
\phi(t,x)
as

\phi(t,x)=H^{x_{1}(t)}_{-1,0}(x)+H^{x_{2}(t)}_{0,1}(x)+g(t,x),

such that

\langle g(t,x),\,\partial_{x}H^{x_{1}(t)}_{-1,0}(x)\rangle_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R})}=0,\,\langle g(t,x),\,\partial_{x}H^{x_{2}(t)}_{0,1}(x)\rangle_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R})}=0.

Also, for all
t\geq 0,
we have that
g(t,x)
has a unique representation as

g(t,x)=P_{1}(t)\partial^{2}_{x}H^{x_{1}(t)}_{-1,0}(x)+P_{2}(t)\partial^{2}_{x}H^{x_{2}(t)}_{0,1}(x)+r(t,x),

(281)

such that
r(t)
satisfies the following new orthogonality conditions

\langle r(t),\,\partial^{2}_{x}H^{x_{1}(t)}_{-1,0}\rangle_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R})}=0,\,\langle r(t),\,\partial^{2}_{x}H^{x_{2}(t)}_{0,1}\rangle_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R})}=0.

(282)

In conclusion, we deduce that

\left\lVert g(t,x)\right\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R})}^{2}=\left\lVert\ddot{H}_{0,1}(x)\right\rVert_{L^{2}}^{2}(P_{1}^{2}+P_{2}^{2})+\left\lVert r(t)\right\rVert_{L^{2}_{x}(\mathbb{R}}^{2}+2P_{1}P_{2}\langle\ddot{H}^{z(t)}_{0,1}(x),\,\ddot{H}_{-1,0}(x)\rangle_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R})}.

(283)

We recall from Theorem
2.9
that
\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\ln{(\frac{1}{\epsilon})}<z(t)
for all
t\geq 0.

Since, from Lemma
2.3
, we have that
\langle\partial^{2}_{x}H^{x_{1}(t)}_{-1,0},\,\partial^{2}_{x}H^{x_{2}(t)}_{0,1}\rangle\lesssim z(t)e^{-\sqrt{2}z(t)}
and
z(t)e^{-\sqrt{2}z(t)}\lesssim\epsilon\ln{(\frac{1}{\epsilon})}
if
0<\epsilon\ll 1,
we deduce from the equation (
283
) that there is a uniform constant
K>1
such that for all
t\geq 0
we have the following estimate

\frac{\left\lVert g(t)\right\rVert_{L^{2}}}{K}\leq\left\lvert P_{1}(t)\right\rvert+\left\lvert P_{2}(t)\right\rvert+\left\lVert r(t)\right\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R})}\leq K\left\lVert\overrightarrow{g(t)}\right\rVert.

(284)

From Theorem
2.9
and orthogonality condition (
282
), we deduce that

\left\langle\partial_{t}r(t,x),\,\partial^{2}_{x}H^{x_{2}(t)}_{0,1}(x)\right\rangle_{L^{2}}=\dot{x}_{2}(t)\left\langle r(t,x),\,\partial^{3}_{x}H^{x_{2}(t)}_{0,1}(x)\right\rangle_{L^{2}}=O\Big(\left\lVert r(t)\right\rVert_{L^{2}}\epsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}\Big).

In conclusion, estimate (
284
) and Lemma
2.3
imply that there is a
K>1
such that

\left\lvert\dot{P}_{1}(t)\right\rvert+\left\lvert\dot{P}_{2}(t)\right\rvert+\left\lVert\partial_{t}r(t)\right\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R})}\leq K\left\lVert\overrightarrow{g(t)}\right\rVert

(285)

for all
t\geq 0.
Finally, Minkowski inequality and estimate (
284
) imply that there is a uniform constant
K>1
such that

\left\lVert\partial_{x}r(t,x)\right\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R})}\leq K\left\lVert\overrightarrow{g(t)}\right\rVert.

(286)

We recall from Theorem
2.10
the following estimate

\frac{\epsilon}{K}\leq\left\lVert\overrightarrow{g(t)}\right\rVert^{2}+\dot{x}_{1}(t)^{2}+\dot{x}_{2}(t)^{2}+e^{-\sqrt{2}z(t)}\leq K\epsilon

(287)

for some uniform constant
K>1.
Now, from hypothesis (
280
), we obtain from Theorem
1.11
and Corollary
1.13
that there are constants
M\in\mathbb{N}
and
C>0
such that for all
t\geq 0
the following inequalities are true

\displaystyle\max_{j\in\{1,\,2\}}\left\lvert x_{j}(t)-d_{j}(t)\right\rvert\leq\epsilon\ln{\Big(\frac{1}{\epsilon}\Big)}^{M+1}\exp\Big(\frac{10C\epsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}t}{\ln{(\frac{1}{\epsilon})}}\Big),

(288)

\displaystyle\max_{j\in\{1,\,2\}}\left\lvert\dot{x}_{j}(t)-\dot{d}_{j}(t)\right\rvert\leq\epsilon^{\frac{3}{2}}\ln{\Big(\frac{1}{\epsilon}\Big)}^{M}\exp\Big(\frac{10C\epsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}t}{\ln{(\frac{1}{\epsilon})}}\Big),

(289)

\displaystyle\max_{j\in\{1,\,2\}}\left\lvert\ddot{x}_{j}(t)-\ddot{d}_{j}(t)\right\rvert\leq\epsilon^{\frac{3}{2}}\ln{\Big(\frac{1}{\epsilon}\Big)}\exp\Big(\frac{10C\epsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}t}{\ln{(\frac{1}{\epsilon})}}\Big),

(290)

for a uniform constant
C>0.

From the partial differential equation (
1
) satisfied by
\phi(t,x)
and the representation (
281
) of
g(t,x)
, we deduce in the distributional sense that for any
h(x)\in H^{1}(\mathbb{R})
that

\left\langle h(x),\,(\ddot{P}_{1}(t)+\dot{x}_{1}(t)^{2})\partial^{2}_{x}H^{x_{1}(t)}_{-1,0}+(\ddot{P}_{2}(t)+\dot{x}_{2}(t)^{2})\partial^{2}_{x}H^{x_{2}(t)}_{0,1}\right\rangle_{L^{2}_{x}(\mathbb{R})}=\left\langle h(x),-P_{1}(t)\Big[\Big(-\partial^{2}_{x}+\ddot{U}(H^{x_{1}(t)}_{-1,0})\Big)\partial^{2}_{x}H^{x_{1}(t)}_{-1,0}\Big]\right\rangle\\
\left\langle h(x),\,-P_{2}(t)\Big[\Big(-\partial^{2}_{x}+\ddot{U}(H^{x_{2}(t)}_{0,1})\Big)\partial^{2}_{x}H^{x_{2}(t)}_{0,1}\Big]-\Big[\partial^{2}_{t}r(t)-\partial^{2}_{x}r(t)+\ddot{U}(H^{x_{2}(t)}_{0,1}+H^{x_{1}(t)}_{-1,0})r(t)\Big]\right\rangle_{L^{2}_{x}(\mathbb{R})}\\
-\left\langle h(x),\,\left[\dot{U}(H^{x_{2}(t)}_{0,1}+H^{x_{1}(t)}_{-1,0})-\dot{U}(H_{0,1}^{x_{2}(t)})-\dot{U}(H_{-1,0}^{x_{1}(t)})\right]-\ddot{x}_{1}(t)\partial_{x}H^{x_{1}(t)}_{-1,0}(x)-\ddot{x}_{2}(t)\partial_{x}H^{x_{2}(t)}_{0,1}(x)\right\rangle_{L^{2}_{x}(\mathbb{R})}\\
\left\langle h(x),\,-P_{1}(t)\Big[\Big(\ddot{U}(H^{x_{2}(t)}_{0,1}+H^{x_{1}(t)}_{-1,0})-\ddot{U}(H^{x_{1}(t)}_{-1,0})\Big)\partial^{2}_{x}H^{x_{1}(t)}_{-1,0}\Big]-P_{2}(t)\Big[\Big(\ddot{U}(H^{x_{2}(t)}_{0,1}+H^{x_{1}(t)}_{-1,0})-\ddot{U}(H^{x_{2}(t)}_{0,1})\Big)\partial^{2}_{x}H^{x_{2}(t)}_{0,1}\Big]\right\rangle\\
+O\left(\left\lVert h\right\rVert_{L^{2}}\left[\left\lVert g(t)\right\rVert_{H^{1}}^{2}+\max_{j\in\{1,\,2\}}\left\lvert\ddot{x}_{j}(t)\right\rvert+\max_{j\in\{1,\,2\}}\left\lvert\dot{P}_{j}(x)\dot{x}_{j}(t)\right\rvert+\max_{j\in\{1,\,2\}}\left\lvert P_{j}(t)\right\rvert e^{-\sqrt{2}z(t)}+\left\lvert P_{j}(t)\ddot{x}_{j}(t)\right\rvert+\left\lvert P_{j}(t)\dot{x}_{j}(t)^{2}\right\rvert\right]\right).

(291)

From Lemma
A.2
and estimates (
288
) and (
290
), we obtain from (
291
) that

\left\langle h(x),\,(\ddot{P}_{1}(t)+\dot{x}_{1}(t)^{2})\partial^{2}_{x}H^{x_{1}(t)}_{-1,0}+(\ddot{P}_{2}(t)+\dot{x}_{2}(t)^{2})\partial^{2}_{x}H^{x_{2}(t)}_{0,1}\right\rangle_{L^{2}_{x}(\mathbb{R})}=\left\langle h(x),-P_{1}(t)\Big[\Big(-\partial^{2}_{x}+\ddot{U}(H^{x_{1}(t)}_{-1,0})\Big)\partial^{2}_{x}H^{x_{1}(t)}_{-1,0}\Big]\right\rangle_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R})}\\
\left\langle h(x),\,-P_{2}(t)\Big[\Big(-\partial^{2}_{x}+\ddot{U}(H^{x_{2}(t)}_{0,1})\Big)\partial^{2}_{x}H^{x_{2}(t)}_{0,1}\Big]-\Big[\partial^{2}_{t}r(t)-\partial^{2}_{x}r(t)+\ddot{U}(H^{x_{2}(t)}_{0,1}+H^{x_{1}(t)}_{-1,0})r(t)\Big]\right\rangle_{L^{2}_{x}(\mathbb{R})}\\
+O\left(\left\lVert h\right\rVert_{L^{2}}\left[\max_{j\in\{1,\,2\}}\left\lvert\ddot{x}_{j}(t)-\ddot{d}_{j}(t)\right\rvert+e^{-\sqrt{2}d(t)}+\left\lvert z(t)-d(t)\right\rvert e^{-\sqrt{2}z(t)}+e^{-2\sqrt{2}z(t)}\right]\right)\\
+O\left(\left\lVert h\right\rVert_{L^{2}}\left[\left\lVert g(t)\right\rVert_{H^{1}}^{2}+\max_{j\in\{1,\,2\}}\left\lvert\ddot{x}_{j}(t)\right\rvert+\max_{j\in\{1,\,2\}}\left\lvert\dot{P}_{j}(x)\dot{x}_{j}(t)\right\rvert+\max_{j\in\{1,\,2\}}\left\lvert P_{j}(t)\right\rvert e^{-\sqrt{2}z(t)}+\left\lvert P_{j}(t)\ddot{x}_{j}(t)\right\rvert+\left\lvert P_{j}(t)\dot{x}_{j}(t)^{2}\right\rvert\right]\right).

(292)

From the condition (
282
), we deduce that

\displaystyle\left\langle\partial^{2}_{t}r(t),\,\partial^{2}_{x}H^{x_{2}(t)}_{0,1}\right\rangle_{L^{2}}=\frac{d}{dt}\left[\dot{x}_{2}(t)\left\langle r(t),\,\partial^{3}_{x}H^{x_{2}(t)}_{0,1}\right\rangle_{L^{2}}\right]+\dot{x}_{2}(t)\left\langle\partial_{t}r(t),\,\partial^{3}_{x}H^{x_{2}(t)}_{0,1}\right\rangle_{L^{2}},

\displaystyle\left\langle\partial^{2}_{t}r(t),\,\partial^{2}_{x}H^{x_{1}(t)}_{-1,0}\right\rangle_{L^{2}}=\frac{d}{dt}\left[\dot{x}_{1}(t)\left\langle r(t),\,\partial^{3}_{x}H^{x_{1}(t)}_{-1,0}\right\rangle_{L^{2}}\right]+\dot{x}_{1}(t)\left\langle\partial_{t}r(t),\,\partial^{3}_{x}H^{x_{1}(t)}_{-1,0}\right\rangle_{L^{2}},

which with the Theorem
2.9
imply that there is a uniform constant
C>0
such that

\left\lvert\left\langle\partial^{2}_{t}r(t),\,\partial^{2}_{x}H^{x_{2}(t)}_{0,1}\right\rangle_{L^{2}}\right\rvert\leq C\epsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}\left\lVert\overrightarrow{r(t)}\right\rVert,\,\left\lvert\left\langle\partial^{2}_{t}r(t),\,\partial^{2}_{x}H^{x_{1}(t)}_{-1,0}\right\rangle_{L^{2}}\right\rvert\leq C\epsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}\left\lVert\overrightarrow{r(t)}\right\rVert.

(293)

From (
284
), (
285
) and (
286
), we obtain that

\left\lVert\overrightarrow{r(t)}\right\rVert\lesssim\left\lVert\overrightarrow{g(t)}\right\rVert.

In conclusion, after we apply the partial differential equation (
292
) in distributional sense to
\partial^{2}_{x}H^{x_{2}(t)}_{0,1},\,\partial^{2}_{x}H^{x_{1}(t)}_{-1,0},
the estimates (
284
), (
285
), (
286
), (
288
), (
290
) and (
293
) imply that there is a uniform constant
K_{1}>0
such that if
\epsilon\ll 1
enough, then for
j\in\{1,\,2\}
we have that for
0\leq t\leq\frac{N\ln{(\frac{1}{\epsilon})}}{\epsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}}

\left\lvert\ddot{P}_{j}(t)+\dot{x}_{j}(t)^{2}\right\rvert\leq K_{1}\Big(e^{-\sqrt{2}d(t)}+\epsilon^{\frac{3}{2}}\ln{\Big(\frac{1}{\epsilon}\Big)}^{M+1}\exp\Big(\frac{10C\epsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}t}{\ln{(\frac{1}{\epsilon})}}\Big)+\epsilon q(\epsilon)\Big),

from which we deduce for all
0\leq t\leq N\frac{\ln{(\frac{1}{\epsilon})}}{\epsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}}
that

\left\lvert\sum_{j=1}^{2}\ddot{P}_{j}(t)+\dot{x}_{j}(t)^{2}\right\rvert\leq 2K_{1}\Big(e^{-\sqrt{2}d(t)}+\epsilon^{\frac{3}{2}}\ln{\Big(\frac{1}{\epsilon}\Big)}^{M+1}\exp\Big(\frac{10C\epsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}t}{\ln{(\frac{1}{\epsilon})}}\Big)+\epsilon q(\epsilon)\Big).

(294)

Since
\left\lvert\sum_{j=1}^{2}\ddot{P}_{j}(t)\right\rvert\geq-\left\lvert\sum_{j=1}^{2}\ddot{P}_{j}(t)+\dot{x}_{j}(t)^{2}\right\rvert+\sum_{j=1}^{2}\dot{x}_{j}(t)^{2},

we deduce from the estimates (
294
) and (
287
) that

\left\lvert\sum_{j=1}^{2}\ddot{P}_{j}(t)\right\rvert\geq\frac{\epsilon}{K}-\Big[e^{-\sqrt{2}z(t)}+\left\lVert\overrightarrow{g(t)}\right\rVert^{2}\Big]-2K_{1}\Big[e^{-\sqrt{2}d(t)}+\epsilon^{\frac{3}{2}}\ln{\Big(\frac{1}{\epsilon}\Big)}^{M+1}\exp\Big(\frac{10C\epsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}t}{\ln{(\frac{1}{\epsilon})}}\Big)\Big]-2K_{1}\epsilon q(\epsilon).

(295)

We recall that from the statement of Theorem
1.11
that

e^{-\sqrt{2}d(t)}=\frac{v^{2}}{8}\sech{(\sqrt{2}vt+c)}^{2},
with

v=\Big(\frac{\dot{z}(0)^{2}}{4}+8e^{-\sqrt{2}z(0)}\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}},

which implies that
v\lesssim\epsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}.
Since we have verified in Theorem
2.9
that
e^{-\sqrt{2}z(t)}\lesssim\epsilon,
the mean value theorem implies that

\left\lvert e^{-\sqrt{2}z(t)}-e^{-\sqrt{2}d(t)}\right\rvert=O(\epsilon\left\lvert z(t)-d(t)\right\rvert),

from which we deduce from
288
that

\left\lvert e^{-\sqrt{2}z(t)}-e^{-\sqrt{2}d(t)}\right\rvert=O\Big(\epsilon^{2}\ln{\Big(\frac{1}{\epsilon}\Big)}^{M+1}\exp\Big(\frac{10C\epsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}t}{\ln{(\frac{1}{\epsilon})}}\Big)\Big).

In conclusion, if
\epsilon\ll 1
enough, we obtain for
0\leq t\leq\frac{N\ln{(\frac{1}{\epsilon})}}{\epsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}}
from (
295
) that

\left\lvert\sum_{j=1}^{2}\ddot{P}_{j}(t)\right\rvert\geq\frac{\epsilon}{K}-\Big[e^{-\sqrt{2}d(t)}+\left\lVert\overrightarrow{g(t)}\right\rVert^{2}\Big]-4K_{1}\Big[e^{-\sqrt{2}d(t)}+\epsilon^{\frac{3}{2}}\ln{\Big(\frac{1}{\epsilon}\Big)}^{M+1}\exp\Big(\frac{10C\epsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}t}{\ln{(\frac{1}{\epsilon})}}\Big)\Big]-2K_{1}\epsilon q(\epsilon).

(296)

The conclusion of the demonstration will follow from studying separate cases in the choice of
0<v,\,c.
We also observe that
K,\,K_{1}
are uniform constants and the value of
N\in\mathbb{N}_{>0}
can be chosen in the beginning of the proof to be as much large as we need.

Case 1.
(
v^{2}\leq\frac{8\epsilon}{(1+4K_{1})2K}.
)
From inequality (
296
), we deduce that

\left\lvert\sum_{j=1}^{2}\ddot{P}_{j}(t)\right\rvert\geq\frac{\epsilon}{2K}-\left\lVert\overrightarrow{g(t)}\right\rVert^{2}-4K_{1}\Big(\epsilon^{\frac{3}{2}}\ln{\Big(\frac{1}{\epsilon}\Big)}^{M+1}\exp\Big(\frac{10C\epsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}t}{\ln{(\frac{1}{\epsilon})}}\Big)\Big)-2K_{1}\epsilon q(\epsilon),

then, from (
280
) we deduce for
0\leq t\leq\frac{\ln{(\frac{1}{\epsilon})}}{\epsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}}
that if
\epsilon
is small enough, then

\left\lvert\sum_{j=1}^{2}\ddot{P}_{j}(t)\right\rvert\geq\frac{\epsilon}{4K},

and so,

\left\lvert\sum_{j=1}^{2}\dot{P}_{j}(t)\right\rvert\geq\frac{\epsilon t}{4K}-\left\lvert\sum_{j=1}^{2}\dot{P}_{j}(0)\right\rvert,

which contradicts the fact that (
285
) and (
280
) should be true for
\epsilon\ll 1.

Case 2.
(
v^{2}\geq\frac{8\epsilon}{(1+4K_{1})2K},\,\left\lvert c\right\rvert>2\ln{(\frac{1}{\epsilon})}.
)
It is not difficult to verify that for
0\leq t\leq\min(\frac{\left\lvert c\right\rvert}{2\sqrt{2}v},N\frac{\ln{(\frac{1}{\epsilon})}}{\epsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}}),
we have that

e^{-\sqrt{2}d(t)}\leq\frac{v^{2}}{8}\sech{(\frac{c}{2})}^{2}\lesssim\epsilon^{3},

then estimate (
296
) implies that
\left\lvert\sum_{j=1}^{2}\ddot{P}_{j}(t)\right\rvert\geq\frac{\epsilon}{4K}
is true in this time interval. Also, since now
v\cong\epsilon^{\frac{1}{2}},
we have that

\frac{\ln{(\frac{1}{\epsilon})}}{\epsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}}\lesssim\frac{\left\lvert c\right\rvert}{2\sqrt{2}v},

so we obtain a contradiction by similar argument to the Case
1.

Case 3.
(
v^{2}\geq\frac{8\epsilon}{(1+4K_{1})2K},\,\left\lvert c\right\rvert\leq 2\ln{(\frac{1}{\epsilon})}.
)
For
1\ll N
enough and
t_{0}=\frac{(1+4K_{1})^{\frac{1}{2}}K^{\frac{1}{2}}\sqrt{2}\ln{(\frac{1}{\epsilon})}}{\epsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}},
we have during the time interval
\left\{t_{0}\leq t\leq 2\frac{(1+4K_{1})^{\frac{1}{2}}K^{\frac{1}{2}}\sqrt{2}\ln{(\frac{1}{\epsilon})}}{\epsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}}\right\}
that
e^{-\sqrt{2}d(t)}\leq\frac{v^{2}}{8}\sech{\Big(2\ln{\Big(\frac{1}{\epsilon}\Big)}\Big)}^{2}\lesssim\epsilon^{5}.
In conclusion, estimate (
295
) implies that
\left\lvert\sum_{j=1}^{2}\ddot{P}_{j}(t)\right\rvert\geq\frac{\epsilon}{4K}
is true in this time interval. From the Fundamental Calculus Theorem, we have that

\left\lvert\sum_{j=1}^{2}\dot{P}_{j}(t)\right\rvert\geq\frac{\epsilon(t-t_{0})}{4K}-\left\lvert\sum_{j=1}^{2}\dot{P}_{j}(t_{0})\right\rvert.

In conclusion, hypothesis (
280
) and estimate (
285
) imply for
T=2\frac{(1+2K_{1})^{\frac{1}{2}}K^{\frac{1}{2}}\sqrt{2}\ln{(\frac{1}{\epsilon})}}{\epsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}}
that

\left\lvert\sum_{j=1}^{2}\dot{P}_{j}(T)\right\rvert\geq\frac{\epsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}(1+2K_{1})^{\frac{1}{2}}\sqrt{2}\ln{(\frac{1}{\epsilon})}}{8K^{\frac{1}{2}}},

which contradicts the fact that (
280
) and (
285
) should be true, which finishes our proof.
∎

Remark B.2
.

Indeed, we can use Theorem
B.1
to verify that there is a sequence
(t_{n})_{n\in\mathbb{R}}
such that
t_{n}\to+\infty
and
\epsilon\lesssim\left\lVert\overrightarrow{g(t_{n})}\right\rVert_{H^{1}\times L^{2}}.

*
